# Warbucks Chemistry: The Demigod's Codex

38 chapters, 1178 quests. Generated by `tools/build_quests.py` from `tools/quest_data.py`; don't edit by hand.

## The Demigod's Path

### Welcome

*Read this first: who you are, how this world works, and how to use this book.*

1. **Welcome, Demigod**: checkmark
   > You remember light pouring from your hands. Now there is only dirt under your nails.
   > What: Welcome to Warbucks Chemistry. You're a demigod who has lost your power, and you'll win it back by mastering chemistry: taking matter apart into its elements and building it back up.
   > How: Follow the lines from quest to quest. Each one tells you what to do and how to do it. Click the checkmark to collect your first reward.
   > Tip: The sleeping bag skips the night without moving your spawn point.
2. **Who You Were**: checkmark *(after: Welcome, Demigod)*
   > Some things you still know without being taught: that water is two parts hydrogen to one of oxygen, that fire is a reaction, not a spirit.
   > What: Your power returns as you rebuild your understanding of matter. Every chapter of this book is one more piece of what you once were.
   > Chemistry: All matter is made of about 90 naturally occurring elements. Rearrange their atoms and you change what a thing is. That's the whole game.
   > How: Click the checkmark to continue.
3. **How Progression Works**: checkmark *(after: Who You Were)*
   > What: The pack has 8 tiers. Each one is unlocked by a chemistry milestone, not just more metal.
   > How: Fire and bronze → coke and steel → hydrogen and flight → electricity → acids → oil and plastics → silicon, PVC and the atom → antimatter.
   > Modern Industrialization's voltage tiers (LV, MV, HV, EV) are the spine. Other mods (Mekanism, AE2, Powah and more) unlock alongside them.
   > Tip: If a recipe looks different from what you remember, it's been gated on purpose. JEI always shows the real recipe.
4. **Your Toolkit**: checkmark *(after: Who You Were)*
   > What: A few mods make everything easier. Learn their keys now.
   > How: R / U over an item: recipes / uses (JEI). M: map and chunk claiming (FTB Chunks).
   > Hold ` while mining to vein mine (FTB Ultimine). Look at a block and Jade shows what it is and what it's doing.
   > Tip: Inventory sorting, crafting-grid tweaks and the trash slot are in your inventory screen; check Controls for their keys.
5. **Reading This Book**: checkmark *(after: How Progression Works)*
   > What: Every quest is written the same way, so you always know where to look.
   > How: What says what the thing is, Chemistry gives the real science, How gives the steps, and Tip saves you time.
   > Quests marked optional sit above or below the main line. They give bonus tokens but never block progress.
   > Tip: Gold lines are your memories returning. They mark the start of each chapter and its big milestones.
6. **Demigod Tokens**: checkmark *(after: Reading This Book)*
   > What: Most quests give Demigod Tokens. Spend them on convenience items by crafting: torches, food, XP, iron, coke, rubber, osmium, backpack upgrades and a Warp Stone.
   > How: Search Demigod Token in JEI and press U to see the whole shop.
   > Tip: Bigger milestones pay more tokens, and later tiers pay more than early ones. Optional quests pay too.
7. **Playing Together**: checkmark *(after: Your Toolkit)*
   > What: On a server, make a team so you share quest progress and claimed land.
   > How: /ftbteams party create <name>, then invite friends with /ftbteams party invite <player>.
   > Tip: Split the work: one player on ore processing, one on power, one exploring. Chemistry rewards a production line.
8. **Give the Game Room**: checkmark *(after: Playing Together)*
   > What: This is a large pack. Too little memory means stutters; too much can cause long pauses.
   > How: In Prism, open the instance settings and allocate about 8-10 GB. The pack's docs/README.md has the recommended Java arguments.
   > Tip: If the game hitches every few seconds, check the memory bar in F3 before blaming a mod.
9. **Begin**: checkmark *(after: Demigod Tokens, Give the Game Room)*
   > The world is waiting to be taken apart and put back together.
   > How: That's everything. Open Getting Started and take your first step.
   > Tip: You can come back to this chapter any time from the chapter list.
10. **The Periodic Table**: checkmark *(after: Who You Were)*
   > What: Chemists know 118 elements. Each one is defined by its number of protons, its atomic number.
   > Chemistry: Dmitri Mendeleev arranged the table in 1869 by repeating properties, and left gaps for elements nobody had found yet. Gallium and germanium later filled them almost exactly as he predicted.
   > Tip: The Periodic Table chapter tracks every element you collect.
11. **Nothing Is Lost**: checkmark *(after: Your Toolkit)*
   > What: Antoine Lavoisier showed in the 1780s that in a closed system, mass before a reaction equals mass after it.
   > Chemistry: Atoms are rearranged, never created or destroyed in a chemical reaction. That's why every equation balances.
   > Tip: The Dissolver and Combiner follow the same rule: what you take apart, you can build back.

### Getting Started

*You wake in a world you didn't make, with a god's patience and none of a god's power. These first steps get you fed, armed, rested and ready for chemistry.*

1. **The Awakening**: checkmark *(after: Begin)*
   > You remember being more than this. The memory is fading, but one idea stays clear: everything is made of elements, and elements can be taken apart and put back together.
   > What: This book walks you from your first log to antimatter. Follow the lines: each quest unlocks the next.
   > How: Click the checkmark on the right to begin.
2. **Tip: Recipes (JEI)**: checkmark *(after: The Awakening)*
   > What: JEI shows every recipe in the pack, including the ones changed for progression.
   > How: Press R over any item to see how it's made, or U to see what it's used in. Try it on a quest icon, then click the checkmark.
   > Tip: Type @modname in the JEI search bar to list everything one mod adds.
3. **Tip: What Am I Looking At?**: checkmark *(after: Tip: Recipes (JEI))*
   > What: Jade shows a small panel at the top of the screen for whatever block you're looking at.
   > How: Look at any block or machine. Jade shows its name, its mod, and for machines their progress, energy and fluids.
   > Tip: When a machine isn't working, look at it first. Jade usually tells you why.
4. **Wood and a Workbench**: `minecraft:crafting_table` *(after: The Awakening)*
   > What: Every tool starts at a crafting table.
   > Chemistry: Wood is mostly cellulose, (C₆H₁₀O₅)ₙ: long chains of sugar molecules. Later you'll take it apart atom by atom.
   > How: Punch a tree for logs, turn them into planks, and put 4 planks in a 2x2 grid. Any wood works.
5. **A Wooden Pick**: `minecraft:wooden_pickaxe` *(after: Wood and a Workbench)*
   > What: A wooden pickaxe is weak, but it can mine stone, and stone is your next step.
   > How: Craft sticks from planks, then 3 planks and 2 sticks in the pickaxe shape.
   > Tip: Don't bother upgrading it. Stone is minutes away.
6. **Stone Age**: `minecraft:stone_pickaxe` *(after: A Wooden Pick)*
   > What: Stone tools last longer and can mine iron ore.
   > How: Mine some cobblestone with the wooden pickaxe, then craft a stone pickaxe.
   > Tip: FTB Ultimine mines a whole vein at once. Hold the ` key (grave/tilde) while breaking blocks.
7. **Something to Eat**: 4x `minecraft:bread` *(after: Wood and a Workbench)*
   > What: Gods don't need to eat. You do, for now.
   > Chemistry: Baking turns starch and protein into new compounds. The brown crust comes from the Maillard reaction between sugars and amino acids.
   > How: Break grass for seeds, farm wheat, and craft bread from 3 wheat.
   > Tip: AppleSkin shows how much hunger and saturation each food restores.
8. **Rest Anywhere**: `comforts:sleeping_bag_white` *(after: Something to Eat)*
   > What: A sleeping bag skips the night without moving your spawn point, so it's great while exploring.
   > How: Craft it from wool. Any colour works for the recipe; this quest wants a white one.
   > Tip: Hammocks skip the day instead.
9. **A Roof Overhead**: checkmark *(after: Rest Anywhere)*
   > What: Monsters spawn in the dark. A small, lit, enclosed room keeps you safe while you work.
   > How: Build four walls and a roof, add a door, and light the inside with torches. Click the checkmark when you're done.
   > Tip: Leave room to grow. Your steam machines in Chapter I need floor space.
10. **Somewhere to Put Things**: 2x `minecraft:chest` *(after: Wood and a Workbench)*
   > What: You'll collect far more than you can carry. Chests hold 27 stacks each, and two side by side make a double chest.
   > How: 8 planks in a ring make a chest.
   > Tip: Inventory sorting works in chests too. Check Controls for the sort key.
11. **Coal**: 16x `minecraft:coal` *(after: Stone Age)*
   > What: Coal fuels furnaces now, and becomes coke and steel in Chapter II.
   > Chemistry: Coal is plant matter buried for millions of years. Heat and pressure drove off most of its hydrogen and oxygen and left mostly carbon.
   > How: Coal ore is the black-speckled stone common in exposed cliffs and mountains. Mine it with any pickaxe.
   > Tip: Save extra coal. The Coke Oven eats a lot of it.
12. **Light in the Dark**: 32x `minecraft:torch` *(after: Coal)*
   > What: Torches stop monsters spawning and light up caves.
   > Chemistry: A torch is combustion: carbon reacting with oxygen, C + O₂ → CO₂, releasing heat and light.
   > How: One coal or charcoal on top of one stick makes 4 torches.
13. **Fire**: `minecraft:furnace`; 4x `minecraft:charcoal` *(after: Stone Age)*
   > What: Fire is your first chemistry tool. Cooking logs in a furnace makes charcoal (more on why in Chapter I).
   > Chemistry: A furnace drives reactions with heat: it cooks food, reduces ores to metal and chars wood into carbon.
   > How: 8 cobblestone in a ring make a furnace. Smelt a log, using a log or planks as fuel.
14. **Iron**: 16x `minecraft:iron_ingot` *(after: Fire)*
   > What: Iron is the metal of your first real tools, buckets and armour.
   > Chemistry: Iron ore is iron oxide. In the furnace, carbon pulls the oxygen away: Fe₂O₃ + 3C → 2Fe + 3CO. That's reduction.
   > How: Mine iron ore (it's common around Y 16 and in mountains) and smelt it.
   > Tip: Iron Furnaces (see the Storage and Logistics chapter) smelt faster once you have spare iron.
15. **Iron Pickaxe**: `minecraft:iron_pickaxe` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Needed for diamonds, redstone and gold, and to start the journey to the Nether.
   > Chemistry: Iron is harder than stone because its atoms sit in a regular metal lattice held by metallic bonds, which bend rather than shatter.
   > How: 3 iron ingots and 2 sticks.
16. **Shield**: `minecraft:shield` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: A shield blocks arrows, melee hits and creeper blasts while you hold right-click.
   > How: 1 iron ingot and 6 planks (check JEI for the shape).
   > Tip: Hold it in your off-hand so you can block and swing at the same time.
17. **Iron Armour**: `minecraft:iron_helmet`; `minecraft:iron_chestplate`; `minecraft:iron_leggings`; `minecraft:iron_boots` *(after: Shield)*
   > What: A full set of iron armour makes caves and the night survivable.
   > How: Helmet, chestplate, leggings and boots take 24 iron ingots in total.
   > Tip: Ultimine through an iron vein and you'll have this set in minutes.
18. **Water**: `minecraft:water_bucket` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Water is the most important chemical in this pack: steam, electrolysis and acids all start with it.
   > Chemistry: H₂O is a small, polar molecule. That polarity lets it dissolve salts and acids, which is why chemists call it the universal solvent.
   > How: 3 iron ingots make a bucket. Fill it at any water source.
19. **A Backpack**: `sophisticatedbackpacks:backpack` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: More inventory, and it can be upgraded later (pickup, magnet, feeding and more).
   > How: Craft it from leather, string and a chest (check JEI). Wear it in the chest slot or keep it in your inventory.
   > Tip: Backpack upgrades are in the Demigod Token shop.
20. **Claim Your Land**: checkmark *(after: Iron Pickaxe, Iron Armour)*
   > What: FTB Chunks protects your base and can keep it loaded while you're away.
   > How: Press M to open the map, then drag over chunks to claim them. Click the checkmark when done.
   > Tip: Right-click a claimed chunk to force-load it. You'll need that for the flight pylon later.
21. **The Paxel**: `mekanismtools:iron_paxel` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: A paxel is a pickaxe, axe and shovel in one tool. Mekanism Tools adds them for every material, from wood to refined obsidian.
   > How: Craft an iron pickaxe, axe and shovel together with 2 sticks (check JEI).
   > Tip: One tool slot instead of three leaves more room for ore.
22. **Tools from Parts**: `silentgear:blueprint_paper`; `silentgear:pickaxe` *(after: The Paxel)*
   > What: Silent Gear builds tools from parts. Each part's stats come from its material, so better chemistry means better tools.
   > How: Make Blueprint Paper, craft a Pickaxe Blueprint, then craft a pickaxe head and rod.
   > Tip: The Tools and Comfort chapter goes deeper.
23. **Ready for Chemistry**: checkmark *(after: Claim Your Land, Water)*
   > Fed, armed, sheltered. Now the real work begins: fire, ore, alloys, and the slow road back to divinity.
   > How: Click to open Chapter I. The copper and tin in the reward are a head start on your first alloy.
   > Tip: Bring spare coal and iron. Chapter I eats both.
24. **Rock Collector**: `minecraft:coal`; `minecraft:raw_iron`; `minecraft:raw_copper`; `minecraft:raw_gold`; `minecraft:redstone`; `minecraft:lapis_lazuli`; `minecraft:diamond`; `minecraft:emerald` *(after: Iron Pickaxe)*
   > What: Collect one drop from every vanilla ore. Emeralds only generate in mountain biomes.
   > Chemistry: Real ores are minerals: iron comes from oxides like hematite (Fe₂O₃), copper often from sulfides like chalcopyrite (CuFeS₂), and diamond is pure carbon.
   > Tip: Every one of these can go into the Dissolver later. Keep spares.
25. **Kitchen Chemistry**: `farmersdelight:cooking_pot`; `farmersdelight:beef_stew` *(after: Something to Eat)*
   > What: Farmer's Delight meals restore more hunger and give the Nourishment and Comfort effects.
   > Chemistry: Stewing denatures proteins: heat unfolds them, which is why meat turns firm and tender.
   > How: Craft a Cooking Pot (iron, bricks, a water bucket and a wooden shovel), set it over a campfire or stove, and cook raw beef, a carrot and a potato.
   > Tip: Check JEI for the Cooking Pot's other recipes.
26. **A Closer Look**: `minecraft:spyglass` *(after: Light in the Dark)*
   > What: A spyglass lets you scout terrain and spot ore in cliff faces from far away.
   > Chemistry: Amethyst is quartz (SiO₂). Its purple colour comes from traces of iron in the crystal.
   > How: 2 copper ingots and 1 amethyst shard. Amethyst geodes sit underground, ringed with smooth basalt.
27. **Paxel Collector**: `mekanismtools:wood_paxel`; `mekanismtools:stone_paxel`; `mekanismtools:gold_paxel`; `mekanismtools:diamond_paxel` *(after: The Paxel)*
   > What: Craft the early paxels you skipped, plus a diamond one for the long haul.
   > Chemistry: Gold is soft because its atoms slide past each other easily. Diamond is the hardest natural material because every carbon atom is locked to four neighbours.
   > How: Same recipe as the iron paxel: pickaxe, axe and shovel of one material, plus 2 sticks.

### I. Fire and Bronze

*Heat drives chemistry: reduce ore with carbon, mix metals into alloys, and let steam do the work.*

1. **Pyrolysis**: 16x `minecraft:charcoal` *(after: Ready for Chemistry)*
   > You remember the first fire you ever tamed. It was smaller than this, and it frightened you more.
   > What: Charcoal is almost pure carbon: fuel, and the reducing agent that turns ore into metal.
   > Chemistry: Heat wood without enough oxygen to burn and the volatile compounds escape as smoke, tar, methanol and acetic acid. What's left is carbon. This is pyrolysis.
   > How: Smelt logs in a furnace.
   > Tip: A tree farm now saves hours later. Charcoal fuels everything until you have coke.
2. **Copper**: 16x `minecraft:copper_ingot` *(after: Ready for Chemistry)*
   > What: Copper is three quarters of bronze, and MI uses it for rotors, gears and wires.
   > Chemistry: Smelting is reduction: carbon pulls oxygen away from the metal oxide and leaves metal behind, CuO + C → Cu + CO.
   > How: Copper ore is common near the surface, especially in dripstone caves. Smelt the raw copper.
3. **Tin**: 8x `modern_industrialization:raw_tin`; 8x `modern_industrialization:tin_ingot` *(after: Copper)*
   > What: Tin is soft on its own, but a little of it makes copper much harder.
   > Chemistry: Real tin comes from cassiterite, SnO₂. Carbon reduces it the same way: SnO₂ + 2C → Sn + 2CO.
   > How: Tin ore here comes from Modern Industrialization (MI). It's grey with pale specks and fairly common underground. Mine it and smelt the raw tin.
4. **The MI Guidebook**: `modern_industrialization:guidebook` *(after: Ready for Chemistry)*
   > What: Modern Industrialization's own manual. It explains each machine and shows multiblock layouts.
   > How: 8 copper nuggets around a book. Break a copper ingot into nuggets first.
   > Tip: JEI (R/U) also shows multiblock shapes in 3D.
5. **The Forge Hammer**: `modern_industrialization:forge_hammer` *(after: Pyrolysis, Copper)*
   > What: MI's first workstation. It crushes ore and ingots into dust and hammers ingots into plates, rods and bolts.
   > How: 3 heavy weighted pressure plates over 4 iron ingots. Place it and right-click to use.
   > Tip: Ores give at most 2x here. Bigger yields come later from acids, not bigger hammers.
6. **Metal Dust**: 3x `modern_industrialization:copper_dust`; `modern_industrialization:tin_dust` *(after: The Forge Hammer, Tin)*
   > What: Solid ingots can't blend. Powders can.
   > Chemistry: Grinding a metal to powder gives it a huge surface area, so the two metals mix evenly when they melt together.
   > How: Put copper and tin ingots in the Forge Hammer: 1 ingot makes 1 dust.
7. **Mixing Bronze**: 6x `modern_industrialization:bronze_dust` *(after: Metal Dust)*
   > What: Bronze dust is the blended alloy, ready to melt into ingots.
   > Chemistry: Traditional bronze is about 88% copper and 12% tin by mass. Tin atoms jam the copper crystal so its layers can't slide, which makes it harder.
   > How: Craft 3 copper dust with 1 tin dust to get 3 bronze dust.
   > Tip: The Steam Mixer later turns the same inputs into 4 bronze dust instead of 3.
8. **The First Alloy**: 16x `modern_industrialization:bronze_ingot` *(after: Mixing Bronze)*
   > Metal that never existed until a mind decided it should. You remember the feeling.
   > What: Bronze is harder than either copper or tin alone, and it's the frame of your whole steam age.
   > Chemistry: An alloy is a mixture of metals. Its properties can beat its ingredients because the mixed atoms resist being pushed out of line.
   > How: Smelt bronze dust in any furnace.
9. **Bronze Plates**: 8x `modern_industrialization:bronze_plate` *(after: The First Alloy)*
   > What: Almost every steam machine is built from plates.
   > How: Hammer bronze ingots in the Forge Hammer. Without a hammer tool, 2 ingots make 1 plate.
   > Tip: With an Iron Hammer in the tool slot it's 1 ingot per plate, and the Steam Compressor later does it 1:1 for free.
10. **Rods, Bolts and Rings**: 2x `modern_industrialization:bronze_rod`; 4x `modern_industrialization:bronze_bolt`; `modern_industrialization:bronze_ring` *(after: Bronze Plates)*
   > What: Gears need bolts and a ring; rings need rods and bolts.
   > How: In the Forge Hammer, 1 bronze ingot makes 1 rod or 2 bolts. Craft rods and bolts into rings (check JEI).
   > Tip: Make a few extra of each. Every gear takes 4 bolts and a ring.
11. **Bronze Gears**: 2x `modern_industrialization:bronze_gear` *(after: Rods, Bolts and Rings)*
   > What: Each machine casing needs a gear at its centre.
   > How: 4 bronze plates, 4 bolts and 1 ring (check JEI for the shape).
   > Tip: Make extra rings and bolts while you're at it. You'll need several casings.
12. **Bronze Machine Casing**: `modern_industrialization:bronze_machine_casing` *(after: Bronze Gears)*
   > What: The core of every steam machine. You'll also need one for the Dissolver in Chapter II.
   > How: 8 bronze plates around 1 bronze gear.
   > Tip: Multiblock hatches each need a casing too. Make a few.
13. **Large Iron Plates**: 5x `modern_industrialization:iron_large_plate` *(after: The Forge Hammer)*
   > What: The head of the Iron Hammer is made of large plates.
   > How: Hammer iron ingots into plates (2 ingots per plate), then craft 4 plates into 1 large plate. That's about 40 iron in total.
   > Tip: Ultimine an iron vein first. This is the hungriest step of the chapter.
14. **The Iron Hammer**: `modern_industrialization:iron_hammer` *(after: Large Iron Plates)*
   > What: Put it in the Forge Hammer's tool slot to unlock better recipes: 1 ingot per plate, and crushing bricks into dust.
   > How: 5 large iron plates and 2 sticks (check JEI). The tool loses durability with each use.
   > Tip: Only use the tool slot when you need it. Plain recipes don't wear it down.
15. **Brick Dust**: 4x `modern_industrialization:brick_dust` *(after: The Iron Hammer)*
   > What: Brick dust is half of fire clay.
   > Chemistry: Potters call crushed fired clay grog. It has already shrunk in the kiln, so mixing it into raw clay stops the new brick cracking when heated.
   > How: Smelt clay balls into bricks, then hammer bricks in the Forge Hammer with the Iron Hammer in the tool slot.
16. **Fire Clay**: 6x `modern_industrialization:fire_clay_dust` *(after: Brick Dust)*
   > What: Fire clay is a refractory: a material that stays solid at temperatures that would ruin ordinary brick.
   > Chemistry: Refractory clays are rich in alumina (Al₂O₃) and silica (SiO₂), which melt far above a fire's temperature.
   > How: Craft 2 clay balls with 2 brick dust to make 3 fire clay dust.
17. **Fire Clay Bricks**: 8x `modern_industrialization:fire_clay_brick` *(after: Fire Clay)*
   > What: Single fire bricks, ready to stack into blocks.
   > Chemistry: Firing drives the bound water out of clay minerals and fuses the grains together. That change can't be undone.
   > How: Smelt fire clay dust in a furnace.
18. **Firebrick Blocks**: 3x `modern_industrialization:fire_clay_bricks` *(after: Fire Clay Bricks)*
   > What: The bronze boiler sits on a row of 3 fire clay brick blocks.
   > How: 4 fire clay bricks in a 2x2 make 1 block.
   > Tip: Chapter II's Steam Blast Furnace is built from dozens of these. Start stockpiling clay.
19. **Bronze Tank**: `modern_industrialization:bronze_tank` *(after: Bronze Machine Casing)*
   > What: A bronze tank stores fluid, and the boiler needs one to hold its water.
   > Chemistry: Bronze resists corrosion from water, which is why it was used for ship fittings for centuries.
   > How: 8 bronze plates around 1 glass block.
20. **Steam Power**: `modern_industrialization:bronze_boiler` *(after: Bronze Machine Casing, Firebrick Blocks, Bronze Tank)*
   > The engines remember you even if the world doesn't. Pressure, heat, motion: old friends.
   > What: Burn fuel, boil water, and the expanding steam does work. Welcome to the industrial revolution.
   > Chemistry: Water expands more than 1,600 times in volume when it boils into steam. That pressure is what drives the machines.
   > How: Craft it from bronze plates, a bronze tank, a furnace and 3 fire clay brick blocks. Give it water and fuel (coal or charcoal), and place steam machines next to it.
   > Tip: Steam flows into adjacent machines, or through MI fluid pipes once you have them.
21. **Steam Furnace**: `modern_industrialization:bronze_furnace` *(after: Steam Power)*
   > What: Smelts with steam instead of fuel. Your first automated machine.
   > How: Place it touching the boiler, or pipe steam to it later.
   > Tip: It smelts the same things as a vanilla furnace, so dust and raw ore can go straight in.
22. **Steam Macerator**: `modern_industrialization:bronze_macerator` *(after: Steam Power)*
   > What: Grinds ore and items into dust without wearing out a tool.
   > How: 1 ore makes 2 raw ore here (capped at 2x until acids).
   > Tip: It also grinds bricks into brick dust, so your Iron Hammer can rest.
23. **Steam Compressor**: `modern_industrialization:bronze_compressor` *(after: Steam Power)*
   > What: Presses ingots into plates 1:1, and plates into curved plates.
   > How: Place it touching the boiler and put ingots in. Curved plates come from plates you put back in.
   > Tip: Machines need lots of plates. Keep this one fed.
24. **Steam Mixer**: `modern_industrialization:bronze_mixer` *(after: Steam Power)*
   > What: Mixes dusts (like copper and tin into bronze) and fluids.
   > Chemistry: Same copper and tin, less loss: a good process wastes less of its inputs. That's called yield.
   > How: 3 copper dust + 1 tin dust makes 4 bronze dust here, one more than by hand.
25. **Steam Cutting Machine**: `modern_industrialization:bronze_cutting_machine` *(after: Steam Power)*
   > What: Cuts ingots into 2 rods, rods into bolts, and logs into 6 planks.
   > How: It needs lubricant as a cutting fluid. You'll make lubricant from creosote and redstone in Chapter II, so build it now and run it then.
   > Tip: Until then, the Forge Hammer makes rods and bolts.
26. **Curved Plates**: 6x `modern_industrialization:bronze_curved_plate` *(after: Steam Furnace, Steam Macerator, Steam Compressor)*
   > What: Pipes are made from curved bronze plates.
   > How: Put bronze plates in the Steam Compressor, or hammer 2 ingots into 1 curved plate.
   > Tip: You need 6 per batch of 16 pipes. Make 12 to have pipes to spare.
27. **Copper Rotors**: 2x `modern_industrialization:copper_rotor` *(after: Steam Compressor, Steam Mixer, Steam Cutting Machine)*
   > What: Rotors push fluid through pipes and pumps.
   > How: 4 copper blades, 1 copper ring and 4 copper bolts. Blades are curved copper plates on a copper rod (check JEI).
   > Tip: Every part comes from the Forge Hammer or the Steam Compressor.
28. **Fluid Pipes**: 8x `modern_industrialization:fluid_pipe` *(after: Curved Plates, Copper Rotors)*
   > What: MI fluid pipes carry water, steam and later every chemical in the pack.
   > How: 6 bronze curved plates, 2 copper rotors and a glass pane make 16 pipes.
   > Tip: Right-click a pipe end with an empty hand to set it to insert or extract. Item pipes need steel gears, so they wait for Chapter II.
29. **Endless Water**: `modern_industrialization:bronze_water_pump` *(after: Fluid Pipes)*
   > Fire, water, metal, motion. The pieces of a world are coming back to you.
   > What: A steam-powered pump that pulls water from the ground, so your boiler never runs dry.
   > How: Copper rotors, copper gears, a bronze tank, a machine casing and fluid pipes (check JEI). Place it next to the boiler, or pipe its water into it.
   > Tip: Next stop: coke and steel in Chapter II.
30. **Bronze Age Hoard**: 4x `modern_industrialization:bronze_block` *(after: The First Alloy)*
   > What: 36 ingots of bronze, stored as blocks.
   > Chemistry: Bronze resists corrosion: its surface forms a thin layer of copper compounds that protects the metal underneath. Bronze statues can last thousands of years.
   > How: 9 bronze ingots make 1 block.
31. **Bronze Paxel**: `mekanismtools:bronze_paxel` *(after: The First Alloy)*
   > What: Mekanism Tools makes paxels from bronze too.
   > Chemistry: Ancient bronze tools replaced stone ones because bronze can be cast, sharpened and repaired. Stone can only be chipped.
   > How: Craft a bronze pickaxe, axe and shovel, then combine them with 2 sticks.
32. **The Wrench**: `modern_industrialization:wrench` *(after: Bronze Plates)*
   > What: MI's wrench rotates MI machines, sets which face they output from, and picks them up quickly.
   > How: 5 bronze plates in a wrench shape (check JEI).
   > Tip: Handy when you rearrange your steam setup for the Coke Oven in Chapter II.
33. **Steam Mining Drill**: `modern_industrialization:steam_mining_drill` *(after: Rods, Bolts and Rings)*
   > What: A fuel-burning mining tool that digs a 3x3 area and can give an easy Silk Touch.
   > How: Furnaces, diamonds, large iron plates, a copper drill head and a bucket (check JEI). Read its tooltip for fuel and mode controls.
   > Tip: Diamonds are the hard part. Mine deep, around Y -59.
34. **Efficient Alloying**: 32x `modern_industrialization:bronze_dust` *(after: Steam Mixer)*
   > What: Mix 32 bronze dust in the Steam Mixer.
   > Chemistry: In real metallurgy, alloy ratios are measured by mass or by number of atoms. Getting the ratio right matters more than the amount.
   > Tip: Pipe the macerator's dust straight into the mixer and you'll never mix by hand again.

### II. Steam and Steel

*Destructive distillation turns coal into coke, and coke turns iron into steel. At the end of this chapter, matter itself opens up to you.*

1. **A Kiln's Worth of Bricks**: 32x `minecraft:bricks` *(after: Fluid Pipes)*
   > You remember smoke rising from a hill of earth, and what came out of it burning hotter than anything before.
   > What: The Coke Oven is built from ordinary brick blocks: one ring of 8 for the controller, and more for its walls.
   > Chemistry: Firing clay drives out the water bound in its minerals and fuses the grains, turning soft mud into ceramic.
   > How: Smelt clay balls into bricks, then craft 4 bricks into a brick block.
   > Tip: Rivers and swamps are full of clay. Ultimine a clay patch.
2. **The Coke Oven**: `modern_industrialization:coke_oven` *(after: A Kiln's Worth of Bricks)*
   > What: The controller of a small multiblock that bakes coal into coke.
   > Chemistry: Coal is carbon mixed with many other compounds. Bake it without air and those volatiles are driven off. This is destructive distillation.
   > How: 8 brick blocks around a furnace.
3. **Hatches**: `modern_industrialization:bronze_item_input_hatch`; `modern_industrialization:bronze_item_output_hatch`; `modern_industrialization:bronze_fluid_input_hatch` *(after: The Coke Oven)*
   > What: MI multiblocks have no inventory of their own. Hatches built into the walls take items and steam in and push products out.
   > How: An item hatch is a hopper on a bronze machine casing; a fluid hatch is a bronze tank on a casing (check JEI for the output versions).
   > Tip: While you hold a hatch, MI can highlight the spots where it fits (a client option in MI's config).
4. **Build the Coke Oven**: checkmark *(after: Hatches)*
   > What: Put the shell together and the controller comes to life.
   > How: Press R on the Coke Oven in JEI or open the MI guidebook to see the 3D layout: a 3x3x3 cube of brick blocks with the controller in one face. Swap wall bricks for hatches: item in, item out, steam in, and a fluid output for creosote.
   > Tip: Pipe steam from your boiler into the fluid input hatch. Click the checkmark once the controller shows it's formed.
5. **Coke**: 16x `modern_industrialization:coke` *(after: Build the Coke Oven)*
   > What: Coke is exactly what a blast furnace needs: strong, porous, nearly pure carbon.
   > Chemistry: With the hydrogen, sulfur and tars baked out, what's left is a carbon skeleton that burns hot and clean.
   > How: Feed coal into the item input hatch. Each coal becomes 1 coke in 30 seconds.
   > Tip: Coal dust works too and comes out as coke dust, which saves a grinding step later.
6. **Byproduct: Creosote**: `modern_industrialization:creosote_bucket` *(after: Build the Coke Oven)*
   > What: Half the time, coking a coal also yields 500 mB of creosote. Nothing is wasted in chemistry: creosote is a boiler fuel.
   > Chemistry: Coal-tar creosote is a mix of phenols, cresols and ring-shaped hydrocarbons, the same family of compounds that makes tar smell the way it does.
   > How: Pipe it out of the fluid output hatch into a tank, or straight into a boiler.
7. **Lubricant**: `modern_industrialization:lubricant_bucket` *(after: Byproduct: Creosote)*
   > What: The Steam Cutting Machine needs lubricant as cutting fluid. Now you can finally run it.
   > How: In the Steam Mixer, 500 mB of creosote and 1 redstone make 500 mB of lubricant.
   > Tip: Each cut uses only 1 mB, so one bucket lasts a very long time.
8. **Coke Dust**: 4x `modern_industrialization:coke_dust` *(after: Coke)*
   > What: Steel is mixed as dust, so the carbon has to be a powder too.
   > Chemistry: Coke is the reducing agent in a real blast furnace: it pulls oxygen out of iron ore and supplies the carbon that becomes steel.
   > How: Grind coke in the Steam Macerator, or coke coal dust directly in the Coke Oven.
9. **Iron Dust**: 14x `modern_industrialization:iron_dust` *(after: Coke Dust)*
   > What: The other ingredient of steel.
   > How: Grind iron ingots in the Steam Macerator or the Forge Hammer (1 ingot makes 1 dust).
   > Tip: Macerating iron ore gives 2 raw iron per ore, which smelts into more ingots than mining alone.
10. **Uncooked Steel**: 7x `modern_industrialization:uncooked_steel_dust` *(after: Iron Dust)*
   > What: A blend of iron and carbon, ready for the blast furnace.
   > Chemistry: Real steel holds far less carbon than this mix suggests, but the idea is right: carbon is the ingredient that turns soft iron into steel.
   > How: Craft 7 iron dust with 2 coke dust to get 7 uncooked steel dust.
11. **Blast Furnace Materials**: 32x `modern_industrialization:fire_clay_bricks`; `minecraft:blast_furnace` *(after: Coke)*
   > What: The Steam Blast Furnace is a tower of fire clay bricks, hotter than anything you've built.
   > How: Make fire clay bricks as in Chapter I (the Steam Macerator grinds bricks into brick dust now). A vanilla blast furnace is 5 iron, a furnace and 3 smooth stone.
   > Tip: Automate it: macerator → crafting → steam furnace. You'll want a lot of these.
12. **The Steam Blast Furnace**: `modern_industrialization:steam_blast_furnace` *(after: Blast Furnace Materials)*
   > What: A multiblock that gets hot enough to melt iron and dissolve carbon into it.
   > How: Craft the controller from 8 fire clay brick blocks around a blast furnace. Check the structure in JEI: a 3x3 tower, four blocks tall, with bronze hatches for items and steam.
   > Tip: Some later recipes in this furnace need a steel hatch. Steel itself only needs bronze ones.
13. **Steel**: 16x `modern_industrialization:steel_ingot` *(after: The Steam Blast Furnace, Uncooked Steel)*
   > Iron that bends without breaking. You remember forging weapons for gods out of something very like this.
   > What: Iron with a small, controlled amount of carbon (about 0.2-2%) is steel: strong, tough, and the backbone of every machine to come.
   > Chemistry: Carbon atoms sit in the gaps between iron atoms and stop the layers sliding. Too much carbon and you get brittle cast iron. Chemistry is about the right ratio.
   > How: Put uncooked steel dust in the blast furnace's item input hatch. Each dust becomes 1 steel ingot in 30 seconds.
14. **Steel Paxel**: `mekanismtools:steel_paxel` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: A sturdier paxel for the long mining trips ahead.
   > How: A steel pickaxe, axe and shovel with 2 sticks (check JEI).
   > Tip: Steel tools last much longer than iron ones. Good for clearing ground for the factory ahead.
15. **Steel Boiler**: `modern_industrialization:steel_boiler` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: More steam per tick than the bronze boiler.
   > How: Steel plates, a steel tank, a furnace and 3 fire clay brick blocks (check JEI).
   > Tip: Steel versions of the steam machines (macerator, compressor and others) run faster too.
16. **Steel Plates**: 16x `modern_industrialization:steel_plate` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: The Dissolver and Combiner are framed in steel plates, and steel gears need them too.
   > How: The Steam Compressor presses 1 steel ingot into 1 plate.
   > Tip: You need at least 8 for the Dissolver and Combiner, plus more for gears. Keep the compressor busy.
17. **Steel Gears**: 2x `modern_industrialization:steel_gear` *(after: Steel Plates)*
   > What: Item pipes and steel machine casings need steel gears.
   > How: Same pattern as bronze: 4 steel plates, 4 steel bolts and 1 steel ring (check JEI).
   > Tip: Steel rods, bolts and rings come from the Forge Hammer, or from the Cutting Machine now that you have lubricant.
18. **Item Pipes**: 16x `modern_industrialization:item_pipe` *(after: Steel Gears)*
   > What: MI item pipes move items between machines, so dust, ingots and coke flow on their own.
   > How: 6 bronze curved plates and 2 steel gears make 16 pipes.
   > Tip: Right-click a pipe end with an empty hand to switch it between insert and extract.
19. **A Simple Generator**: `generatorgalore:copper_generator` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: Generator Galore's Copper Generator burns solid fuel into FE (Forge Energy) and pushes it into machines it touches.
   > Chemistry: Burning releases the chemical energy stored in carbon bonds as heat; the generator turns that heat into electricity.
   > How: Copper ingots around a furnace, with a block of redstone (check JEI).
20. **Forge Energy**: `generatorgalore:iron_generator` *(after: A Simple Generator)*
   > What: Some machines run on FE instead of steam. You'll need it for the Dissolver.
   > How: Upgrade the Copper Generator: surround it with iron ingots and a block of redstone to make the Iron Generator.
   > Tip: A Mekanism Heat Generator or Powah Furnator also works. Burn coke for long runs.
21. **Heat and Pressure**: 2x `minecraft:magma_block`; 2x `minecraft:piston` *(after: Steel Plates)*
   > What: Besides steel plates and a bronze machine casing, the Dissolver needs 2 magma blocks and 2 pistons.
   > Chemistry: Magma blocks are rock hot enough to glow, a fitting heat source for a machine that tears compounds apart.
   > How: Magma blocks generate in underwater ravines and caves, or can be crafted from 4 magma cream. Pistons need iron, redstone, cobblestone and planks.
22. **Pressure and Precision**: `minecraft:diamond`; 2x `minecraft:obsidian` *(after: Steel Plates)*
   > What: The Combiner needs a diamond, 2 obsidian and a piston, plus steel plates and a casing.
   > Chemistry: Obsidian is volcanic glass: lava that cooled too fast for crystals to form.
   > How: Pour water onto a lava source to make obsidian, and mine it with a diamond pickaxe. Diamonds are most common near the bottom of the world.
23. **The Dissolver**: `alchemistry:dissolver` *(after: Heat and Pressure, Forge Energy)*
   > Your first divine gift returns.
   > What: The Dissolver breaks any item into the elements and compounds it's made of.
   > How: Steel plates, pistons, magma blocks and a Bronze Machine Casing (check JEI). Power it with FE, insert anything, and collect the elements.
   > Tip: Some things dissolve into compounds first. Put those back in to break them down further.
24. **The Combiner**: `alchemistry:combiner` *(after: Pressure and Precision, Forge Energy)*
   > What: The reverse: arrange elements and the Combiner builds them back into items and blocks.
   > How: Steel plates, a diamond, obsidian, a piston and a Bronze Machine Casing (check JEI). It runs on FE too.
   > Tip: Taking the world apart and rebuilding it to your design is what this pack is about.
25. **Wood, Undone**: 4x `chemlib:cellulose` *(after: The Dissolver)*
   > What: Your first compound out of the Dissolver.
   > Chemistry: Cellulose is a chain of glucose units, (C₆H₁₀O₅)ₙ. It's the most common organic compound on Earth and gives wood its strength.
   > How: Dissolve logs: each log gives 1 cellulose. Dissolve the cellulose again to get 6 carbon, 10 hydrogen and 5 oxygen.
26. **Graphite**: 4x `chemlib:graphite` *(after: The Dissolver)*
   > What: Coal and charcoal dissolve into graphite, a pure form of carbon.
   > Chemistry: In graphite, carbon atoms form flat sheets of hexagons that slide over each other. That's why pencil 'lead' leaves a mark.
   > How: Dissolve coal or charcoal: each gives 2 graphite. Each graphite dissolves into 4 carbon.
27. **First Entries in the Periodic Table**: 16x `chemlib:hydrogen`; 16x `chemlib:carbon`; 16x `chemlib:oxygen`; 16x `chemlib:iron` *(after: Wood, Undone, Graphite, The Combiner)*
   > What: Dissolve whatever you have lying around and collect your first elements: H (1), C (6), O (8), Fe (26).
   > Chemistry: The number is the atomic number: how many protons are in the nucleus. Change it and you have a different element.
   > How: Cellulose gives carbon, hydrogen and oxygen; graphite gives carbon. Each iron ingot gives 16 iron.
   > Tip: The Periodic Table chapter tracks every element from here on.
28. **Your First Synthesis**: 4x `chemlib:water` *(after: First Entries in the Periodic Table)*
   > Two hydrogen, one oxygen, and a world's worth of oceans. You remember making the first ones.
   > What: Build water from its elements in the Combiner. It's a trivial compound, but you made it from scratch. That's the point.
   > Chemistry: 2H₂ + O₂ → 2H₂O. Hydrogen and oxygen share electrons in covalent bonds, and the bent shape of the molecule makes it polar.
   > How: Pick water as the target in the Combiner's GUI, then supply 2 hydrogen and 1 oxygen per water.
29. **Fuel Reserve**: 4x `modern_industrialization:coke_block` *(after: Coke)*
   > What: A stockpile of coke for boilers, generators and future steel.
   > Chemistry: Coke is porous. Air flows through it, which is why it burns so hot and steadily in a furnace.
   > Tip: A full Coke Oven running overnight fills these quickly.
30. **Steel Upgrades**: `modern_industrialization:steel_upgrade`; `modern_industrialization:steel_macerator` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: A Steel Upgrade turns a bronze steam machine into its faster steel version.
   > How: Craft Steel Upgrades from a steel machine casing, bronze parts, fluid pipes and fire clay bricks (check JEI), then combine one with the Bronze Macerator.
   > Tip: Upgrade the machines that bottleneck you first, usually the macerator.
31. **Proof of Composition**: 16x `chemlib:copper`; 16x `chemlib:tin` *(after: First Entries in the Periodic Table)*
   > What: Dissolve a copper ingot and a tin ingot to see the two elements that make bronze.
   > Chemistry: Copper is element 29 and tin is element 50. Every bronze ingot you made in Chapter I was these two, mixed.
   > How: Each copper or tin ingot dissolves into 16 of its element.
32. **Sweet Chemistry**: `chemlib:sucrose` *(after: First Entries in the Periodic Table)*
   > What: Dissolve sugar into sucrose, then sucrose into its elements.
   > Chemistry: Sucrose is glucose and fructose joined together. Dissolved, it gives 12 carbon, 22 hydrogen and 11 oxygen.
   > How: Sugar cane makes sugar; the Dissolver turns 1 sugar into 1 sucrose.
33. **Carbon Is Carbon**: 128x `chemlib:graphite` *(after: First Entries in the Periodic Table)*
   > What: A diamond dissolves into 128 graphite. Expensive, but it proves a point.
   > Chemistry: Diamond and graphite are both pure carbon, called allotropes. In diamond every atom bonds to four neighbours in 3D; in graphite, to three in flat sheets.
   > Tip: Don't dissolve your last diamond. You'll need them for machines.

### III. Divine Ascent

*Walk through fire, brew in the dark, and take back the sky. Flight needs the Nether and your first Alchemistry machine: fire and chemistry together.*

1. **A Door of Fire**: 10x `minecraft:obsidian`; `minecraft:flint_and_steel` *(after: Iron Pickaxe)*
   > You remember the sky. Not looking up at it: standing in it.
   > What: a Nether portal is a 4x5 frame of obsidian (corners optional), lit with flint and steel.
   > Chemistry: obsidian is volcanic glass. Lava cooled so fast its atoms never had time to form crystals.
   > How: mine obsidian with a diamond pickaxe, or pour water over still lava to make it in place.
   > Tip: a bucket of water and a bucket of lava let you cast the frame block by block.
2. **Into the Fire**: visit `minecraft:the_nether` *(after: A Door of Fire)*
   > What: the Nether is a chemistry lab: quartz (SiO₂), blaze powder and nether wart all come from here.
   > How: bring food, armour, blocks and a way home. NetherPortalFix keeps portals linked properly.
   > Tip: water evaporates instantly here and beds explode. Bring a respawn plan, not a bed.
3. **Blaze Rods**: 4x `minecraft:blaze_rod` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: blaze rods craft the brewing stand, and blaze powder fuels it.
   > How: blazes spawn around spawners in Nether Fortresses. Explorer's Compass can point you to one.
   > Tip: snowballs hurt blazes, and fire resistance makes the fight easy.
4. **Nether Wart**: 8x `minecraft:nether_wart` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: the base reagent for almost every potion.
   > How: it grows on soul sand in fortress stairwells. Bring some soul sand home and replant it; it grows in the Overworld too.
   > Tip: one stack of wart grown at home covers every brew in this chapter.
5. **Nether Quartz**: 16x `minecraft:quartz` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: Nether quartz ore is everywhere in Nether netherrack. The Infusion Pylon needs it.
   > Chemistry: quartz is silicon dioxide, SiO₂. Much later you'll reduce it with carbon to get pure silicon for circuits.
   > Tip: keep a spare stack. Quartz dust plus coke becomes silicon in Chapter V.
6. **Blackstone**: 16x `minecraft:blackstone` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: the Infusion Pylon is framed in blackstone.
   > Chemistry: blackstone is a basalt-like volcanic rock, rich in dark iron and magnesium minerals.
   > How: it fills basalt deltas and bastion remnants. Any pickaxe mines it.
7. **The Brewer's Art**: `minecraft:brewing_stand` *(after: Blaze Rods, Nether Wart)*
   > What: a brewing stand turns water bottles into potions, one ingredient at a time.
   > Chemistry: brewing is extraction and reaction: each ingredient changes what's dissolved in the bottle.
   > How: 1 blaze rod + 3 cobblestone. Blaze powder in the top-left slot fuels 20 brews.
   > Tip: look up Potion of Flying in JEI (from Apothic Attributes). The next quests walk the whole chain.
8. **The Awkward Potion**: checkmark *(after: The Brewer's Art)*
   > What: the Awkward Potion does nothing by itself, but almost every potion starts from it.
   > How: fill glass bottles with water, put three in the stand, add nether wart on top.
   > Tip: brew three at a time. Every ingredient affects all three bottles at once.
9. **Slow Falling**: 4x `minecraft:phantom_membrane` *(after: The Awkward Potion)*
   > What: step 1 toward flight. Awkward Potion + phantom membrane = Potion of Slow Falling.
   > How: phantoms appear at night when someone in the world hasn't slept for three or more days. Fight them on a roof.
   > Tip: one membrane brews three bottles, so four membranes go a long way.
10. **Levitation**: 4x `minecraft:fermented_spider_eye` *(after: Slow Falling)*
   > What: step 2. Slow Falling + fermented spider eye = Potion of Levitation (Apothic Attributes).
   > Chemistry: fermentation is yeast turning sugar into alcohol and acids. Here it 'corrupts' the effect into its opposite.
   > How: fermented spider eye = spider eye + sugar + brown mushroom (shapeless).
11. **Lifting Gas**: 2x `chemlib:hydrogen` *(after: Levitation, Lighter than Air)*
   > What: step 3. In this pack, Flying is made with hydrogen instead of End chorus fruit, so you can fly before the End.
   > Chemistry: hydrogen (H₂) is the lightest gas there is, about 14 times lighter than air. It lifted the first passenger balloons in 1783.
   > How: get hydrogen from the Alchemistry Dissolver (dissolve water or plants). You need 2 per Flying potion.
   > Tip: the brewing-stand route (popped chorus fruit) still works too, once you reach the End.
12. **Potion of Flying**: checkmark *(after: Lifting Gas)*
   > What: the Potion of Flying gives creative-style flight for 8 minutes.
   > How: in a crafting grid: 1 Potion of Levitation + 2 hydrogen (check JEI). Tick this when you've made one.
   > Tip: don't drink it yet. The next step makes it last far longer.
13. **Extended Flight**: 8x `minecraft:redstone` *(after: Potion of Flying)*
   > What: redstone stretches a potion's duration. Flying + redstone = Long Flying (15 min). Add redstone again for Extra Long Flying (30 min).
   > How: brew redstone into your Flying potions, then into the Long ones.
   > Tip: a Potion Filter needs a full hour, which is just two Extra Long potions. Brew at least two.
14. **Potion Filter**: `pylons:potion_filter` *(after: Extended Flight)*
   > What: a Potion Filter holds a potion effect for the Infusion Pylon.
   > How: drink a potion, then right-click the filter to store the effect. It activates once it holds 1 hour of that effect.
   > Tip: combine two filters with the same effect by holding one in each hand and right-clicking.
15. **A Full Hour**: checkmark *(after: Potion Filter)*
   > What: the filter shows how much effect time it holds. It needs 60 minutes of Flying to work in a pylon.
   > How: two Extra Long Flying (30 min each), four Long Flying (15 min), or about eight regular potions.
   > Tip: drink and load one potion at a time, so none of the effect runs out first.
16. **Lighter than Air**: 8x `chemlib:hydrogen` *(after: The Dissolver)*
   > What: your flight pylon needs hydrogen, and only the Dissolver can pull it out of matter as an item.
   > Chemistry: H is element 1: one proton, one electron. Hydrogen balloons carried people aloft in 1783.
   > How: dissolve water, plants or wood and collect the hydrogen.
   > Tip: feed the Dissolver from a hopper and it runs while you're in the Nether.
17. **Wings of a God**: `pylons:infusion_pylon` *(after: A Full Hour, Nether Quartz, Blackstone, Lighter than Air)*
   > The sky is yours again. You had forgotten how small the world looks from here.
   > What: the Infusion Pylon applies a filter's effect to you anywhere. With a Flying filter, that's creative-style flight.
   > How: craft it from Nether quartz, blackstone and hydrogen from your Dissolver (see JEI), place it, and put the full filter inside.
   > Tip: the pylon only works while its chunk is loaded. Force-load it (see the next quest).
18. **Keep It Running**: checkmark *(after: Wings of a God)*
   > What: if the pylon's chunk unloads, your flight stops. Over a long trip that means a long fall.
   > How: open the FTB Chunks map (press M), claim the chunk with the pylon, then right-click it again to force-load it.
   > Tip: force-load your Dissolver and brewing setup too, so they keep working while you travel.
19. **Fortress Found**: 32x `minecraft:nether_brick` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: Nether Fortresses hold blazes, wither skeletons and nether wart.
   > How: Explorer's Compass points to structures. Mine nether bricks from the fortress walls.
   > Tip: build a small portal near the fortress and your next trip is much shorter.
20. **Fire Resistance**: 4x `minecraft:magma_cream` *(after: The Brewer's Art)*
   > What: Fire Resistance makes lava and blaze fire harmless. Swimming in lava becomes a shortcut.
   > How: magma cubes drop magma cream in basalt deltas, or craft it from blaze powder + slimeball. Brew it into an Awkward Potion.
   > Tip: add redstone for an 8-minute version before any lava-heavy trip.
21. **Ghast Tears**: 2x `minecraft:ghast_tear` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: ghast tears brew Regeneration and drop rarely, since the ghast must die over solid ground.
   > How: reflect a fireball back with a punch or arrow, then catch the tear before it burns in lava.
   > Tip: a bow and patience beat chasing ghasts across lava lakes.
22. **Ancient Debris**: 2x `minecraft:ancient_debris` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: ancient debris smelts into netherite scrap, the base of netherite gear.
   > How: it hides deep in the Nether (around Y 8-22) and resists explosions, so bed or TNT mining works.
   > Tip: try the Dissolver on a scrap later and see what this 'element' really is.
23. **Fire into Source**: `ars_nouveau:volcanic_sourcelink` *(after: Whispers of Magic)*
   > What: the Volcanic Sourcelink turns burning fuel into Source, the energy behind Ars Nouveau.
   > How: craft it from source gems, gold and a lava bucket, place it near a Source Jar and give it fuel. The Worn Notebook explains the details.
   > Tip: it suits a Nether-themed base: blaze rods and lava-adjacent fuels keep it going.
24. **Archwood**: 8x `ars_nouveau:blue_archwood_log` *(after: Whispers of Magic)*
   > What: archwood is Ars Nouveau's magic wood, used for its jars, tables and rituals.
   > How: look for brightly coloured trees in archwood forests; any colour works, but this quest wants blue.
   > Tip: archwood saplings grow at home, so a few logs become a farm.
25. **Whispers of Magic**: `ars_nouveau:worn_notebook`; 4x `ars_nouveau:source_gem` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: the Worn Notebook teaches Ars Nouveau spellcrafting. Source gems are its basic magical material.
   > How: the notebook is a book + lapis lazuli (shapeless). Source gems come from the Imbuement Chamber (lapis or amethyst + Source).
   > Tip: its Ritual of Flight is a second way to fly near your base. See the Ars Nouveau page (Magic and Relics).

### IV. The Electric Age

*Electricity is the tool that splits compounds apart. First you have to make it, and build the circuits that use it.*

1. **Rubber**: 8x `modern_industrialization:rubber_sheet` *(after: Steel)*
   > You remember lightning answering when you called it. Now you'll have to build it a road.
   > What: rubber sheets insulate cables and form circuit boards.
   > Chemistry: natural rubber is polyisoprene, (C₅H₈)ₙ: long chains that don't let electrons through.
   > How: check JEI (press R on Rubber Sheet) for MI's rubber recipes, from plant matter or, later, synthetic rubber.
2. **Wires and Cables**: 8x `modern_industrialization:copper_wire`; 8x `modern_industrialization:tin_cable` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: copper wire goes into every component. Tin cable carries LV power between machines.
   > Chemistry: copper's single outer electron moves freely between atoms, which is why it conducts so well.
   > How: the Steel Wiremill pulls plates into wire. Cables combine wire with rubber sheets.
   > Tip: tin cable is rated for LV (32 EU/t). Higher tiers need better cable.
3. **Battery Alloy**: 4x `modern_industrialization:battery_alloy_ingot` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: battery alloy makes the casing and grids of redstone batteries.
   > Chemistry: lead hardened with antimony is the grid metal of real lead-acid batteries. Pure lead is too soft.
   > How: mix lead and antimony dust (check JEI), then smelt. Lead deposits sit around Y -24 to 16.
4. **Analog Circuit Board**: 2x `modern_industrialization:analog_circuit_board` *(after: Rubber)*
   > What: the base every analog circuit is soldered onto.
   > How: rubber sheet, copper plate, rubber sheet stacked in a column.
   > Tip: once you have an Assembler, it makes these automatically.
5. **Capacitor**: 2x `modern_industrialization:capacitor` *(after: Rubber, Wires and Cables)*
   > What: capacitors store a small charge and smooth out the current.
   > Chemistry: two conductors (gold plates) separated by an insulator (rubber): charge builds up on each side.
   > How: gold plates, copper wire and a rubber sheet (see JEI).
6. **Resistor**: 3x `modern_industrialization:resistor` *(after: Wires and Cables)*
   > What: resistors limit current so the rest of the circuit doesn't burn out.
   > Chemistry: carbon conducts, but poorly. A pinch of coal dust between fine copper wires makes a carbon resistor.
   > How: paper, copper fine wire and coal dust. One craft gives three.
7. **Inductor**: 2x `modern_industrialization:inductor` *(after: Wires and Cables)*
   > What: inductors resist sudden changes in current. The Polarizer also needs them.
   > Chemistry: current through a coil makes a magnetic field; changing the current fights that field.
   > How: copper wire wound around a steel rod.
8. **Redstone Battery**: 2x `modern_industrialization:redstone_battery` *(after: Battery Alloy, Wires and Cables)*
   > What: a simple rechargeable battery. Every LV hull holds two.
   > How: battery alloy plate and curved plates, tin cable and redstone (see JEI).
   > Tip: the LV Storage Unit is built from six of these, so make spares.
9. **Analog Circuit**: 2x `modern_industrialization:analog_circuit` *(after: Analog Circuit Board, Capacitor, Resistor, Inductor)*
   > What: the LV circuit. Mekanism's Basic Control Circuit also needs one, so MI and Mekanism advance together.
   > How: 2 resistors, 1 inductor, 2 capacitors, the board, and 3 copper wire (see JEI for the layout).
   > Tip: make four or five now. The hull, turbine and each machine all eat them.
10. **Steel Machine Casing**: `modern_industrialization:steel_machine_casing` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: a steel frame. The LV hull is built around it.
   > How: steel plates (see JEI). You've made the bronze one already; this is the steel upgrade.
   > Tip: the LV hull uses one casing each, and every LV machine needs a hull. Make a few.
11. **LV Machine Hull**: `modern_industrialization:basic_machine_hull` *(after: Analog Circuit, Redstone Battery, Steel Machine Casing)*
   > Your hands remember wiring this. Your mind is still catching up.
   > What: LV (Low Voltage) is the first electric tier. Every LV machine is built on this hull.
   > How: steel machine casing + analog circuit + 2 redstone batteries + 3 tin cable.
12. **Magnetic Steel Rod**: 2x `modern_industrialization:steel_rod_magnetic` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: the core of an electric motor.
   > Chemistry: iron-based alloys hold magnetism because their electron spins line up in domains.
   > How: a steel rod ringed by redstone. Later the Polarizer magnetises rods with electricity.
13. **Electric Motor**: 2x `modern_industrialization:motor` *(after: Magnetic Steel Rod, Wires and Cables)*
   > What: motors drive turbines, pistons, conveyors and robot arms. Nearly every LV machine needs one.
   > Chemistry: current in a coil next to a magnet creates a force; that's the motor effect. Run it backwards and it's a generator.
   > How: magnetic steel rod, steel rods, copper wire and tin cable (see JEI).
14. **Steam to Electricity**: `modern_industrialization:lv_steam_turbine` *(after: LV Machine Hull, Electric Motor)*
   > What: a turbine turns your boiler's steam into EU, MI's electricity.
   > How: pipe steam in, and run tin cable from the turbine to your machines.
   > Tip: watch the turbine's steam input. If machines stall, add boilers before adding turbines.
15. **Piston**: 2x `modern_industrialization:piston` *(after: Electric Motor)*
   > What: MI's piston component, used in the macerator, compressor and robot arm.
   > How: steel plates, steel rods, steel gear, tin cable and a motor.
   > Tip: the Electric Macerator and Electric Compressor each need pistons, so make four.
16. **Conveyor**: 2x `modern_industrialization:conveyor` *(after: Electric Motor, Rubber)*
   > What: conveyors move items inside machines. The Assembler, Packer and Cutting Machine need them.
   > How: rubber sheets, two motors and tin cable.
   > Tip: motors are the bottleneck here. Keep a few spare.
17. **Electric Compressor**: `modern_industrialization:electric_compressor` *(after: Steam to Electricity, Piston)*
   > What: plates, curved plates and rings for every part you'll build next.
   > How: LV hull, two pistons, analog circuits and tin cable.
   > Tip: it doubles as a second plate source while the steam compressor keeps running.
18. **Electric Wiremill**: `modern_industrialization:electric_wiremill` *(after: Steam to Electricity)*
   > What: copper wire and fine wire in bulk. Circuits use a lot of it.
   > How: LV hull, motors and analog circuits (see JEI).
   > Tip: feed it from your compressor's plate output.
19. **Polarizer**: `modern_industrialization:polarizer` *(after: Steam to Electricity, Inductor)*
   > What: magnetic steel rods for motors, and magnetic cupronickel wire for heating coils.
   > Chemistry: a strong field lines up the magnetic domains in the metal, and they stay lined up.
   > How: LV hull, inductors, tin wire and tin cable.
20. **Electric Mixer**: `modern_industrialization:electric_mixer` *(after: Steam to Electricity)*
   > What: mixing dusts into alloys (bronze, invar, cupronickel, battery alloy) and fluids into mixtures.
   > How: LV hull, motor, tin rotor, glass, a fluid pipe and analog circuits.
   > Tip: you'll need it for cupronickel (copper + nickel) and invar (iron + nickel) right away.
21. **Electric Macerator**: `modern_industrialization:electric_macerator` *(after: Piston, Steam to Electricity)*
   > What: ore to crushed dust, ingots back to dust. Still capped at 2x per ore until acids.
   > How: needs an invar rotary blade (invar gear + diamond dust). Invar is iron + nickel; mix the dusts.
   > Tip: the hull quest reward included one. Build a second for throughput.
22. **Electric Furnace**: `modern_industrialization:electric_furnace` *(after: Magnetic Cupronickel Wire)*
   > What: fast, fuel-free smelting.
   > Chemistry: current through a resistive wire turns into heat (Joule heating), the same idea as a toaster.
   > How: LV hull wrapped in magnetic cupronickel wire, with analog circuits.
23. **Robot Arm**: 2x `modern_industrialization:robot_arm` *(after: Piston)*
   > What: robot arms move parts inside the Assembler and Packer.
   > How: two motors, a piston, steel rods, tin cable and an analog circuit.
   > Tip: the Assembler takes two and the Packer two more.
24. **Cutting Machine**: `modern_industrialization:electric_cutting_machine` *(after: Conveyor, Steam to Electricity)*
   > What: cutting ingots into rods and bolts, and logs into more planks.
   > How: it uses an invar rotary blade, motors and a conveyor. Its cuts use a little lubricant (check JEI for where to get it).
   > Tip: logs give six planks here instead of four.
25. **Magnetic Cupronickel Wire**: 8x `modern_industrialization:cupronickel_wire_magnetic` *(after: Polarizer, Electric Mixer)*
   > What: the heating wire for the Electric Furnace and Electric Blast Furnace.
   > Chemistry: cupronickel (copper + nickel) resists current more than copper and survives heat. Coins and ship pipes use it too.
   > How: mix copper and nickel dust, smelt, draw into wire, then magnetise it in the Polarizer.
26. **Assembler**: `modern_industrialization:assembler` *(after: Robot Arm, Conveyor)*
   > What: circuits and machine parts, automatically, from a stocked input.
   > How: LV hull, two robot arms, two conveyors and two analog circuits.
   > Tip: MI shows every assembler recipe in JEI. Feed it sheets, wire and plates and leave it running.
27. **Packer**: `modern_industrialization:electric_packer` *(after: Robot Arm, Conveyor)*
   > What: compacting ingots into blocks and double ingots, and assembling a few parts. You'll want bauxite blocks next chapter.
   > How: robot arms, conveyors, analog circuits and tin cable on an LV hull.
   > Tip: unpacking is a separate machine, the Unpacker. Build one if you store metal as blocks.
28. **Electric Blast Furnace**: `modern_industrialization:electric_blast_furnace`; 8x `modern_industrialization:cupronickel_coil` *(after: Magnetic Cupronickel Wire)*
   > Fire you can measure in volts. You used to do this without the volts.
   > What: MI's big furnace multiblock. With cupronickel coils it reaches the heat for aluminum and silicon.
   > How: the controller is an LV hull wrapped in magnetic cupronickel wire. Check the structure in JEI or the MI guidebook, then add an LV energy input hatch and item hatches.
   > Tip: hold a hatch or wrench near the controller to see the valid positions highlighted.
29. **Osmium**: 8x `mekanism:raw_osmium` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: Mekanism builds almost everything from osmium: its infuser, casings and circuits.
   > Chemistry: real osmium is the densest natural element, 22.6 g/cm³. A litre of it weighs about as much as a big dog.
   > How: osmium ore is common in small veins underground. Smelt the raw osmium into ingots.
30. **Mekanism: Metallurgic Infuser**: `mekanism:metallurgic_infuser` *(after: Osmium)*
   > What: Mekanism's entry machine. It infuses metals with other materials.
   > How: iron ingots, redstone, an osmium ingot and two furnaces.
   > Tip: it runs on FE. Your Chapter II generator works, or a Mekanism Heat Generator (Power chapter).
31. **Infused Alloy**: 4x `mekanism:alloy_infused` *(after: Mekanism: Metallurgic Infuser)*
   > What: Mekanism's basic alloy. The Enrichment Chamber and many basic machines need it.
   > Chemistry: a real alloy mixes metals at the atomic level. Here redstone plays the part of the second element.
   > How: put redstone in the infuser's left slot (it becomes an infusion buffer), then feed copper ingots.
32. **Mekanism Circuits**: 2x `mekanism:basic_control_circuit`; `mekanism:steel_casing` *(after: Mekanism: Metallurgic Infuser, Analog Circuit)*
   > What: in this pack, a Basic Control Circuit is MI Analog Circuit + osmium. Each Mekanism circuit tier needs the matching MI circuit.
   > How: the Steel Casing (steel, glass and osmium) is the frame for most Mekanism machines.
   > Tip: make several casings at once. Every Mekanism machine in the next chapter needs one.
33. **Enrichment Chamber**: `mekanism:enrichment_chamber` *(after: Mekanism Circuits, Infused Alloy)*
   > What: ore → 2 dust. It also turns dirty dust into clean dust in the higher-tier lines.
   > How: steel casing, infused alloy, iron and a basic control circuit.
   > Tip: 3x, 4x and 5x need gases and acids: that's the next chapter.
34. **Crusher**: `mekanism:crusher` *(after: Mekanism Circuits)*
   > What: crushes ingots to dust and, later, ore clumps into dirty dust in the 3x+ lines.
   > How: steel casing, basic control circuits, redstone and lava buckets.
   > Tip: it also turns ingots back into dust, handy for alloy recipes.
35. **The Wrench**: `modern_industrialization:wrench` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: MI's wrench adjusts machines and pipes. Read its tooltip for the controls.
   > How: bronze plates (see JEI).
   > Tip: holding it near a multiblock controller highlights where the hatches can go.
36. **Pipe Config Card**: `modern_industrialization:config_card` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: saves an item or fluid pipe connection's settings and pastes them onto other connections.
   > How: sneak + use on a configured pipe connection to save; use on another connection to apply. Sneak + use on air clears it.
   > Tip: it can also camouflage pipes as any block. The card's tooltip explains how.
37. **LV Storage Unit**: `modern_industrialization:lv_storage_unit` *(after: Steam to Electricity, Redstone Battery)*
   > What: stores EU, so machines keep running when the turbine can't keep up for a moment.
   > How: six redstone batteries, battery alloy plates and an LV hull.
   > Tip: put it between turbine and machines so short dips don't stop anything.
38. **Electric Water Pump**: `modern_industrialization:electric_water_pump` *(after: Steam to Electricity)*
   > What: pumps water from nothing but power, for boilers and (soon) electrolysis.
   > How: an LV hull with a pump, tin rotor, pipes and an analog circuit.
   > Tip: next chapter, water becomes hydrogen and oxygen. You'll want lots of it.
39. **The Spectrometer**: `warbuckscore:spectrometer` *(after: Analog Circuit)*
   > You remember seeing the colours inside things, the light every element gives off.
   > What: a hand-held scanner (Warbucks Core, made for this pack). Right-click to read the ore signal in the loaded chunks around you.
   > Chemistry: heated atoms give off light only at certain wavelengths, an emission spectrum unique to each element. That's how helium was found in the Sun before anyone found it on Earth.
   > How: amethyst prism, steel plates, copper wire and an analog circuit (check JEI). It lists each element with its strength, direction, distance and depth.
   > Tip: a "strong" signal is usually a rich deposit. Walk towards it and scan again.
40. **A Rich Lead Deposit**: 64x `modern_industrialization:lead_ore` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: rich ore deposits hold hundreds of ores in one chunk, at a metal-specific depth. Lead's sits around Y -24 to 16.
   > How: strip-mine at that depth and check ore with Jade. FTB Ultimine clears a vein in one go.
   > Tip: galena (lead sulfide, PbS) is the world's main lead ore, and it often carries silver.
41. **Osmium Hoard**: 32x `mekanism:ingot_osmium` *(after: Osmium)*
   > What: every Mekanism machine, casing and circuit costs osmium. A stockpile saves trips.
   > How: smelt or enrich raw osmium into ingots.
   > Tip: the Enrichment Chamber doubles osmium ore, so process it there once it's built.

### V. Electrochemistry and Acids

*Electricity splits compounds into elements. Acids dissolve what fire can't. Ore processing becomes chemistry.*

1. **Mekanism Gases**: `mekanism:electrolytic_separator` *(after: Mekanism Circuits)*
   > You remember pulling water apart with a word. A machine and a cable will have to do.
   > What: the Electrolytic Separator makes hydrogen and oxygen as gases you can pipe and store.
   > Chemistry: 2H₂O → 2H₂ + O₂. Current drives the reaction backwards from burning hydrogen.
   > How: feed it water and FE. Gases leave from the coloured sides (configure them with the Configurator).
2. **Pressurized Tubes**: 16x `mekanism:basic_pressurized_tube` *(after: Mekanism Gases)*
   > What: gases need sealed tubes. Item pipes and fluid pipes won't carry them.
   > How: craft basic pressurized tubes (see JEI) and run them from the separator's outputs.
   > Tip: vent what you don't need yet. A full output tank stops the whole separator.
3. **A Source of Sulfur**: 8x `modern_industrialization:sulfur_dust` *(after: Electric Macerator)*
   > What: sulfur is where sulfuric acid starts.
   > Chemistry: S is element 16. Volcanoes and fossil fuels are full of it; most industrial sulfur is stripped out of oil and gas.
   > How: macerate crushed lignite coal (about half the time you get sulfur dust), or centrifuge lava later. Press U on Sulfur Dust in JEI for all sources.
4. **Contact Process 1: SO₂**: `mekanism:chemical_oxidizer` *(after: Pressurized Tubes, A Source of Sulfur)*
   > What: the Chemical Oxidizer turns a solid into a gas. Sulfur dust becomes sulfur dioxide.
   > Chemistry: S + O₂ → SO₂. It's the sharp smell of a struck match.
   > How: one sulfur dust gives 100 mB of SO₂. Pipe it out with pressurized tubes.
5. **Contact Process 2: SO₃**: `mekanism:chemical_infuser` *(after: Contact Process 1: SO₂)*
   > What: the Chemical Infuser combines two gases. SO₂ plus oxygen from your separator gives sulfur trioxide.
   > Chemistry: 2SO₂ + O₂ → 2SO₃. Real plants pass the gases over a vanadium oxide (V₂O₅) catalyst at about 450 °C: the 'contact' in the name.
   > How: SO₂ in one side, O₂ in the other.
6. **Water Vapor**: `mekanism:rotary_condensentrator` *(after: Contact Process 2: SO₃)*
   > What: the Rotary Condensentrator converts fluids to gases and back. It turns water into water vapor.
   > How: set it to gas mode (the toggle in its GUI), pipe water in and pressurized tube out.
   > Tip: it also turns Mekanism gases into buckets, which is handy for storage and trading.
7. **Contact Process 3: H₂SO₄**: checkmark *(after: Contact Process 2: SO₃, Water Vapor)*
   > What: a second Chemical Infuser combines SO₃ with water vapor into sulfuric acid gas. Tick this when acid is flowing.
   > Chemistry: SO₃ + H₂O → H₂SO₄. Real plants dissolve SO₃ in existing acid first (oleum), because SO₃ and water alone make a corrosive mist.
   > Tip: store the acid in a chemical tank. The 5x ore line drinks it.
8. **Salt**: 16x `modern_industrialization:salt_dust` *(after: Electric Macerator)*
   > What: salt is the start of the chlor-alkali industry: chlorine, sodium and everything made from them.
   > Chemistry: NaCl is an ionic crystal: Na⁺ and Cl⁻ held together by charge. Split it and you get a soft, reactive metal and a toxic green gas.
   > How: MI salt ore is found underground and drops salt dust.
9. **Brine**: checkmark *(after: Salt, Contact Process 1: SO₂)*
   > What: brine is salty water, and Mekanism splits it into chlorine. Tick this when you have brine.
   > How: put salt dust in a Chemical Oxidizer (15 mB brine per dust), or build a Thermal Evaporation Plant (see the side quest).
   > Tip: a second oxidizer just for salt saves you from swapping inputs.
10. **Chlorine**: checkmark *(after: Brine, Mekanism Gases)*
   > What: the Electrolytic Separator also takes brine. Out come chlorine and sodium. Tick this when chlorine is flowing.
   > Chemistry: real brine electrolysis gives Cl₂ at one electrode and H₂ + NaOH at the other. Mekanism simplifies it to sodium + chlorine.
   > Tip: use a second separator so your hydrogen and oxygen line keeps running.
11. **Hydrogen Chloride**: checkmark *(after: Chlorine, Contact Process 2: SO₃)*
   > What: hydrogen chloride drives the 4x ore line. Tick this when HCl is flowing.
   > Chemistry: H₂ + Cl₂ → 2HCl. Dissolved in water it's hydrochloric acid, the same acid in your stomach.
   > How: hydrogen in one side of a Chemical Infuser, chlorine in the other.
12. **3x Ore: Oxygen**: `mekanism:purification_chamber` *(after: Mekanism Gases, Enrichment Chamber, Crusher)*
   > What: before acids, every route caps at 2x per ore. Purifying ore with oxygen gives 3x.
   > How: ore + O₂ → 3 clumps. Crusher: clumps → dirty dust. Enrichment Chamber: dirty dust → dust.
   > Tip: Mekanism shows each step in JEI. Chain them with pipes and side configuration.
13. **4x Ore: Hydrogen Chloride**: `mekanism:chemical_injection_chamber` *(after: 3x Ore: Oxygen, Hydrogen Chloride)*
   > What: injecting hydrogen chloride splits ore into shards: 4x.
   > How: ore + HCl → 4 shards. Purification Chamber: shards → clumps, then on through your 3x line.
   > Tip: your 3x machines are now the back half of the 4x line. Nothing is wasted.
14. **Dissolving Ore**: `mekanism:chemical_dissolution_chamber` *(after: 4x Ore: Hydrogen Chloride, Contact Process 3: H₂SO₄)*
   > What: the Dissolution Chamber dissolves ore in sulfuric acid, giving a dirty slurry.
   > Chemistry: acid leaching: H₂SO₄ turns metal minerals into soluble sulfates, leaving the rock behind.
   > How: feed it ore and sulfuric acid gas. The slurry comes out as a chemical.
15. **Washing Slurry**: `mekanism:chemical_washer` *(after: Dissolving Ore)*
   > What: the Chemical Washer rinses impurities out of the slurry.
   > Chemistry: washing removes the dissolved gangue (waste rock) so only the metal salt is left to crystallise.
   > How: slurry in, water in, clean slurry out. An MI electric water pump keeps the water coming.
16. **5x Ore: Dissolution**: `mekanism:chemical_dissolution_chamber`; `mekanism:chemical_washer`; `mekanism:chemical_crystallizer` *(after: Washing Slurry)*
   > Five from one. You used to call that a small miracle. Now you call it hydrometallurgy.
   > What: the Chemical Crystallizer turns clean slurry into crystals: 5x per ore.
   > Chemistry: this is hydrometallurgy, the way most of the world's copper is really refined.
   > How: crystals → Injection Chamber (shards) → Purification Chamber (clumps) → Crusher → Enrichment Chamber.
17. **Ore Byproducts**: `modern_industrialization:centrifuge`; 4x `modern_industrialization:silver_dust` *(after: 5x Ore: Dissolution, Electronic Circuit)*
   > What: real ores are never pure. Send Mekanism's dirty dust through MI's Centrifuge instead of the Enrichment Chamber for the same dust plus a chance at a bonus metal.
   > Chemistry: iron → nickel, copper → gold, gold → silver, lead → silver (galena), tin → tungsten, osmium → iridium, uranium → lead (decay).
   > How: the Centrifuge needs an electronic circuit. Route dirty dust to it, and rare metals arrive as a bonus.
18. **Bauxite**: 16x `modern_industrialization:bauxite_dust` *(after: Electric Blast Furnace)*
   > You remember a metal an emperor once served to his most honoured guests. It was never rare, only stubborn.
   > What: bauxite is the only practical aluminum ore. MI bauxite ore drops bauxite dust.
   > Chemistry: bauxite is mostly aluminium hydroxides, Al(OH)₃ and AlO(OH), stained red by iron oxide.
   > How: rich bauxite deposits sit around Y -16 to 24. No furnace will free the metal: it goes through the Bayer process first.
19. **Lye for the Digester**: `modern_industrialization:sodium_hydroxide_bucket` *(after: Electric Mixer, The Combiner)*
   > What: the Bayer process dissolves bauxite in hot lye, so you need sodium hydroxide solution first.
   > Chemistry: NaOH is a strong base: it fully splits into Na⁺ and OH⁻ in water, and OH⁻ is what pulls aluminium into solution.
   > How: combine Na + O + H in the Alchemistry Combiner (check JEI on Sodium Hydroxide), then Mixer: 1 crystal + 250 mB water → 250 mB lye.
   > Tip: the digester gives most of the lye back, so a few crystals go a long way. Later the Chemical Reactor makes it in bulk.
20. **Bayer Digestion**: `modern_industrialization:sodium_aluminate_solution_bucket` *(after: Bauxite, Lye for the Digester)*
   > What: the first step of every aluminium refinery since 1888: hot lye dissolves the aluminium out of bauxite.
   > Chemistry: Al(OH)₃ + NaOH → NaAl(OH)₄ (sodium aluminate). Iron and titanium oxides don't dissolve; they settle out as 'red mud'.
   > How: Mixer: 4 bauxite dust + 1000 mB lye → 1000 mB sodium aluminate solution + 2 tiny iron dust + 1 tiny titanium dust.
   > Tip: pipe the solution straight into the next Mixer; a bucket is only needed for this quest.
21. **Precipitation**: 8x `chemlib:aluminum_hydroxide_dust` *(after: Bayer Digestion)*
   > What: pure aluminium hydroxide, crystallised back out of the solution, with the lye recovered.
   > Chemistry: NaAl(OH)₄ → Al(OH)₃ + NaOH. Diluting the liquor makes the reaction run backwards; the impurities stayed behind in the red mud.
   > How: Mixer: 1000 mB sodium aluminate + 1000 mB water → 4 aluminium hydroxide dust + 900 mB lye.
   > Tip: loop the 900 mB of lye back into the digester. That loop is why the Bayer process is cheap.
22. **Alumina**: 4x `chemlib:aluminum_oxide_dust` *(after: Precipitation)*
   > What: alumina (aluminium oxide) is the white powder that actually goes into the smelter.
   > Chemistry: calcination: 2Al(OH)₃ → Al₂O₃ + 3H₂O at around 1000 °C. Alumina also makes sapphire and ruby, coloured by traces of iron, titanium or chromium.
   > How: Electric Blast Furnace: 2 aluminium hydroxide dust → 1 alumina dust.
23. **The Hall-Héroult Cell**: `modern_industrialization:hall_heroult_cell` *(after: Alumina)*
   > What: a multiblock smelter that splits alumina with electricity. It's built from steel and LV parts, because it's what makes your first aluminium.
   > Chemistry: alumina melts at 2072 °C, far too hot. Dissolved in molten cryolite it melts near 950 °C. Current then splits it: 2Al₂O₃ + 3C → 4Al + 3CO₂, burning the carbon anode away.
   > How: controller from steel large plates, analog circuits, cupronickel coils and an LV hull. Check JEI (press U on the controller) for the 3x3x3 shape: steel casings top and bottom, a ring of cupronickel coils with the controller in the middle layer.
   > {@pagebreak}
