   > How: put one between your generators and machines. Configure its sides with the Configurator.
   > Tip: cubes upgrade in place: craft the next tier around the old one.
10. **Bigger Cubes**: `mekanism:advanced_energy_cube` *(after: Store It, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: the Advanced Energy Cube holds and moves much more energy.
   > How: it needs an Advanced Control Circuit, which in this pack needs an MI electronic circuit (MV).
   > Tip: Elite and Ultimate cubes follow the HV and EV circuits.
11. **Evaporation Ponds**: `mekanism:thermal_evaporation_controller`; `mekanism:brine_bucket` *(after: Bigger Cubes)*
   > What: the Thermal Evaporation Plant boils water down into brine, and brine down into liquid lithium.
   > Chemistry: real lithium comes from salt flats: brine sits in ponds until the water evaporates and the salts concentrate.
   > How: a multiblock tower of Thermal Evaporation Blocks, Valves and one Controller. See Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks in JEI.
   > Tip: taller towers and more heat run faster. Feed it plain water.
12. **Lithium**: 8x `mekanism:dust_lithium` *(after: Evaporation Ponds)*
   > What: lithium dust, for induction cells (and later, tritium).
   > Chemistry: lithium is the lightest metal and gives up its electron easily, which is why lithium-ion batteries store so much energy for their weight.
   > How: run brine through the plant again for liquid lithium. Turn it into gas in a Rotary Condensentrator, then crystallise it in a Chemical Crystallizer.
   > Tip: check JEI with R on Lithium Dust to confirm each step.
13. **Induction Matrix**: 16x `mekanism:induction_casing`; `mekanism:induction_port`; `mekanism:basic_induction_cell`; `mekanism:basic_induction_provider` *(after: Lithium)*
   > Power stored, waiting, obedient. This feels familiar.
   > What: a hollow multiblock battery. Cells inside set how much it stores; providers set how fast it charges and discharges.
   > How: casings form the shell (Structural Glass works for the walls), ports move energy in and out. Layout: Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks.
   > Tip: switch one port to output with the Configurator. Start small; you can rebuild it larger later.
14. **Wireless Grid**: `fluxnetworks:basic_flux_storage`; `fluxnetworks:flux_plug`; `fluxnetworks:flux_point` *(after: Induction Matrix, EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: Flux Networks sends power through the air: Plugs take it in, Points give it out, Storage buffers it.
   > How: the Flux Core needs an MI processing unit (EV) in this pack. Link all devices to one network in their GUI.
   > Tip: put a Flux Plug on your induction matrix's output port and every Point on the network draws from it.
15. **EU Storage**: `modern_industrialization:lv_storage_unit` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: MI's storage unit holds EU, Modern Industrialization's electricity.
   > How: built from redstone batteries, battery alloy plates and an LV hull. Put it between your turbine and your machines.
   > Tip: a buffer lets your steam turbine keep running while machines are idle.
16. **MV Steam Turbine**: `modern_industrialization:mv_steam_turbine` *(after: EU Storage, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: a bigger steam turbine that outputs MV power.
   > Chemistry: a turbine converts the energy of hot, expanding steam into rotation, then a generator turns rotation into current.
   > How: assemble it from an MV hull, aluminum rotors and large motors (check JEI).
17. **Transformers**: `modern_industrialization:lv_mv_transformer` *(after: MV Steam Turbine)*
   > What: a transformer moves energy between two neighbouring voltage tiers (LV and MV here).
   > How: place it between an MV cable and your LV machines (or the other way round). Check which face is which in its tooltip.
   > Tip: real grids do the same: high voltage for transmission, low voltage at the socket.
18. **HV Steam Turbine**: `modern_industrialization:hv_steam_turbine` *(after: Transformers, HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: the HV steam turbine, for HV machine lines.
   > How: check JEI for the recipe. Feed it from a large boiler.
   > Tip: more steam than one turbine can eat? Add a second turbine on the same steam line.
19. **Large Steam Turbine**: `modern_industrialization:large_steam_turbine` *(after: HV Steam Turbine, EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: MI's multiblock steam turbine. It eats steam by the thousands of mB and makes serious EU.
   > How: the controller needs an HV steam turbine, a processing unit (EV) and a highly advanced hull. Layout: JEI or the MI guidebook.
   > Tip: pair it with a large boiler or a reactor's steam output.
20. **Energized Steel**: 8x `powah:steel_energized` *(after: Uranium Dioxide)*
   > What: Powah's basic alloy, made by the Energizing Orb.
   > How: place Energizing Rods aimed at the orb and power them. Put 1 iron + 1 gold in the orb for 2 Energized Steel.
   > Tip: more rods (and better rods) charge faster.
21. **Powah Energy Cells**: `powah:energy_cell_basic` *(after: Energized Steel)*
   > What: Powah's energy storage block, with tiers from Starter to Nitro.
   > How: iron, a dielectric casing and Powah capacitors. Higher tiers use better capacitors and crystals (check JEI).
   > Tip: cells pass power through each other, so a wall of them makes one big buffer.
22. **Thermo Generator**: `powah:thermo_generator_starter` *(after: Powah Energy Cells)*
   > What: a generator that needs a hot block underneath and a coolant fed in.
   > Chemistry: thermoelectric plates make current from a temperature difference (the Seebeck effect). Hotter below and colder inside means more power.
   > How: place it on lava or magma and pipe water in.
23. **Powah Reactor**: 36x `powah:reactor_basic` *(after: Thermo Generator)*
   > What: a multiblock reactor fuelled by the uraninite you oxidised yourself.
   > Chemistry: uraninite is UO₂. Its uranium releases heat as it fissions; the reactor turns that heat into FE.
   > How: place the reactor blocks in a stack and they form one reactor (check JEI). Feed it uraninite.
   > Tip: fuel, temperature and coolant all show in its GUI. Watch the temperature as you scale up.
24. **Photovoltaics**: `mekanismgenerators:solar_generator`; `powah:solar_panel_starter` *(after: MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: solar panels from Mekanism and Powah.
   > Chemistry: silicon solar cells use a p-n junction: a photon knocks an electron loose and the junction pushes it into a current.
   > How: place them under open sky. They stop at night and slow in rain.
   > Tip: pair solar with an energy cube to cover the night.
25. **Advanced Solar**: `mekanismgenerators:advanced_solar_generator` *(after: Photovoltaics)*
   > What: Mekanism's Advanced Solar Generator, a larger array with much more output.
   > How: check JEI for the recipe. Give it space: it's bigger than one block.
   > Tip: like the basic panel, it only works in daylight. Buffer it with a cube.
26. **Wind Power**: `mekanismgenerators:wind_generator` *(after: Photovoltaics)*
   > What: Mekanism's Wind Generator. It works day and night.
   > How: it makes more power the higher it's placed. Build it on a mountain or a tall tower.
   > Tip: it needs open space around it, so leave room.
27. **Reactor Safety**: 2x `mekanismgenerators:fission_reactor_logic_adapter` *(after: Fission)*
   > What: Logic Adapters let redstone read the reactor and switch it off.
   > Chemistry: a fission reactor makes heat by splitting heavy nuclei. If heat can't escape fast enough, the reactor takes damage; at 100% it melts down.
   > How: set one adapter to emit a signal on damage or high temperature (pick the mode in its GUI) and wire it to another in Activation mode.
   > Tip: start at a low burn rate (0.1 mB/t), keep coolant flowing, and drain waste and heated coolant constantly.
28. **Industrial Turbine**: 16x `mekanismgenerators:turbine_casing` *(after: Reactor Safety)*
   > What: Mekanism's multiblock turbine. Feed it steam from fission or a boiler and it makes huge amounts of FE.
   > How: casings, valves, vents, rotors with blades, a rotational complex, a pressure disperser and electromagnetic coils. Layout: Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks.
   > Tip: a Saturating Condenser turns spent steam back into water, closing the loop.
29. **D-T Fuel**: `mekanism:solar_neutron_activator`; `mekanismgenerators:hohlraum` *(after: Industrial Turbine, Lithium)*
   > What: fusion fuel: deuterium and tritium, the two heavy isotopes of hydrogen.
   > Chemistry: deuterium comes from heavy water (D₂O). Tritium is bred from lithium by neutrons: ⁶Li + n → ³H + ⁴He.
   > How: electrolyse heavy water for deuterium; put lithium in the Solar Neutron Activator for tritium. Combine them into D-T fuel in the Chemical Infuser, then fill a Hohlraum with it (a chemical tank's item slot works; see its tooltip).
   > Tip: heavy water comes from a Mekanism Electric Pump with a Filter Upgrade.
30. **Fusion**: `mekanismgenerators:fusion_reactor_controller` *(after: D-T Fuel, Transmutation by Neutron)*
   > The power of a star, in a box you built. This is what you were.
   > What: Mekanism's fusion reactor, the top of the power tree.
   > Chemistry: D + T → ⁴He + n + 17.6 MeV, millions of times more energy per kilogram than any chemical reaction.
   > How: a filled Hohlraum in the controller, then a burst from a Laser through a Laser Amplifier to ignite it. Layout: Just Enough Mekanism Multiblocks.
31. **Upgrade in Place**: `generatorgalore:iron_to_gold_upgrade` *(after: A Faster Burn)*
   > What: Generators Galore upgrade kits turn a placed generator into the next tier, keeping its fuel and connections.
   > How: gold ingots, an item frame and a redstone block for iron → gold (check JEI for the others). Right-click the placed generator.
   > Tip: the tree branches: gold leads to diamond or to the food-burning Culinary Generator.
32. **Eight Times the Burn**: `generatorgalore:iron_generator_8x` *(after: A Faster Burn, An Ancient City)*
   > What: 8x generators burn fuel eight times as fast and make eight times the FE in one block.
   > How: eight generators of a tier around an echo shard (check JEI). Echo shards come from Ancient City chests.
   > Tip: a 64x version exists too, for when space matters more than echo shards.
33. **Burning a Star**: `generatorgalore:netherstar_generator` *(after: Generator Collector)*
   > You remember lighting stars. Burning one feels like a waste, and also like coming home.
   > What: the Nether Star Generator burns Nether Stars for an enormous amount of FE.
   > Chemistry: real stars run on fusion: hydrogen nuclei fuse into helium (4 ¹H → ⁴He) and a little mass becomes a lot of energy, E = mc².
34. **Blazing Crystals**: 8x `powah:crystal_blazing`; `powah:energy_cell_blazing` *(after: Powah Reactor, Blaze Rods)*
   > What: every Powah tier is built from a crystal made in the Energizing Orb. Blazing crystals unlock the blazing tier of cells, generators and cables.
   > How: energize a blaze rod (or four blaze powder) with 120,000 FE. Then craft blazing-tier parts (check JEI).
   > Tip: feed the energizing rods from your biggest power source; crystals charge faster with more rods.
35. **Niotic Crystals**: 4x `powah:crystal_niotic`; `powah:energy_cell_niotic` *(after: Blazing Crystals)*
   > What: the niotic tier. Reactors and generators get much stronger.
   > Chemistry: diamond is pure carbon in a rigid lattice where every atom bonds to four others. Pumping energy into it is Powah's magic, but diamond really does conduct heat better than any metal.
   > How: energize a diamond with 300,000 FE (check JEI).
36. **Spirited Crystals**: 4x `powah:crystal_spirited`; `powah:energy_cell_spirited` *(after: Niotic Crystals)*
   > What: the spirited tier, one step below the top.
   > Chemistry: emerald is the mineral beryl, Be₃Al₂Si₆O₁₈. A trace of chromium (Cr³⁺) replacing some aluminum gives it its green colour.
   > How: energize an emerald with 1,000,000 FE (check JEI). A villager trading hall pays off here.
37. **Nitro Crystals**: 16x `powah:crystal_nitro` *(after: Spirited Crystals)*
   > What: nitro crystals build the strongest Powah reactors and cells.
   > How: energize a Nether Star, two redstone blocks and a blazing crystal block with 20,000,000 FE. One craft makes sixteen.
   > Tip: save your Nether Stars for this; the Wither is the only reliable source (check JEI for a Hostile Neural Networks wither model).
38. **Ender Power**: `powah:ender_cell_basic`; `powah:player_transmitter_basic` *(after: Powah Energy Cells)*
   > What: Ender Cells share one energy pool across your base; the Player Transmitter charges the items in your inventory.
   > How: both need an Ender Core (an eye of ender, dielectric casing and a tiny capacitor, energized). Check JEI, and link them with a Binding Card.
   > Tip: a player transmitter keeps jetpacks and the MekaSuit charged while you work.
39. **Herculean Storage**: `fluxnetworks:herculean_flux_storage` *(after: Wireless Grid)*
   > What: Herculean (and then Gargantuan) Flux Storage holds far more energy in one block, shared with the whole flux network.
   > How: Basic Flux Storage blocks around glass panes (check JEI). Gargantuan is the same with Herculean blocks.
   > Tip: flux storage keeps its energy when broken, so you can carry charged batteries to a new base.
40. **Flux Controller**: `fluxnetworks:flux_controller`; `fluxnetworks:flux_configurator` *(after: Wireless Grid)*
   > What: the Flux Controller sends network energy wirelessly into players' inventories. The Configurator copies settings between flux devices.
   > How: flux blocks, a Flux Core and flux dust (check JEI). The Flux Core needs an MI processing unit in this pack.
   > Tip: turn charging off for your armor slots if it fights with other chargers.

### Mob Data and Big Crafting

*Monifactory-inspired machinery: simulate mobs, craft on giant tables, and fold whole factories into a block.*

1. **Deep Learning**: `hostilenetworks:deep_learner`; `hostilenetworks:blank_data_model` *(after: Iron)*
   > You remember knowing every creature by name. You can learn them again, one fight at a time.
   > What: Hostile Neural Networks records mobs into data models. Trained models later make mob drops without the mob.
   > How: craft the Deep Learner and a Blank Data Model (check JEI). Put models in the Deep Learner and keep it in your inventory while you fight.
   > Tip: start with a mob whose drops you'll need in bulk, like blazes, slimes or endermen.
2. **Your First Model**: `hostilenetworks:data_model` *(after: Deep Learning)*
   > What: a data model attuned to one mob type. It starts at Faulty and levels up as you kill that mob.
   > How: right-click a mob with a Blank Data Model, or craft a specific model (check JEI). Then hunt with the Deep Learner.
   > Tip: tiers go Faulty, Basic, Advanced, Superior, Self Aware. Higher tiers are more accurate, so fewer simulations fail.
3. **Simulation**: `hostilenetworks:sim_chamber`; 4x `hostilenetworks:prediction_matrix` *(after: Your First Model, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: the Simulation Chamber runs a trained model on FE and prediction matrices to make predictions and mob drops.
   > How: it needs an MV electronic circuit (aluminum chemistry). Insert a model of Basic tier or better, matrices and power.
   > Tip: pipe matrices in and drops out; one chamber per model keeps things simple.
4. **Loot Fabricator**: `hostilenetworks:loot_fabricator` *(after: Simulation)*
   > You remember making things appear from nothing. This is the mortal version: data, power and patience.
   > What: turns predictions into the specific drop you pick (blaze rods, ender pearls, slime balls and more).
   > How: also needs an MV electronic circuit. Feed it predictions from the chamber and select the output in its GUI.
   > Tip: simulated blazes are a cheap blaze-rod source for Silent Gear alloys and brewing.
5. **Self Aware**: checkmark *(after: Simulation)*
   > What: train one data model all the way to Self Aware, the top tier.
   > How: keep killing that mob with the model in your Deep Learner, or let the Simulation Chamber train it. Click the checkmark when you've done it.
   > Tip: a Self Aware model almost never fails a simulation.
6. **Twilight Predictions**: 4x `hostilenetworks:twilight_prediction` *(after: Simulation, The Twilight Forest)*
   > What: Twilight Forest mobs give their own prediction type.
   > How: train a model on a Twilight mob and run it in the Simulation Chamber.
   > Tip: the Loot Fabricator shows which drops each prediction can become.
7. **Black Iron Parts**: `extendedcrafting:black_iron_slate`; `extendedcrafting:basic_component`; `extendedcrafting:basic_catalyst` *(after: Steel)*
   > What: Extended Crafting tables are built from black iron slates plus a component and a catalyst of matching tier.
   > How: black iron ingot = iron ingot + black dye (shapeless). Two ingots make a slate. A component takes a slate, luminessence (glowstone, redstone, gunpowder) and iron; four components ring a black iron ingot for the catalyst.
   > Tip: make extra black iron; every table tier and the Crafting Core need more.
8. **Extended Crafting**: 8x `extendedcrafting:black_iron_ingot`; `extendedcrafting:basic_table` *(after: Black Iron Parts)*
   > What: the Basic Table is a 3x3 table for Extended Crafting recipes, and the base of every bigger table.
   > How: black iron slate, basic component and catalyst, an iron block and a crafting table.
   > Tip: each bigger table is crafted from the one before it, so don't use this one up as decoration.
9. **5x5: Advanced Table**: `extendedcrafting:advanced_table` *(after: Extended Crafting, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: a 5x5 crafting grid for mid-game recipes.
   > Chemistry: its MI electronic circuit rests on aluminum, won by electrolysis of alumina: 2Al₂O₃ → 4Al + 3O₂.
   > How: the Basic Table, gold blocks, advanced component and catalyst, plus the electronic circuit (check JEI).
10. **7x7: Elite Table**: `extendedcrafting:elite_table` *(after: 5x5: Advanced Table, HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: a 7x7 grid. Crystaltine and many late parts are crafted here.
   > Chemistry: the MI digital circuit needs polyethylene, chains of ethylene (C₂H₄) from cracking oil.
   > How: the Advanced Table, diamond blocks, elite component and catalyst, plus the digital circuit.
11. **9x9: Ultimate Table**: `extendedcrafting:ultimate_table` *(after: 7x7: Elite Table, EV: Processing Units)*
   > You remember building worlds on a table far bigger than this. Nine by nine will do for now.
   > What: the endgame's biggest crafts happen here, including the Seed of a New World.
   > Chemistry: the MI processing unit needs PVC: polyvinyl chloride, (C₂H₃Cl)ₙ, made from ethylene and chlorine.
   > How: the Elite Table, emerald blocks, ultimate component and catalyst, plus the processing unit.
12. **Packaged Automation**: `packagedauto:packager`; `packagedauto:unpackager` *(after: The God's Archive, 5x5: Advanced Table)*
   > What: Packaged Auto lets AE2 autocraft with Extended Crafting tables, through the PackagedExCrafting addon.
   > How: encode recipes in the Encoder, then set up a Packager, a PackagedExCrafting crafter and an Unpackager by your ME network (tooltips and JEI explain each block).
   > Tip: one setup per table size handles every recipe of that size.
13. **Ender Crafting**: `extendedcrafting:ender_crafter`; 2x `extendedcrafting:ender_alternator` *(after: Black Iron Parts, Into the Fire)*
   > What: the Ender Crafter finishes its recipes over time, faster with more Ender Alternators nearby.
   > How: ender ingots (iron + ender pearl), eyes of ender and a crafting table (check JEI).
   > Tip: a ring of alternators around the crafter speeds it up a lot.
14. **Combination Crafting**: `extendedcrafting:crafting_core`; 4x `extendedcrafting:pedestal` *(after: 7x7: Elite Table)*
   > What: put the main item on the Crafting Core and the extras on Pedestals around it. The core uses FE to fuse them.
   > How: elite components and catalysts, a frame and black iron (check JEI). JEI shows every combination recipe.
   > Tip: pipe FE into the core; it won't start without power.
15. **Crystaltine**: 4x `extendedcrafting:crystaltine_ingot` *(after: 7x7: Elite Table)*
   > What: a late crafting metal made on the Elite Table.
   > Chemistry: lapis lazuli's blue comes from lazurite, a sodium aluminosilicate with trapped sulfur ions (S₃⁻).
   > How: diamonds, lapis, iron, gold and a Nether Star in a 7x4 pattern (check JEI).
16. **Compact Machines**: `compactmachines:personal_shrinking_device` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: fold an entire factory into a single block. The Personal Shrinking Device lets you walk inside it.
   > How: shrinking and enlarging modules, an eye of ender, copper, iron and glass panes (check JEI).
   > Tip: it also shows you which machine room you're in and takes you back out.
17. **Machine Walls**: 16x `compactmachines:wall` *(after: Compact Machines)*
   > What: Compact Machine Walls frame every machine block.
   > How: crafted from polished deepslate (check JEI).
   > Tip: inside a room the walls are unbreakable; these crafted ones aren't.
18. **Atom Modules**: 2x `compactmachines:shrinking_module`; 2x `compactmachines:enlarging_module` *(after: Machine Walls)*
   > What: every Compact Machine needs one Atom Shrinking Module and one Atom Enlarging Module.
   > How: an eye of ender, a light pressure plate, a stone button and a piston (sticky for shrinking).
   > Tip: make spares; each new machine eats a pair.
19. **Step Inside**: visit `compactmachines:compact_world` *(after: Atom Modules)*
   > You remember holding a world in your palm. This one is smaller, but it's yours.
   > What: craft a Compact Machine, place it, and right-click it with the Personal Shrinking Device to enter its room.
   > How: walls + both modules + a metal sets the size: copper = tiny, iron = small, gold = normal, obsidian = giant, netherite = colossal (check JEI for the rest).
   > Tip: tunnels carry items, fluids and power through the walls; perfect for a compact chemical line.
20. **Hide the Cables**: `cable_facades:facade_wrench` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: Cable Facades disguise cables and pipes as any block. Tidy factories, same throughput.
   > How: craft the Facade Wrench, then apply blocks to cables (check JEI).
   > Tip: facades on MI pipes make chemical lines much easier to look at.
21. **Teleporting Cake**: `telepastries:overworld_cake` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: TelePastries cakes teleport you to their dimension when you eat a slice.
   > How: the Overworld Cake brings you home. Refill it with saplings; the Nether Cake takes obsidian.
   > Tip: place the Overworld Cake in your Nether hub for a quick ride home.
22. **Cake at the End**: `telepastries:end_cake` *(after: Teleporting Cake, The End)*
   > What: a cake that sends you to the End.
   > How: a cake surrounded by eyes of ender. Refill it with eyes of ender.
   > Tip: leave it in your base so the End is one bite away.
23. **Training Data: Blazes**: kill 30x `minecraft:blaze` *(after: Your First Model, Into the Fire)*
   > What: every kill with a matching model in your Deep Learner adds data. Blaze models are worth it: blaze rods feed brewing, Powah and Create's Blaze Burners.
   > How: put a blaze data model in the Deep Learner, keep it in your inventory, and hunt blazes at a Nether Fortress.
   > Tip: a blaze model at a high tier turns the Simulation Chamber into an endless rod farm.
24. **Training Data: Endermen**: kill 30x `minecraft:enderman` *(after: Your First Model)*
   > What: ender pearls go into Entangled blocks, AE2 seeds, Powah ender cells and teleporting. An enderman model makes them renewable.
   > How: load an enderman data model and hunt them at night, in warped forests, or in the End.
   > Tip: wear a carved pumpkin, or look at their feet, so they don't attack you first.
25. **Three Worlds of Data**: 4x `hostilenetworks:overworld_prediction`; 4x `hostilenetworks:nether_prediction`; 4x `hostilenetworks:end_prediction` *(after: Simulation, The End)*
   > What: each simulation gives a prediction for the mob's home dimension as well as the drop. Overworld, Nether and End predictions are used in recipes and Fabrication Directives.
   > How: run simulations of one mob from each dimension, such as a zombie, a blaze and an enderman.
   > Tip: predictions stack up fast; send them to storage with a pipe.
26. **The Data Center**: `hostilenetworks:data_center`; `hostilenetworks:data_center_io_port` *(after: Loot Fabricator)*
   > You remember watching a million lives at once. This is a pale copy, but a useful one.
   > What: the Data Center runs up to 25 Self Aware data models at the same time, each making one drop per cycle.
   > How: a Sim Chamber, a Loot Fabricator, a Nether Star, echo shards and obsidian. Build a 7x7x7 hollow shell: obsidian floor, black stained glass walls and ceiling, with the controller in a bottom wall facing out (JEI shows the rules).
   > Tip: IO ports in the shell let pipes feed inputs and pull the drops. A Fabrication Directive picks which drop each slot makes.
27. **Flux Crafting**: `extendedcrafting:flux_crafter`; 2x `extendedcrafting:flux_alternator` *(after: Extended Crafting)*
   > What: the Flux Crafter runs special recipes using FE delivered by nearby Flux Alternators, like the Flux Star.
   > How: gold and redstone ingots (check JEI). Place alternators around the crafter and power them.
   > Tip: JEI's Flux Crafter tab lists every recipe and the FE each one needs.
28. **Auto Tables**: `extendedcrafting:basic_auto_table` *(after: Crystaltine)*
   > What: Auto Tables remember a recipe and craft it with FE whenever the ingredients are inside. Each table size has its own.
   > How: a crafting table of the same size plus crystaltine components (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Ultimate Auto Table is also the heart of Packaged ExCrafting's Ultimate Crafter (see the Applied Energistics page).
29. **A Room for Every Line**: 4x `compactmachines:machine` *(after: Step Inside)*
   > What: one compact machine per chemical line keeps your base tidy: a sulfuric acid room, a chlorine room, a petrochemical room.
   > How: craft more machines (check JEI for the sizes) and enter each with the Personal Shrinking Device.
   > Tip: a room only runs while the chunk with its machine block is loaded, so claim and force-load your factory.

### Automation and Logic

*Make the factory run itself.*

1. **A Computer**: `computercraft:computer_normal` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > You never did your own chores. You remember having things that did them for you.
   > What: CC: Tweaked computers run Lua programs: monitor tanks, balance chemical lines, build a base dashboard.
   > How: place it and right-click. Type help, or edit startup to write a program that runs on boot.
   > Tip: Mekanism machines can be read by computers directly. Put a computer next to one and try peripheral.getNames().
2. **Monitors**: 4x `computercraft:monitor_advanced` *(after: A Computer)*
   > What: monitors show program output on a wall. Placed together, they join into one big screen.
   > How: put monitors next to the computer and run monitor <side> <program> to send output to them.
   > Tip: advanced monitors show colour and accept clicks, so you can make touch buttons.
3. **Wired Networks**: 2x `computercraft:wired_modem`; 16x `computercraft:cable` *(after: Monitors)*
   > What: wired modems and networking cable connect a computer to machines that aren't touching it.
   > How: put a modem on the machine, run cable back to a modem on the computer, and right-click the machine's modem to attach it.
   > Tip: each attached peripheral gets a name. List them with peripheral.getNames().
4. **Ender Modem**: `computercraft:wireless_modem_advanced`; `computercraft:pocket_computer_advanced` *(after: Wired Networks)*
   > What: the Ender Modem sends messages with no range limit, even between dimensions. A pocket computer carries a terminal with you.
   > How: use rednet on both ends: one computer broadcasts tank levels, your pocket computer reads them.
   > Tip: a remote alarm for your reactor is a very good idea.
5. **Turtles**: `computercraft:turtle_normal` *(after: A Computer)*
   > What: turtles are programmable robots that mine, build and farm.
   > How: craft one with a tool (a pickaxe makes a mining turtle) and fuel it with coal.
   > Tip: try the built-in excavate and tunnel programs first.
6. **Advanced Peripherals**: `advancedperipherals:energy_detector`; `advancedperipherals:me_bridge` *(after: Wired Networks, The God's Archive)*
   > What: extra peripherals: the Energy Detector measures power flow; the ME Bridge reads (and requests crafts from) your whole AE2 network.
   > How: place them on your wired network like any other peripheral.
   > Tip: an ME Bridge program can keep your stock of every intermediate topped up.
7. **Base Dashboard**: `advancedperipherals:fluid_detector`; 8x `computercraft:monitor_advanced` *(after: Advanced Peripherals)*
   > What: a wall screen that shows your chemical stocks, power and flow rates at a glance.
   > How: the Fluid Detector measures flow through a pipe. Combine it with tank readings and the energy detector in one program.
   > Tip: colour lines red when a tank drops below 20%. You'll spot problems from across the room.
8. **Buses**: `ae2:import_bus`; `ae2:export_bus`; `ae2:storage_bus` *(after: The God's Archive)*
   > What: an import bus pulls items out of a machine, an export bus pushes them in, and a storage bus lets ME see inside a chest or tank.
   > How: place them on cable facing the machine. Set filters in each bus's GUI.
   > Tip: Applied Mekanistics is in the pack, so buses and storage also handle Mekanism gases like hydrogen and SO₂.
9. **Crafting CPU**: 2x `ae2:crafting_unit`; `ae2:1k_crafting_storage` *(after: Buses)*
   > What: a crafting CPU is a multiblock that works out and tracks autocrafting jobs.
   > How: build a solid box of crafting units with at least one crafting storage, and connect it to your network.
   > Tip: more storage lets it plan bigger jobs; co-processing units run more steps at once.
10. **Encoding Patterns**: `ae2:pattern_encoding_terminal`; 8x `ae2:blank_pattern` *(after: Crafting CPU)*
   > What: a pattern is a recipe card: these inputs make that output.
   > How: in the Pattern Encoding Terminal, switch to Processing mode for machine recipes and fill in the inputs and outputs.
   > Tip: processing patterns work for any machine: MI, Mekanism or the Combiner.
11. **Autocraft a Chemical Line**: `ae2:pattern_provider`; `ae2:molecular_assembler` *(after: Encoding Patterns)*
   > What: pattern providers push ingredients into machines; molecular assemblers do crafting-table recipes.
   > How: put a provider against an MI or Mekanism machine, load a processing pattern, and send the output back into the network.
   > Tip: chain patterns (sulfur → SO₂ → SO₃ → H₂SO₄) and one request runs the whole line.
12. **Level Emitters**: `ae2:level_emitter` *(after: Base Dashboard, Buses)*
   > What: a level emitter gives a redstone signal when the network holds more (or less) than a number you set.
   > How: set the item and the amount, then wire the signal to a machine's redstone control.
   > Tip: stop the Electrolytic Separator when hydrogen hits your target and you never waste power on a full tank.
13. **Fully Automated Sulfuric Acid**: 16x `modern_industrialization:sulfuric_acid_bucket`; checkmark *(after: Autocraft a Chemical Line, Level Emitters)*
   > The factory hums without you. You remember this kind of quiet.
   > What: a line that makes sulfuric acid on its own and stops when the tanks are full.
   > Chemistry: 2SO₂ + O₂ → 2SO₃ (industry uses a vanadium oxide catalyst), then SO₃ + H₂O → H₂SO₄.
   > How: Separator (O₂) → Oxidizer (S → SO₂) → Infuser (SO₃) → Rotary Condensentrator (water vapour) → Infuser (H₂SO₄). Feed sulfur with a bus or LaserIO and gate it with a level emitter.
   > Tip: when it runs a full day with no help, fill 16 buckets and click the checkmark.
14. **Menril Resin**: 16x `integrateddynamics:crystalized_menril_chunk` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: Integrated Dynamics builds everything from crystallised Menril, a resin from Menril trees.
   > Chemistry: drying resin is just evaporation: the liquid loses its solvent and hardens, like tree sap turning into amber.
   > How: squeeze Menril logs in a Squeezer for resin, then dry the resin in a Drying Basin.
   > Tip: Menril trees grow wild in the Overworld. Plant the saplings once you find one.
15. **Integrated Dynamics**: 8x `integrateddynamics:cable`; `integrateddynamics:logic_programmer`; 4x `integrateddynamics:variable` *(after: Menril Resin)*
   > What: read a value (tank level, item count), apply logic, and act on it, all with blocks and cards.
   > How: a Reader part (e.g. Fluid Reader) on a tank exposes values. Write one onto a Variable Card in the Logic Programmer.
   > Tip: a Redstone Writer can then turn that value into a signal: perfect for stopping a separator when its tank is full.
16. **Variable Store**: `integrateddynamics:variablestore`; `integrateddynamics:labeller` *(after: Integrated Dynamics)*
   > What: the Variable Store holds variable cards so the whole network can use them; the Labeller names them.
   > How: put cards in the store and reference them from any writer or display panel on the network.
   > Tip: name cards after what they measure ('H2 tank %'). You'll thank yourself later.
17. **Challenge: The Self-Stopping Separator**: checkmark *(after: Variable Store)*
   > What: make a Mekanism Electrolytic Separator turn itself off when its hydrogen tank is 90% full, and back on below 50%.
   > How: a Reader on the tank → a comparison in the Logic Programmer → a Redstone Writer on the separator (set it to redstone control in its GUI).
   > Tip: the gap between 50% and 90% (hysteresis) stops it flickering on and off.
   > Click the checkmark once it works.
18. **Super Factory Manager**: `sfm:manager`; 8x `sfm:cable`; `sfm:disk` *(after: Integrated Dynamics)*
   > What: one Manager block runs a short program that moves items, fluids and energy between everything on its cables.
   > How: put the disk in the Manager and write a program, e.g. EVERY 20 TICKS DO INPUT FROM a OUTPUT TO b END.
   > Tip: the in-game editor has examples. Start with one input and one output.
19. **Labels**: `sfm:labelgun`; `sfm:network_tool` *(after: Super Factory Manager)*
   > What: the Label Gun tags inventories with names (like a and b above) for your programs.
   > How: set a label in the gun, then right-click each machine. The Network Tool shows what's connected.
   > Tip: label by job ('sulfur_in', 'acid_out'), not by machine type.
20. **Logistics Network**: 2x `logisticsnetworks:logistics_node` *(after: Labels)*
   > What: Logistics Network nodes move items between inventories according to rules you set.
   > How: place nodes on the machines and set each node's direction and rules in its GUI (check JEI and the tooltips).
   > Tip: nodes are compact: good for tight spaces where cables won't fit.
21. **Logistics Filters**: `logisticsnetworks:tag_filter`; `logisticsnetworks:amount_filter` *(after: Logistics Network)*
   > What: filters narrow what a node moves: by tag, by amount and more.
   > How: put a filter in a node's slot and set it up in its GUI (check JEI for all the filter types).
   > Tip: a tag filter for 'c:dusts' sends every dust to one chest.
22. **LaserIO Cards**: 2x `laserio:card_item` *(after: LaserIO)*
   > What: cards tell each LaserIO node what to move and in which direction.
   > How: put a card in a node's side slot. Set it to Extract on the source and Insert on the destination.
   > Tip: right-click with a card in your hand to open its settings.
23. **Fluids and Gases by Laser**: 2x `laserio:card_fluid`; 2x `laserio:card_chemical` *(after: LaserIO Cards)*
   > What: fluid cards move liquids; chemical cards move Mekanism gases, like hydrogen and SO₂.
   > How: same as item cards: Extract on the tank, Insert on the machine.
   > Tip: one node can run several cards on one side. Hydrogen and oxygen out of one separator, two cards.
24. **LaserIO Filters**: `laserio:filter_basic`; `laserio:filter_tag`; `laserio:filter_count` *(after: Fluids and Gases by Laser)*
   > What: filters go inside cards. Basic matches a list, Tag matches a tag, Count keeps a set number in stock.
   > How: put the filter in a card's filter slot and set it up.
   > Tip: a count filter on an insert card keeps exactly 64 sulfur in the oxidizer and no more.
25. **Overclocking**: 2x `laserio:overclocker_card`; 2x `laserio:overclocker_node` *(after: LaserIO Filters)*
   > What: overclockers make LaserIO move more, faster.
   > How: overclocker cards go in a card's upgrade slot; overclocker nodes go in the node.
   > Tip: most lines don't need it. Add it when a machine starves.
26. **Printers and Disks**: `computercraft:printer`; `computercraft:disk_drive` *(after: A Computer)*
   > What: a Disk Drive lets you save programs on floppy disks and carry them between computers. A Printer prints text onto paper.
   > How: check JEI. Put a disk in the drive and it appears as disk/ in the computer's files.
   > Tip: print a list of your reactions and their inputs and bind the pages into a book: a real lab notebook.
27. **Advanced Turtle**: `computercraft:turtle_advanced` *(after: Turtles)*
   > What: advanced turtles have colour screens and can be clicked, which makes menus easy.
   > How: an advanced computer with gold instead of iron around it (check JEI). Give it a pickaxe to make a Mining Turtle.
   > Tip: run excavate 16 on a mining turtle and it digs a 16x16 hole down to bedrock, bringing everything back.
28. **Geo Scanner**: `advancedperipherals:geo_scanner` *(after: Advanced Peripherals)*
   > What: the Geo Scanner lists every block around it within a radius, including ores, for your computer to read.
   > Chemistry: real geologists find ore bodies by measuring gravity, magnetism and how rocks conduct electricity, rather than digging blind.
   > How: check JEI. Put it next to a computer (or in a pocket computer) and call scan(8).
   > Tip: write a program that counts ore blocks by type: it's a quick way to check a deposit before setting up a mine.
29. **Chat Box and Player Detector**: `advancedperipherals:chat_box`; `advancedperipherals:player_detector` *(after: Advanced Peripherals)*
   > What: the Chat Box lets a computer send chat messages; the Player Detector tells it who is nearby.
   > How: check JEI. Place them next to a computer.
   > Tip: have the base warn everyone in chat when a tank of hydrogen is nearly full or an induction matrix is running low.
30. **Inventory Manager**: `advancedperipherals:inventory_manager`; `advancedperipherals:memory_card` *(after: Advanced Peripherals)*
   > What: the Inventory Manager lets a computer move items into and out of your own inventory, once you bind it with a Memory Card.
   > How: right-click the Memory Card to bind it to yourself, then put it in the manager (check JEI).
   > Tip: a button that refills your hotbar with torches and food from storage is a classic first program.
31. **Integrated Tunnels**: checkmark *(after: Variable Store)*
   > What: Integrated Tunnels adds importer, exporter and interface parts for Integrated Dynamics cables: items, fluids and energy move along the network, controlled by variables.
   > How: craft an Item Importer and an Item Exporter (check JEI), put them on cables at a chest and a machine, and give the exporter a variable card that says what to move. Click the checkmark when it works.
   > Tip: a fluid exporter with a boolean variable from a tank reader only fills the reactor when it's below half.
32. **Integrated Terminals**: `integratedterminals:terminal_storage_portable` *(after: Integrated Tunnels)*
   > What: Integrated Terminals adds a Storage Terminal part that shows everything connected to an Integrated Dynamics network, plus a portable version.
   > How: a Storage Terminal part between two crystalized menril chunks makes the portable terminal (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Crafting Job Terminal shows what Integrated Crafting is working on.
33. **Integrated Crafting**: checkmark *(after: Integrated Tunnels)*
   > What: Integrated Crafting adds a Crafting Interface: give it recipe variables and it crafts on demand through the ID network, much like AE2 patterns.
   > How: craft a Crafting Interface (check JEI), write recipes into variable cards with the Logic Programmer, and put them in the interface. Click the checkmark when one craft works.
   > Tip: it's a cheaper autocrafter than AE2 for small chains, like rubber sheets or redstone batteries.
34. **Your First SFM Program**: checkmark *(after: Super Factory Manager)*
   > What: Super Factory Manager runs small text programs written on a disk. Labels name the blocks it talks to.
   > How: label a chest a and a furnace b with the Label Gun, then write this program on the disk and put it in the manager. Click the checkmark when items start moving.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Program:
   > EVERY 20 TICKS DO
   >     INPUT FROM a
   >     OUTPUT TO b
   > END
   > Tip: add a side, like OUTPUT TO b TOP SIDE, to choose which face of the block is used. The manager's built-in examples cover fluids and energy too.

### The Mechanical Age

*Create: rotation, presses and mixers. An early mechanical age that runs alongside steam and feeds into Modern Industrialization.*

1. **Andesite Alloy**: 16x `create:andesite_alloy` *(after: Iron)*
   > Gears and shafts. You remember a world that ran on clockwork, and you liked it.
   > What: andesite alloy, Create's first building material.
   > How: craft andesite with iron nuggets (zinc nuggets work too). Later a Mechanical Mixer makes it in bulk.
   > Tip: hold W over almost any Create block to watch its Ponder tutorial.
2. **Andesite Casing**: 8x `create:andesite_casing` *(after: Andesite Alloy)*
   > What: casing blocks are the shell for gearboxes, deployers and more.
   > How: right-click a stripped log or stripped wood with andesite alloy. That's 'item application', and Create uses it a lot.
   > Tip: strip logs with an axe first. Any wood type works.
3. **Rotational Power**: 8x `create:shaft`; 8x `create:cogwheel`; `create:water_wheel` *(after: Andesite Casing)*
   > What: Create runs on rotation, not electricity. A water wheel on flowing water turns shafts and cogs.
   > How: water must flow across the wheel's blades. Shafts carry rotation straight; cogwheels pass it sideways.
   > Tip: every machine costs stress units (SU). If the network is over capacity, everything stops.
4. **Goggles and Wrench**: `create:goggles`; `create:wrench` *(after: Rotational Power)*
   > What: Engineer's Goggles show speed and stress when you look at a machine; the wrench rotates and picks up Create blocks.
   > How: wear the goggles in your helmet slot and look at a shaft or generator.
   > Tip: sneak-right-click with the wrench to pick a block up instantly.
5. **Windmills**: `create:windmill_bearing`; 8x `create:white_sail` *(after: Rotational Power)*
   > What: a windmill bearing with sails attached spins without water.
   > How: place the bearing, build at least 8 sails onto the front of it (the Ponder shows how), and right-click the bearing.
   > Tip: more sails means more stress capacity but slower speed. Check the numbers with your goggles.
6. **Create: Connected**: `create_connected:brass_gearbox`; `create_connected:kinetic_bridge` *(after: Windmills)*
   > What: Create: Connected adds small conveniences: compact gearboxes, a kinetic bridge that carries rotation across gaps, and more.
   > How: check JEI for everything the mod adds.
   > Tip: the kinetic bridge saves long runs of shafts.
7. **Create Deco**: 8x `createdeco:brass_catwalk`; 8x `createdeco:andesite_sheet_metal` *(after: Andesite Casing)*
   > What: catwalks, sheet metal, cage lamps and other industrial building blocks.
   > How: check JEI for Create Deco recipes. Many use a press or the stonecutter.
   > Tip: catwalks over your machine lines make maintenance much easier.
8. **Gearboxes and Belts**: 2x `create:gearbox`; 4x `create:belt_connector` *(after: Windmills)*
   > What: gearboxes turn rotation through corners; belts carry both rotation and items between two shafts.
   > How: belts are made from dried kelp. Click one shaft with a belt, then the other, to link them.
   > Tip: belts make great machine feeders: items ride past presses and fans on their own.
9. **Steam Engines**: 2x `create:steam_engine`; 4x `create:fluid_tank` *(after: Heated Mixing)*
   > What: Create's boiler: a fluid tank full of water, heated by Blaze Burners, drives steam engines on its sides.
   > Chemistry: water expands about 1,600 times when it boils. That pressure pushes the pistons.
   > How: pump water into the tank, put fuelled Blaze Burners underneath, and place engines on its sides with a shaft in front. Check the Ponder.
   > Tip: a bigger tank and more burners raise the boiler level. Goggles show what's limiting it.
10. **Pressing and Mixing**: `create:mechanical_press`; `create:basin` *(after: Gearboxes and Belts)*
   > What: the Mechanical Press flattens ingots into plates and compacts items in a basin.
   > How: put the press over a depot, a belt or a basin, and power it.
   > Tip: plates are shared with MI in this pack, so pressed plates work in MI recipes too.
11. **Heated Mixing**: `create:mechanical_mixer`; `create:blaze_burner` *(after: Pressing and Mixing, Blaze Rods)*
   > What: a mixer over a basin, heated by a Blaze Burner, makes alloys and fluids.
   > Chemistry: heat speeds up reactions and melts metals so they can mix into alloys.
   > How: catch a blaze in an Empty Blaze Burner, put it under the basin, feed it fuel, and put the mixer above.
   > Tip: feed the burner with a deployer or a mechanical arm so it never goes cold.
12. **Crushing**: `create:millstone`; 2x `create:crushing_wheel` *(after: Gearboxes and Belts)*
   > What: millstones and crushing wheels grind ore into crushed ore and stone into gravel and sand.
   > How: two crushing wheels side by side, spinning toward each other. Drop items in from above.
   > Tip: in this pack they give at most 2x per ore block, like everything before acids.
13. **Bulk Washing**: `create:encased_fan`; 8x `create:crushed_raw_iron` *(after: Crushing)*
   > What: an encased fan blowing through water washes crushed ore into nuggets plus a byproduct.
   > How: put flowing water in front of the fan and pass items through the air stream (a belt works).
   > Tip: washed crushed iron gives 9 nuggets and a 75% chance of redstone.
14. **Rose Quartz**: 4x `create:rose_quartz`; 4x `create:polished_rose_quartz` *(after: Bulk Washing)*
   > What: rose quartz, for electron tubes.
   > How: craft 1 Nether quartz with 8 redstone, then polish it: hold sandpaper with the rose quartz in your off-hand and use it (a deployer can do this too).
   > Tip: washing iron makes redstone, so your ore line can feed this.
15. **Electron Tubes**: 4x `create:electron_tube` *(after: Rose Quartz)*
   > What: electron tubes go into deployers, mechanical crafters and other smart machines.
   > How: polished rose quartz on top of an iron plate.
   > Tip: make a stack. You'll need more than you think.
16. **Brass**: 8x `create:zinc_ingot`; 8x `create:brass_ingot` *(after: Heated Mixing)*
   > A golden metal, mixed in a basin. The old skill comes back to your hands.
   > What: brass, the key metal of Create's second age.
   > Chemistry: brass is a copper-zinc alloy, usually about two-thirds copper. Zinc makes it harder and gives the gold colour.
   > How: 1 copper + 1 zinc in a heated mixer gives 2 brass. Zinc ore generates in the world.
17. **Brass Casing**: 8x `create:brass_casing` *(after: Brass)*
   > What: brass casing, for mechanical crafters, arms and brass tunnels.
   > How: right-click a stripped log or stripped wood with a brass ingot.
   > Tip: a deployer holding brass ingots can apply it automatically.
18. **Deployers**: `create:brass_hand`; 2x `create:deployer` *(after: Brass, Electron Tubes)*
   > What: a deployer acts like a player's hand: it places, uses and applies items.
   > How: an andesite casing, an electron tube and a brass hand (pressed brass sheets).
   > Tip: deployers over a belt are how Create builds things in steps: that's sequenced assembly.
19. **Precision**: `create:precision_mechanism`; 4x `create:mechanical_crafter` *(after: Deployers, Brass Casing)*
   > Each step in its place, every time. You used to build worlds this way.
   > What: the precision mechanism, made by sequenced assembly: one item passes the same stations several times.
   > How: start with a gold plate, then deploy a cogwheel, a large cogwheel and an iron nugget onto it, 5 loops. JEI shows the sequence.
   > Tip: some outputs are scrap. Sort them off the end of the belt and recycle them.
20. **Mechanical Arm**: `create:mechanical_arm` *(after: Precision)*
   > What: the mechanical arm moves items between depots, belts, basins and machines in its reach.
   > How: hold the arm and click inputs and outputs before you place it. Check the Ponder.
   > Tip: one arm can feed a whole ring of depots around it.
21. **Rotation to Electricity**: `createaddition:alternator` *(after: Precision, Analog Circuit)*
   > What: Crafts and Additions' Alternator turns rotation into FE.
   > Chemistry: a coil turning in a magnetic field pushes electrons along the wire: electromagnetic induction.
   > How: in this pack it needs an MI analog circuit (LV). Feed it fast rotation.
22. **Electricity to Rotation**: `createaddition:electric_motor` *(after: Rotation to Electricity)*
   > What: the Electric Motor turns FE back into rotation, with a speed you set.
   > How: give it FE from your MI or Mekanism grid and set the speed in its GUI.
   > Tip: motors free you from water wheels and windmills. Put a Create line anywhere.
23. **Wires and Connectors**: `createaddition:rolling_mill`; 4x `createaddition:connector`; `createaddition:copper_spool` *(after: Rotation to Electricity)*
   > What: the Rolling Mill turns ingots into rods and plates into wire. Spools of wire link connectors into power lines.
   > How: roll copper plates into wire, wind it onto a spool, then click two connectors with the spool to string a wire.
   > Tip: connectors carry power over long gaps without cables on the ground.
24. **Accumulators**: 2x `createaddition:capacitor`; `createaddition:modular_accumulator` *(after: Wires and Connectors)*
   > What: the Modular Accumulator stores FE, and several placed together merge into one bigger battery.
   > Chemistry: the capacitor here is zinc over copper, the same pair Volta used in the first battery in 1800.
   > How: needs brass casing, capacitors, copper rods and electrum wire. Electrum is gold + silver mixed hot.
   > Tip: put it between your alternators and your machines to smooth out spikes.
25. **Create: New Age**: `create_new_age:generator_coil`; `create_new_age:basic_energiser` *(after: Rotation to Electricity)*
   > What: magnet-and-coil generators, and Energisers that charge items with electricity.
   > How: the Generator Coil needs an analog circuit in this pack. Spin it among magnets; the Ponder shows the layout.
   > Tip: the Basic Energiser is just an andesite casing and a lightning rod.
26. **Charging Metal**: 4x `create_new_age:overcharged_iron` *(after: Create: New Age)*
   > What: overcharged iron, made by charging an iron ingot in an Energiser.
   > How: power the Energiser with FE and put iron underneath it (on a depot or belt). Each ingot takes 1,000 FE.
   > Tip: overcharged metals make better wire and magnets.
27. **Layered Magnets**: 4x `create_new_age:layered_magnet` *(after: Charging Metal)*
   > What: stronger magnets for New Age generators.
   > How: overcharged iron and overcharged gold in layers (check JEI).
   > Tip: a stronger field induces more current in the same coil.
28. **Thorium Reactor**: 8x `create_new_age:thorium`; `create_new_age:reactor_rod` *(after: Charging Metal, EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: New Age's nuclear reactor, fuelled by thorium.
   > Chemistry: thorium-232 isn't fissile itself; it absorbs a neutron and becomes uranium-233, which is.
   > How: the rods need an MI processing unit (EV), so it arrives alongside Mekanism fission. Check JEI and the Ponder for the build.
   > Tip: treat it like any reactor: cooling first, then power.
29. **Liquid Experience**: `create_enchantment_industry:mechanical_grindstone`; `create_enchantment_industry:experience_bucket` *(after: Precision)*
   > What: Enchantment Industry turns experience into a fluid you can store and pipe.
   > How: the Mechanical Grindstone strips enchantments off items; a Grindstone Drain collects the XP as fluid. Check JEI.
   > Tip: feed it the enchanted junk from your mob farm.
30. **Automated Enchanting**: `create_enchantment_industry:blaze_enchanter` *(after: Liquid Experience)*
   > What: the Blaze Enchanter spends liquid XP to enchant items automatically.
   > How: it's a smithing upgrade of a Blaze Burner with an enchanting table (check JEI). Give it an enchanting template and XP.
   > Tip: put it beside a belt and a deployer for a hands-free enchanting line.
31. **The Printer**: `create_enchantment_industry:printer` *(after: Automated Enchanting)*
   > What: the Printer copies written and enchanted books, paid for in liquid XP.
   > How: check JEI and the Ponder for the setup.
   > Tip: find a good enchanted book once, then print it as often as your XP lasts.
32. **Kinetic Battery**: `create_connected:kinetic_battery` *(after: Create: Connected)*
   > What: the Kinetic Battery charges from rotation and gives it back later, so a windmill's power isn't wasted when nothing is running.
   > How: check JEI and its Ponder scene (hold W over it). Put it on a shaft line to charge it.
   > Tip: a charged battery can be picked up and moved, like a wind-up spring.
33. **Bioethanol**: `createaddition:seed_oil_bucket`; `createaddition:bioethanol_bucket` *(after: Heated Mixing)*
   > What: Crafts & Additions makes liquid fuel from plants: seed oil, then biomass, then bioethanol, which burns in a Liquid Blaze Burner.
   > Chemistry: real bioethanol comes from yeast fermenting sugar: C₆H₁₂O₆ → 2 C₂H₅OH + 2 CO₂. Plant oils are triglycerides, long carbon chains that store a lot of energy.
   > How: compact seeds in a press and basin for seed oil; mix crops with seed oil over heat for biomass; mix biomass, sugar and cinder flour for bioethanol (check JEI).
   > Tip: it's a renewable fuel for Create steam engines that never touches your oil wells.
34. **Tesla Coil**: `createaddition:tesla_coil` *(after: Accumulators)*
   > What: the Tesla Coil charges FE items on belts or depots below it, and zaps anything that walks under it.
   > How: a mechanical crafting recipe with brass, a capacitor, electron tubes and a copper spool (check JEI). Power it with FE.
   > Tip: put a belt of jetpacks or gadgets under it for an automatic charging station.
35. **Heat Engines**: `create_new_age:stirling_engine`; `create_new_age:basic_solar_heating_plate` *(after: Create: New Age)*
   > What: New Age moves heat through heat pipes. Solar heating plates collect it from sunlight; a Stirling Engine turns it into slow rotation, and a Boiler Heater uses it in place of a blaze burner.
   > Chemistry: a real Stirling engine heats and cools a sealed gas. Hot gas expands and pushes a piston; cooled gas contracts and pulls it back. No fuel burns inside it.
   > How: heat pipes, iron and glass for the plate; a copper block, heat pipes and a shaft for the engine (check JEI and the Ponder scenes).

### Tools and Comfort

*Better tools from better materials, and a soft place to land.*

1. **Repair Kits**: `silentgear:crude_repair_kit` *(after: Tools from Parts)*
   > You remember tools that never wore out. These will, but you can mend them.
   > What: Silent Gear tools don't break for good; at zero durability they just stop working until repaired.
   > How: craft a Crude Repair Kit, load it with matching material, then combine it with the tool in a crafting grid.
   > Tip: a better kit (Sturdy, then Crimson and Azure) holds more material and repairs more per use.
2. **Blaze Gold**: 4x `silentgear:blaze_gold_ingot` *(after: Repair Kits, Into the Fire)*
   > What: Silent Gear's first magic alloy. It's a key ingredient of the Material Grader and the Starlight Charger.
   > Chemistry: gold (Au) is a noble metal: it barely reacts with anything, which is why it's found as native nuggets. Blaze gold is fictional; the blaze powder does the work.
   > How: gold ingot + 2 blaze powder in the Alloy Forge, or check JEI for the early crafting route.
   > Tip: keep a stack spare. Blaze gold blocks also go into the Starlight Charger.
3. **Material Grader**: `silentgear:material_grader` *(after: Blaze Gold)*
   > What: grades Silent Gear materials. A higher grade adds a stat bonus to every part made from that material.
   > How: quartz, iron, blaze gold and an Advanced Upgrade Base (check JEI). Feed it materials plus a catalyst (check JEI for which items count).
   > Tip: grade only the materials you'll use for heads and main parts; rods and bindings matter less.
4. **Salvager**: `silentgear:salvager` *(after: Material Grader)*
   > What: breaks old Silent Gear tools back into parts, so upgrading doesn't waste materials.
   > How: place it and drop in the old tool. Some material is lost on each salvage.
   > Tip: salvage before you throw anything in lava; the parts often have most of their value left.
5. **Crimson Iron**: 8x `silentgear:crimson_iron_ingot` *(after: Repair Kits, Into the Fire)*
   > What: crimson iron ore generates in Nether biomes. It's the base of crimson steel and the Alloy Forge.
   > How: mine crimson iron ore in the Nether (Jade shows the name), then smelt the raw ore.
   > Tip: run a few through the Alchemistry Dissolver and see which real elements it gives back.
6. **Crimson Steel**: 4x `silentgear:crimson_steel_ingot` *(after: Crimson Iron)*
   > What: a tough, heat-loving alloy for tool heads and the Alloy Forge frame.
   > Chemistry: real steel is iron with a little carbon (under about 2%); the carbon atoms jam the iron lattice so it can't slip. Crimson steel is the fictional cousin.
   > How: the first ingots are a shaped craft (crimson iron, blaze rods, magma cream); check JEI.
7. **The Alloy Forge**: `silentgear:alloy_forge` *(after: Crimson Steel)*
   > What: mixes metals into Silent Gear alloys: blaze gold, crimson steel (3 per batch), azure electrum and tyrian steel.
   > How: crimson steel ingots, blackstone and an iron block (check JEI). Fuel it like a furnace.
   > Tip: alloying in the forge gives more ingots per batch than the crafting-table route.
8. **Azure Electrum**: 4x `silentgear:azure_electrum_ingot` *(after: The Alloy Forge, The End)*
   > What: azure silver ore generates in the End. Alloyed with gold it becomes azure electrum, a fast, enchantable metal.
   > Chemistry: real electrum is a natural gold–silver alloy (Au–Ag); the ancient Lydians struck the first coins from it.
   > How: Alloy Forge: an azure silver block, 2 gold ingots and an ender pearl.
9. **Tyrian Steel**: 4x `silentgear:tyrian_steel_ingot` *(after: Azure Electrum)*
   > What: the top Silent Gear alloy: crimson steel, azure electrum, crushed shulker shell and netherite scrap.
   > Chemistry: Tyrian purple was a real dye made from sea snails, once worth its weight in silver. The steel just borrowed the colour.
   > How: Alloy Forge (check JEI). Crush shulker shells first.
10. **Bronze Paxel**: `mekanismtools:bronze_paxel` *(after: The Paxel, The First Alloy)*
   > What: a paxel is a pickaxe, axe and shovel in one tool. Bronze is tougher than iron.
   > Chemistry: bronze is copper with about 12% tin (Cu–Sn). Tin atoms stiffen copper's soft lattice.
   > How: craft a bronze pickaxe, axe and shovel, then combine them with a stick (check JEI).
11. **Osmium Paxel**: `mekanismtools:osmium_paxel` *(after: Bronze Paxel, Mekanism: Metallurgic Infuser)*
   > What: Mekanism's signature metal as a paxel: more durability and speed than bronze.
   > Chemistry: osmium (Os) is the densest natural element at about 22.6 g/cm³, twice as dense as lead.
   > How: osmium ore generates in the Overworld; smelt or process it, then craft the tools and combine.
12. **Diamond Paxel**: `mekanismtools:diamond_paxel` *(after: Osmium Paxel)*
   > What: the diamond paxel mines everything the diamond pickaxe can, in one slot.
   > Chemistry: diamond is pure carbon with every atom bonded to four others in a rigid 3D lattice. Graphite is the same carbon in flat sheets.
   > How: diamond pickaxe, axe and shovel plus a stick (check JEI).
13. **The Ultimate Paxel**: `mekanismtools:refined_obsidian_paxel` *(after: Diamond Paxel)*
   > You remember a hammer that could split mountains. This is a start.
   > What: refined obsidian is Mekanism's toughest material, and its paxel is the best of the line.
   > How: infuse obsidian dust with diamond in the Metallurgic Infuser, then press the refined obsidian dust in the Osmium Compressor (check JEI).
   > Tip: the same ingots make the Atomic Disassembler and much of Mekanism's late gear.
14. **Steel Paxel**: `mekanismtools:steel_paxel` *(after: Bronze Paxel, Steel)*
   > What: a sidegrade to bronze, made from the steel you already produce.
   > Chemistry: steel is iron with a little carbon from coke. Too much carbon (cast iron) makes it brittle.
   > How: steel pickaxe, axe and shovel plus a stick (check JEI).
15. **Atomic Disassembler**: `mekanism:atomic_disassembler` *(after: The Ultimate Paxel)*
   > What: an electric tool that mines, chops and digs, with vein and area modes. It runs on FE instead of durability.
   > How: refined obsidian, atomic alloy, infused alloy and an Energy Tablet (check JEI). Charge it in an energy cube.
   > Tip: sneak + right-click to switch modes.
16. **Bottled Oxygen**: `mekanism:scuba_mask`; `mekanism:scuba_tank` *(after: Mekanism: Metallurgic Infuser)*
   > What: the Scuba Mask and Tank let you breathe underwater for as long as the tank has oxygen.
   > Chemistry: the Electrolytic Separator splits water: 2H₂O → 2H₂ + O₂. The O₂ fills the tank; the H₂ is fuel.
   > How: steel, a control circuit and a chemical tank (check JEI). Fill the tank from a Chemical Tank or the separator's oxygen output.
   > Tip: great for ocean monuments and underwater prospecting.
17. **Free Runners**: `mekanism:free_runners` *(after: Bottled Oxygen)*
   > What: electric boots: auto step-up and no fall damage while charged.
   > How: infused alloy, a basic control circuit and an Energy Tablet (check JEI).
   > Tip: the armoured version adds protection; upgrade once you have more alloy.
18. **MekaSuit**: `mekanism:mekasuit_helmet`; `mekanism:mekasuit_bodyarmor` *(after: Free Runners, EV: Processing Units)*
   > You remember armour that was never armour, only certainty. This is the closest a mortal gets.
   > What: Mekanism's modular power armour. Add modules for flight, night vision, magnet, radiation shielding and more.
   > Chemistry: it needs HDPE sheets (polyethylene: long C₂H₄ chains) and polonium pellets. Real ²¹⁰Po is made by hitting bismuth-209 with neutrons; Mekanism makes it from nuclear waste in the Solar Neutron Activator.
   > How: netherite armour, HDPE sheets, ultimate control circuits, polonium pellets and induction cells (check JEI). Install modules in the Modification Station.
19. **Hazmat Gear**: `mekanism:hazmat_mask` *(after: Bottled Oxygen)*
   > What: the full hazmat suit stops Mekanism radiation from building up in you. Cheap and worth having before fission.
   > Chemistry: lead (Pb) is dense and has many electrons, so it absorbs gamma rays well.
   > How: lead ingots and orange dye for each piece (check JEI).
20. **Hydrogen Jetpack**: `mekanism:jetpack` *(after: Bottled Oxygen)*
   > What: Mekanism's jetpack burns hydrogen gas. Short hops, not true flight.
   > Chemistry: 2H₂ + O₂ → 2H₂O. Burning hydrogen gives only water and a lot of energy.
   > How: steel, tin, a basic control circuit and a chemical tank. Fill it with H₂ from the Electrolytic Separator.
21. **Jetpacks**: `ironjetpacks:basic_coil`; `ironjetpacks:jetpack` *(after: Analog Circuit)*
   > What: Iron Jetpacks run on FE. Every tier needs a coil, and in this pack the coils need MI circuits.
   > How: Basic coil = analog circuit (LV). Craft the coil, then cells, thrusters, strap and the jetpack for your metal (check JEI).
   > Tip: better metals fly faster and hold more FE. Charge it in any energy cube.
22. **Advanced Coil**: `ironjetpacks:advanced_coil` *(after: Jetpacks, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: unlocks the middle jetpack tiers.
   > How: needs an MI electronic circuit (MV, aluminum chemistry). Check JEI for which jetpacks use it.
   > Tip: JEI shows which jetpacks use this coil.
23. **Elite Coil**: `ironjetpacks:elite_coil` *(after: Advanced Coil, HV: Digital Circuits)*
   > What: faster, longer flights.
   > How: needs an MI digital circuit (HV, polyethylene).
   > Tip: the digital circuit is your polyethylene line paying off; make a few extra.
24. **Ultimate Coil**: `ironjetpacks:ultimate_coil` *(after: Elite Coil, EV: Processing Units)*
   > What: the top jetpack tier.
   > How: needs an MI processing unit (EV, PVC chemistry).
   > Tip: by now the Angel Ring may suit you better; the jetpack still has the best speed.
25. **The Angel Ring**: `angelring:diamond_ring`; `angelring:angel_ring` *(after: Jetpacks, HV: Digital Circuits, The End)*
   > You remember the sky being yours. Now it is again.
   > What: portable creative flight, no fuel, from a Curios ring slot.
   > Chemistry: its MI digital circuit needs polyethylene: n C₂H₄ → (C₂H₄)ₙ, ethylene from your distillation tower.
   > How: Elytra, ghast tears, diamond blocks and Nether Stars plus the digital circuit. Craft the Diamond Ring first, then the Angel Ring.
26. **Hammock**: `comforts:hammock_white` *(after: Rest Anywhere)*
   > What: skips the day the way a sleeping bag skips the night, without moving your spawn.
   > How: white wool, string and sticks. Hang it between two Rope and Nails placed on facing walls (check JEI).
   > Tip: useful when you want night-only mobs, like the ones a Deep Learner needs.
27. **Warp Stone**: `waystones:warp_stone` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Waystones (found in villages, or crafted) let you teleport between places you've visited. A Warp Stone works from anywhere.
   > How: amethyst, ender pearls and emeralds (check JEI). Activate a waystone first by right-clicking it.
   > Tip: it has a cooldown, so don't count on it in an emergency.
28. **Tool Belt**: `toolbelt:belt` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: a belt worn in a Curios slot that holds tools and swaps them into your hand with a key.
   > How: leather, string and an iron ingot. Add pouches for more slots.
   > Tip: set the belt key in Controls. Great for paxel, sword, hoe and wrench.
29. **Mining Gadget**: `mininggadgets:mininggadget_simple` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: a mining laser that breaks blocks at range. Upgrades add 3x3 (and bigger) size, efficiency, magnet and void filters.
   > How: iron, gold, diamond and redstone (check JEI). Charge it with FE.
   > Tip: a void filter for stone and dirt keeps your inventory clean on long ore runs.
30. **Bindings and Grips**: `silentgear:binding`; `silentgear:grip` *(after: Tools from Parts)*
   > What: bindings and grips are optional Silent Gear parts. Each adds its material's traits and stats to the tool.
   > How: make the Binding and Grip blueprints, then craft the parts from string, vines, leather or wool (check JEI). Add them to a tool in a crafting grid.
   > Tip: the Material Book (or JEI) shows every material's traits. Hover a part to see what it adds.
31. **Coatings**: `silentgear:coating_smithing_template` *(after: Bindings and Grips, Material Grader)*
   > What: a coating covers a finished tool's main part in another material, like diamond or netherite, changing its stats and colour.
   > Chemistry: real tools are coated too: drill bits get a thin layer of titanium nitride (TiN) or diamond to stay sharp much longer.
   > How: craft the coating smithing template (check JEI), then apply the coating material at a smithing table.
32. **Silent Jewellery**: 1x any of `or(item(silentgear:ring)item(silentgear:bracelet)item(silentgear:necklace))` *(after: Bindings and Grips)*
   > What: Silent Gear jewellery carries material traits as curios: a ring of a lucky or magnetic material gives you that trait while worn.
   > How: a ring shank, bracelet band or necklace chain plus a gem setting, made with the matching blueprint (check JEI).
   > Tip: try different metals and gems. The traits list on each material shows what jewellery will do.
33. **Armour from Parts**: 4x any of `or(item(silentgear:helmet)item(silentgear:chestplate)item(silentgear:leggings)item(silentgear:boots))` *(after: Bindings and Grips, Crimson Steel)*
   > What: Silent Gear armour is built from plates of any material, so your best alloy becomes your best armour.
   > How: helmet, chestplate, legging and boot plates from their blueprints, then assemble each piece (check JEI). Any four pieces.
   > Tip: crimson steel and azure electrum plates have strong traits; check the Material Book before choosing.
34. **Primordial Goo**: `justdirethings:gooblock_tier1` *(after: Iron)*
   > You remember a slime that turned stone into anything you asked. This one is less clever, but it tries.
   > What: Just Dire Things goo converts the blocks it touches. Tier 1 goo turns an iron block into raw ferricore ore.
   > How: clay, sugar, rotten flesh and dirt (check JEI). Place an iron block against the goo and wait (JEI shows how long).
   > Tip: JEI's goo tab shows every conversion and which goo tier it needs.
35. **Ferricore**: 8x `justdirethings:ferricore_ingot`; `justdirethings:ferricore_wrench` *(after: Primordial Goo)*
   > What: ferricore is the first Just Dire Things metal: tools, the Pocket Generator, machines and upgrade blanks.
   > How: mine the raw ferricore ore the goo makes and smelt it (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Ferricore Wrench rotates and picks up JDT machines.
36. **Blazegold**: `justdirethings:gooblock_tier2`; 4x `justdirethings:blazegold_ingot` *(after: Ferricore, Into the Fire)*
   > What: tier 2 goo turns gold blocks into raw blazegold ore. Blazegold makes the next tier of JDT tools and machines.
   > How: upgrade tier 1 goo with blaze powder, redstone and Nether wart (check JEI), then feed it gold blocks.
   > Tip: goo only converts blocks touching it, so surround it with several gold blocks at once.
37. **Celestigem**: `justdirethings:gooblock_tier3`; 4x `justdirethings:celestigem` *(after: Blazegold, The Dragon's Breath)*
   > What: tier 3 goo turns diamond blocks into celestigem ore. Celestigem tools take powerful upgrades.
   > How: upgrade tier 2 goo with ender pearls, end stone and dragon's breath (check JEI).
   > Tip: JDT's flight upgrade needs End Crystals, so portable flight here comes after the End, as the pack intends.
38. **Eclipse Alloy**: `justdirethings:gooblock_tier4`; 2x `justdirethings:eclipsealloy_ingot` *(after: Celestigem, An Ancient City)*
   > What: tier 4 goo turns netherite blocks into eclipse alloy ore, JDT's top material.
   > How: upgrade tier 3 goo with sculk, a sculk shrieker and echo shards from an Ancient City (check JEI). Then sacrifice a netherite block.
   > Tip: it's expensive. Decide which eclipse alloy tool you want before you start.
39. **Pocket Generator**: `justdirethings:pocket_generator` *(after: Ferricore)*
   > What: the Pocket Generator burns fuel in your inventory and charges your FE items.
   > How: ferricore, a furnace, coal and a redstone block (check JEI).
   > Chemistry: burning coal is oxidation: C + O₂ → CO₂ releases about 394 kJ per mole of carbon as heat.
40. **Thinking with Portals**: `justdirethings:portalgun` *(after: Celestigem)*
   > You remember stepping between places without walking. You'd forgotten how much fun it was.
   > What: the Portal Gun shoots two linked portals onto walls. Walk in one, come out the other.
   > Tip: there is an upgraded Portal Gun too; JEI has its recipe.

### Storage and Logistics

*A god needs somewhere to keep things.*

1. **Sophisticated Barrel**: `sophisticatedstorage:barrel` *(after: A Backpack)*
   > You remember vaults that held the stars. A barrel is humbler, but it's a start.
   > What: Sophisticated Storage chests and barrels take upgrades, just like your backpack. Sophisticated Storage Plus raises their limits.
   > How: craft a wooden barrel (check JEI). Tier upgrades improve it in place later.
   > Tip: barrels keep their contents when broken, so moving a full one is easy.
2. **Tier Upgrades**: `sophisticatedstorage:basic_to_iron_tier_upgrade` *(after: Sophisticated Barrel)*
   > What: tier upgrades turn a placed chest or barrel into the next tier (copper, iron, gold, diamond, netherite) without moving items.
   > How: craft the upgrade (check JEI) and right-click it on the placed container.
   > Tip: you can skip tiers with the direct upgrades, like basic → iron.
3. **Compacting**: `sophisticatedstorage:compacting_upgrade` *(after: Tier Upgrades)*
   > What: the Compacting Upgrade squeezes 9 nuggets into an ingot and 9 ingots into a block as items arrive.
   > Chemistry: a block holds the same metal atoms as 9 ingots; only the packing changes, so no mass is lost either way.
   > How: craft it (check JEI) and put it in an upgrade slot. Add a filter so it only compacts what you choose.
4. **Storage Controller**: `sophisticatedstorage:controller`; 2x `sophisticatedstorage:storage_link` *(after: Compacting)*
   > What: the Controller joins touching Sophisticated containers into one inventory; Storage Links extend its reach.
   > How: place it next to your chests and barrels. Pipes into the controller reach every connected container.
   > Tip: one pipe into the controller is a simple sorting system: items go where matching items already are.
5. **Drawers**: `functionalstorage:oak_1` *(after: Sophisticated Barrel)*
   > What: Functional Storage drawers hold lots of a single item and show it on the front.
   > How: craft an oak drawer (1, 2 or 4 slots; check JEI). Right-click to insert, left-click to take.
   > Tip: upgrades (copper, gold, diamond, netherite) multiply drawer capacity.
6. **Compacting Drawer**: `functionalstorage:compacting_drawer` *(after: Drawers)*
   > What: a compacting drawer stores nuggets, ingots and blocks of one metal as a single pool, and hands out whichever you ask for.
   > How: craft it (check JEI), then insert the item you want to track.
   > Tip: perfect for copper and tin near your bronze setup.
7. **Drawer Controller**: `functionalstorage:storage_controller` *(after: Compacting Drawer)*
   > What: the Storage Controller links nearby drawers into one inventory you (and pipes) can use.
   > How: place it among your drawers. Use the Linking Tool to add drawers further away.
   > Tip: a Controller Extension gives you a second access point.
8. **An Early Network**: `toms_storage:storage_terminal`; `toms_storage:inventory_connector` *(after: Drawers)*
   > What: Tom's Simple Storage connects your chests into one searchable terminal. It's your network until AE2.
   > How: place an Inventory Connector touching your chests (or link them with Inventory Cables), and a Storage Terminal on it.
   > Tip: point it at a drawer controller and every drawer shows up in the terminal.
9. **Crafting Terminal**: `toms_storage:crafting_terminal` *(after: An Early Network)*
   > What: a terminal with a crafting grid that pulls ingredients from the network. JEI's + button fills it for you.
   > How: upgrade a Storage Terminal (check JEI).
   > Tip: a Wireless Terminal (also in JEI) lets you use it from across the base.
10. **Faster Furnaces**: `ironfurnaces:iron_furnace` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Iron Furnaces are faster furnaces in metal tiers. A generator augment turns one into a small FE source.
   > Chemistry: smelting iron ore is a reduction: carbon pulls the oxygen off, Fe₂O₃ + 3CO → 2Fe + 3CO₂.
   > How: ring a furnace with iron (check JEI). Upgrade kits move it up a tier without breaking it.
11. **Pipez**: 8x `pipez:item_pipe` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: simple pipes for moving items, fluids and energy between non-MI machines.
   > How: place a line from source to target, then set the end touching the source to extract (the pipe tooltip explains how).
   > Tip: MI machines have their own pipes; Pipez is best for everything else.
12. **LaserIO**: 2x `laserio:laser_node` *(after: Pipez, LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: Laser Nodes move items, fluids and energy between machines without cables.
   > How: place nodes on machines, link them with the Laser Wrench, and add cards to set what moves (check JEI).
   > Tip: one node can serve all six faces of a block, great for compact chemical setups.
13. **Entangled**: checkmark *(after: Iron, Sophisticated Barrel)*
   > What: an Entangled Block is a window into another inventory: bind it to a chest or machine, and anything put in the Entangled Block goes into the bound block, even across dimensions.
   > How: ender pearls, obsidian and a wooden chest (check JEI). Right-click the target with the Entangled Binder (pearls, obsidian and a diamond), then right-click the Entangled Block. Click the checkmark when one is working.
   > Tip: put one next to a remote ore processor and pipe the output straight home. Both chunks must be loaded.
14. **Storage in Motion**: `sophisticatedstorageinmotion:storage_minecart` *(after: Tier Upgrades)*
   > What: Sophisticated Storage in Motion puts upgradeable chests and barrels on minecarts, boats and rafts, upgrades and all.
   > How: combine a minecart with a Sophisticated chest or barrel (check JEI). Boats and rafts work the same way.
   > Tip: a hopper upgrade on a storage minecart makes a simple ore train between your mine and base.
15. **Stack Upgrades**: `sophisticatedstorage:stack_upgrade_tier_1` *(after: Tier Upgrades)*
   > What: stack upgrades multiply how many items fit in each slot of a chest or barrel. Higher tiers multiply more.
   > How: an upgrade base and storage blocks (check JEI). Insert it in an upgrade slot.
   > Tip: a barrel with a few stack upgrades holds a whole mine's worth of cobblestone or raw ore.
16. **Drawer Upgrades**: `functionalstorage:diamond_upgrade` *(after: Drawer Controller)*
   > What: storage upgrades multiply a drawer's capacity. Each tier (copper, gold, diamond, netherite) holds more.
   > How: craft the upgrades in order (check JEI) and right-click the drawer to open its upgrade slots.
   > Tip: the Iron Downgrade does the reverse, limiting a drawer to a single stack per slot.
17. **Pullers and Pushers**: `functionalstorage:puller_upgrade`; `functionalstorage:pusher_upgrade` *(after: Drawer Upgrades)*
   > What: utility upgrades: the Puller pulls items from the block it faces, the Pusher pushes items into it. No pipes needed.
   > How: check JEI. Put one in a drawer's utility slot and set its direction in the drawer's menu.
   > Tip: a drawer with a Puller behind a macerator collects all the dust without a single pipe.
18. **Fluid Drawers**: `functionalstorage:fluid_1` *(after: Drawer Upgrades)*
   > What: fluid drawers store a fluid instead of items, and show it on the front.
   > Chemistry: handy for small stocks of acids: one drawer of H₂SO₄, one of HCl, one of HNO₃. A real lab keeps acids in separate, labelled containers too.
   > How: check JEI. Pipes and MI fluid pipes can fill and drain them; the storage upgrades work on them too.
