19. **Ender Drawer**: `functionalstorage:ender_drawer` *(after: Drawer Upgrades)*
   > What: every Ender Drawer with the same frequency shares one inventory, wherever it is.
   > How: check JEI. Link drawers with the Linking Tool to give them the same frequency.
   > Tip: put one at your mine and one at your smelters to send ore home.
20. **Inventory Cables**: 16x `toms_storage:inventory_cable`; `toms_storage:inventory_cable_connector` *(after: An Early Network)*
   > What: inventory cables carry the Inventory Connector's reach to chests that aren't touching it.
   > How: run cables from the connector and put a Cable Connector on each chest or drawer (check JEI).
   > Tip: framed cables can wear any block's texture, so they hide in walls.
21. **Wireless Terminal**: `toms_storage:adv_wireless_terminal` *(after: Crafting Terminal)*
   > What: the Advanced Wireless Terminal opens your Tom's network from a long way off.
   > How: check JEI, then right-click it on a Storage Terminal to bind it.
   > Tip: it's the cheapest remote storage access until AE2.

### Applied Energistics

*The archive of a god: every item as data, every recipe on demand.*

1. **Feeding the Network**: `ae2:energy_acceptor`; `ae2:energy_cell` *(after: AE2 Storage)*
   > What: the ME network turns FE into its own AE energy. The Energy Acceptor converts it; Energy Cells buffer it.
   > How: connect an Energy Acceptor to any FE source (an energy cube, a Powah cell, a Flux Point) and to your cables. Add an Energy Cell next to the controller (check JEI).
   > Tip: a big autocraft can drain the network fast. A Dense Energy Cell keeps it from browning out mid-job.
2. **Growing Certus**: 2x `ae2:growth_accelerator` *(after: AE2 Storage)*
   > What: Growth Accelerators speed up certus quartz buds growing on budding certus blocks.
   > Chemistry: real quartz (SiO₂) crystals grow from silica-rich water over thousands of years. Certus is fictional, but the idea of a seed crystal pulling dissolved material out of solution is real.
   > How: iron, fluix glass cable, quartz glass and a fluix block (check JEI). Place them touching budding certus and power them.
   > Tip: flawless budding certus never decays. Find it in meteorites and never break it with a normal pickaxe.
3. **P2P Tunnels**: 2x `ae2:me_p2p_tunnel` *(after: Cables and Channels)*
   > What: a pair of P2P tunnels carries 32 channels (or items, fluids, FE and redstone) through a single dense cable.
   > How: place both tunnels, then copy the input's settings to the output with a Memory Card (check JEI).
   > Tip: right-click a P2P tunnel with a bucket, a redstone dust or an FE item to change what it carries.
4. **The Matter Condenser**: `ae2:condenser`; `ae2:singularity` *(after: Feeding the Network)*
   > What: the Matter Condenser destroys items. With a 64k component inside, it squeezes them into Matter Balls or a Singularity.
   > How: craft it (check JEI), put a storage component in it, set it to Singularity and feed it cobblestone from a pipe. It takes a lot of items.
   > Tip: singularities unlock MEGA Cells' accumulation processor and every AdvancedAE machine, so start a cobblestone generator early.
5. **Quantum Bridge**: 8x `ae2:quantum_ring`; `ae2:quantum_link` *(after: The Matter Condenser, P2P Tunnels)*
   > What: two Quantum Network Bridges joined by entangled singularities act like one cable, even across dimensions.
   > How: build a 3x3 ring of 8 Quantum Rings around a Quantum Link Chamber at each end, then put one of a pair of entangled singularities in each chamber.
   > Tip: both ends must be chunk-loaded. Use FTB Chunks to force-load them.
6. **Spooky Action**: 2x `ae2:quantum_entangled_singularity` *(after: The Matter Condenser)*
   > You remember being in two places at once. It felt exactly like this.
   > What: you made a pair of Quantum Entangled Singularities by blowing up a singularity with ender pearl dust.
   > Chemistry: real entangled particles share one quantum state: measure one and you know the other, however far apart. Sadly it can't send messages faster than light; AE2 is kinder.
7. **AE2 Storage**: `ae2:drive`; `ae2:terminal` *(after: The God's Archive)*
   > You remember a mind that held every name of every thing. The archive is waking.
   > What: ME Drives hold storage cells; terminals let you see and search everything.
   > How: connect the drive and a terminal to your ME Controller with cables (check JEI).
   > Tip: keep power stable: an Energy Cell buffers the network.
8. **Cables and Channels**: 16x `ae2:fluix_smart_cable`; 4x `ae2:fluix_covered_dense_cable` *(after: AE2 Storage)*
   > What: each ME device uses one channel. Normal cables carry 8 channels, dense cables 32. The controller sends 32 out of each face.
   > How: craft fluix cables, then smart cables (they show channel use as lines) and dense cables (check JEI).
   > Tip: build a dense-cable backbone from the controller and branch normal cables off it. Smart cables show you where channels run out.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Deep dive: if a device's light is off, it has no channel. Count devices on each normal cable (max 8). Drives, terminals, buses and interfaces all count. Plain cables, quartz fibre and energy cells don't.
   > How: the Network Tool (check JEI) shows the channel count of any cable when you right-click it.
9. **Storage Cells**: 2x `ae2:cell_component_1k`; `ae2:item_storage_cell_1k` *(after: AE2 Storage)*
   > What: cells are where the network actually stores things. Bigger components (4k, 16k, 64k, 256k) hold more.
   > Chemistry: certus and fluix are fictional, but the logic processors behind them echo real chips: doped silicon (Si).
   > How: craft components from processors and certus quartz, then put one in a cell housing (check JEI).
10. **Bigger Components**: `ae2:cell_component_16k`; `ae2:cell_component_64k` *(after: Storage Cells)*
   > What: each tier combines three of the last with a calculation processor and quartz glass, holding four times as many bytes.
   > How: 4k from 1k, 16k from 4k, 64k from 16k (check JEI). Put them in housings, or into the Matter Condenser.
   > Tip: a cell holds up to 63 different item types whatever its size, so use small cells for many odd items and big cells for bulk ores and dusts.
11. **Autocrafting**: `ae2:crafting_terminal` *(after: Storage Cells)*
   > What: pattern providers plus molecular assemblers turn whole chemical chains into one click.
   > How: a Crafting Terminal, some crafting storage, and patterns encoded in a Pattern Encoding Terminal (check JEI).
   > Tip: processing patterns can drive MI machines too: send the inputs, wait for the outputs. The Automation and Logic page walks through a full sulfuric acid line.
12. **Wireless Access**: `ae2:wireless_crafting_terminal` *(after: AE2 Storage)*
   > What: reach your ME network from anywhere in range of a Wireless Access Point.
   > How: check JEI. Link the terminal by putting it in the access point, and charge it in the Charger.
   > Tip: put the access point near the middle of your base for the best coverage.
13. **Universal Terminal**: `ae2wtlib:wireless_universal_terminal` *(after: Wireless Access)*
   > What: AE2 Wireless Terminals merges the crafting, pattern access and pattern encoding terminals into one Universal Terminal.
   > How: combine two wireless terminals in a crafting grid (check JEI); add more by crafting them in again.
   > Tip: a Magnet Card from the same mod pulls nearby drops straight into your network. A Quantum Bridge Card works across dimensions.
14. **Infinite Range**: `aeinfinitybooster:infinity_card` *(after: Wireless Access, Into the Fire)*
   > What: the Infinity Booster Card removes the Wireless Access Point's range limit within one dimension.
   > How: a Nether Star, wireless boosters, eyes of ender and netherite ingots (check JEI). The star means fighting the Wither first.
   > Tip: put it in the Wireless Access Point, not the terminal.
15. **Across Worlds**: `aeinfinitybooster:dimension_card` *(after: Infinite Range)*
   > What: the Dimension Card lets a Wireless Access Point reach you in any dimension: the Nether, the Twilight Forest, the Moon.
   > How: four Infinity Cards, four Nether Stars and an eye of ender (check JEI).
   > Tip: the access point's chunk must stay loaded while you're away.
16. **ME Requester**: `merequester:requester` *(after: Autocrafting)*
   > What: the ME Requester keeps a set amount of an item in stock by ordering crafts automatically.
   > How: check JEI. Set "keep 64 HDPE" and it never runs out.
   > Tip: it needs autocrafting patterns for whatever it keeps in stock.
17. **Requester Terminal**: `merequester:requester_terminal` *(after: ME Requester)*
   > What: the Requester Terminal lists every ME Requester on the network, so you can edit all your stock rules from one place.
   > How: an illuminated panel, a logic processor and a Requester, shapeless (check JEI).
   > Tip: keep a stock rule for each acid and solvent: H₂SO₄, HCl and HNO₃ are inputs to almost everything late game.
18. **Entro Crystals**: `extendedae:entro_seed`; 8x `extendedae:entro_crystal` *(after: Autocrafting)*
   > What: entro crystals feed every ExtendedAE machine. They grow like certus, from Budding Entroized Fluix.
   > How: craft Entro Seeds (sand, ender dust, redstone, glowstone, sky dust), then right-click a placed Fluix Block with a seed to make it budding. Harvest the clusters.
   > Tip: Growth Accelerators work on budding entro too.
19. **Crystal Assembler**: `extendedae:machine_frame`; `extendedae:crystal_assembler` *(after: Entro Crystals)*
   > What: the Crystal Assembler makes ExtendedAE's blocks from several items (and sometimes a fluid) at once.
   > How: crush entro to dust in the Inscriber, drop it in water with a gold ingot and lapis for an entro ingot, then build a Machine Frame and the Assembler (check JEI).
   > Tip: it accepts pipes and patterns, so the network can drive it like any other machine.
20. **ExtendedAE**: `extendedae:ex_pattern_provider`; `extendedae:ex_drive` *(after: Autocrafting, Crystal Assembler)*
   > What: ExtendedAE adds larger versions of AE2 blocks: the Extended Pattern Provider holds many more patterns, the Extended Drive many more cells.
   > How: made in the Crystal Assembler from the normal blocks plus concurrent processors (check JEI).
   > Tip: one extended provider can feed a whole row of chemical reactors.
21. **Wireless Connectors**: 2x `extendedae:wireless_connect` *(after: ExtendedAE)*
   > What: a pair of Wireless Connectors links two parts of your network without a cable between them.
   > How: made two at a time in the Crystal Assembler. Bind them with the Wireless Setup Kit (check JEI).
   > Tip: great for reaching a reactor room behind thick shielding.
22. **Assembler Matrix**: 8x `extendedae:assembler_matrix_frame`; `extendedae:assembler_matrix_crafter`; `extendedae:assembler_matrix_pattern` *(after: ExtendedAE)*
   > What: the Assembler Matrix is a multiblock that replaces walls of molecular assemblers: crafter blocks do the work, pattern blocks hold the patterns.
   > How: build a hollow box of frames and walls (or glass), with crafter, pattern and speed cores inside (check JEI for the rules).
   > Tip: it's for crafting-table recipes only. Processing patterns for MI machines still need pattern providers.
23. **MEGA Cells**: 4x `megacells:sky_steel_ingot`; `megacells:item_storage_cell_1m` *(after: Storage Cells)*
   > What: MEGA Cells adds 1M to 256M cells, bulk cells and bigger crafting storage.
   > Chemistry: sky steel is made by dropping charged certus, iron and sky stone into lava: heat drives the alloying, like a tiny smelter.
   > How: throw the ingredients into a lava pool for sky steel, then build the 1M component (check JEI).
24. **Sky Bronze and Sky Osmium**: 4x `megacells:sky_bronze_ingot`; 4x `megacells:sky_osmium_ingot` *(after: MEGA Cells)*
   > What: the same lava trick with copper gives sky bronze, and with osmium gives sky osmium. Both go into MEGA's larger parts.
   > Chemistry: bronze is really copper plus tin (Cu + Sn); here the sky stone plays the tin. Osmium (Os) is the densest natural element, 22.6 g/cm³.
   > How: drop charged certus, the ingot and a sky stone block into lava together. Each throw makes two ingots.
25. **Accumulation Processors**: 4x `megacells:accumulation_processor` *(after: MEGA Cells, The Matter Condenser)*
   > What: the accumulation processor is MEGA's fourth processor, needed for every 1M+ part.
   > How: inscribe a singularity between the calculation and engineering presses to make the Accumulation Press, then print sky steel with it (check JEI).
   > Tip: keep the press. The Inscriber can copy it with an iron block, like the other presses.
26. **MEGA Crafting CPU**: `megacells:1m_crafting_storage`; 4x `megacells:mega_crafting_unit` *(after: Accumulation Processors)*
   > What: MEGA crafting storage and units build a separate, much larger crafting CPU.
   > How: check JEI. MEGA blocks only join other MEGA blocks, so build it as its own multiblock.
   > Tip: the Seed of a New World chain is huge. One big CPU saves splitting the job by hand.
27. **MEGA Pattern Provider**: `megacells:mega_pattern_provider` *(after: MEGA Crafting CPU)*
   > What: a pattern provider with many more pattern slots than the normal one.
   > How: made as a cable part and turned into a block in the crafting grid (check JEI).
   > Tip: put one on each multi-input MI machine, like the Chemical Reactor or the Large Diesel Generator's fuel line.
28. **Bulk Cells**: `megacells:bulk_item_cell`; `megacells:compression_card` *(after: MEGA Crafting CPU)*
   > What: a Bulk Item Cell holds a single item type in near-unlimited amounts. The Compression Card stores nuggets, ingots and blocks as one pool.
   > How: the bulk component needs a 1M component and accumulation processors; the card is an advanced card plus a matter ball (check JEI).
   > Tip: perfect for cobblestone (condenser feed), salt dust or coal coke.
29. **Gas in the Network**: `appmek:chemical_storage_cell_1k` *(after: Storage Cells, MV: Aluminum)*
   > What: Applied Mekanistics lets AE2 store and move Mekanism chemicals: H₂, O₂, Cl₂, HCl, SO₂ and the rest.
   > Chemistry: a 1k cell holds a lot of gas because the network stores amounts, not tanks. Real gases need pressure vessels.
   > How: a chemical cell housing (osmium, quartz glass, redstone) + a 1k component. Import buses on chemical tanks fill it.
   > Tip: processing patterns can pull stored SO₂ and O₂ straight from the cell to feed your sulfuric acid line.
30. **A Tank Farm in a Cell**: `appmek:chemical_storage_cell_64k` *(after: Gas in the Network, Bigger Components)*
   > What: a 64k chemical cell replaces a room full of chemical tanks.
   > Chemistry: one mole of any ideal gas fills about 22.4 L at 0 °C and 1 atm. Stored as data, it fills nothing at all.
   > How: chemical housing plus a 64k component, or upgrade a smaller cell in the crafting grid (check JEI).
31. **Shielded Storage**: `megacells:radioactive_chemical_cell` *(after: A Tank Farm in a Cell, MEGA Cells, Fission)*
   > What: normal chemical cells refuse radioactive chemicals. MEGA's Radioactive Chemical Cell holds one, like nuclear waste or plutonium.
   > Chemistry: spent fuel stays dangerous because unstable nuclei keep decaying, giving off α, β and γ radiation for years.
   > How: check JEI for the radioactive component and cell.
   > Tip: a cell never leaks, unlike a broken tank. It's the safest place to keep waste before you reprocess it.
32. **Energy in a Cell**: 4x `appflux:charged_redstone`; `appflux:fe_1k_cell` *(after: Storage Cells)*
   > What: Applied Flux stores FE in AE2 cells and moves it through the network with Flux Accessors.
   > How: redstone crystal (redstone block, fluix and glowstone dropped in water) → charge it in the AE2 Charger → FE cell core and housing (check JEI).
   > Tip: the housing needs insulating resin, which needs silicon: a little chemistry even here.
33. **Flux Accessor**: `appflux:flux_accessor`; `appflux:induction_card` *(after: Energy in a Cell)*
   > What: a Flux Accessor pushes FE from your FE cells into any machine it touches. An Induction Card lets an I/O bus or interface do the same.
   > How: an energy acceptor, energy processors (iron block charged into the Energy Press, then inscribed), copper and glowstone (check JEI).
   > Tip: one FE cell plus accessors can replace a tangle of power cables around a dense machine room.
34. **Reaction Chamber**: `advanced_ae:reaction_chamber`; 2x `advanced_ae:shattered_singularity` *(after: The Matter Condenser)*
   > What: AdvancedAE's Reaction Chamber combines items and a fluid with lots of energy. Its first job: shattering singularities.
   > How: a matter condenser, a vibration chamber, fluix dust, glowstone and a bucket (check JEI). Feed it a singularity, ender dust, sky dust and lava.
   > Tip: it uses a lot of power per craft. Put it on its own Energy Acceptor.
35. **Quantum Alloy**: 4x `advanced_ae:quantum_alloy` *(after: Reaction Chamber)*
   > What: quantum alloy is AdvancedAE's top material, for the quantum computer and gear.
   > How: press a shattered singularity into quantum-infused dust, react it with water into Quantum Infusion, then react that with copper ingots, singularities and shattered singularities (check JEI).
   > Chemistry: copper (Cu) is the base for a reason: after silver it's the best electrical conductor of any metal.
36. **Directional Providers**: `advanced_ae:adv_pattern_provider` *(after: ExtendedAE)*
   > What: the Advanced Pattern Provider lets a processing pattern send each input to a chosen side of the machine.
   > How: an Extended Pattern Provider, a logic processor, an ender pearl and redstone (check JEI). Encode patterns in the Advanced Pattern Encoder.
   > Tip: ideal for machines with separate input faces, like a Mekanism machine that takes gas on one side and items on another.
37. **Quantum Computer**: `advanced_ae:quantum_core`; 8x `advanced_ae:quantum_structure`; `advanced_ae:quantum_multi_threader` *(after: Quantum Alloy, MEGA Crafting CPU)*
   > You remember thinking a thousand thoughts at once. The machine remembers how, too.
   > What: the Quantum Computer is a multiblock crafting CPU: huge storage, many parallel jobs, and accelerators that multiply speed.
   > How: a Quantum Core inside a shell of Quantum Structure blocks, with multi-threaders, accelerators and storage blocks (check JEI for size limits).
   > Tip: build it next to your main pattern providers so every chemical line can run at once.
38. **Import and Export Cards**: `ae2importexportcard:import_card`; `ae2importexportcard:export_card` *(after: Wireless Access)*
   > What: these cards go in a wireless terminal. The Import Card pushes chosen items from your inventory into the network; the Export Card keeps chosen items stocked in your inventory.
   > How: an import (or export) bus, redstone blocks, an advanced card and processors (check JEI).
   > Tip: set the Import Card to cobblestone, dirt and raw ores, and mining trips never fill your inventory.
39. **Packaged Extended Crafting**: `packagedexcrafting:basic_crafter` *(after: Packaged Automation, Crystaltine)*
   > What: Packaged ExCrafting adds crafters that run Extended Crafting recipes from packages: one for each table size, plus combination and ender crafting.
   > How: an auto table of the right size, an ME package component, crystaltine components, black iron and a redstone catalyst (check JEI).
   > Tip: encode the recipe in the Packaged Auto encoder, just like normal packages.
40. **The Ultimate Crafter**: `packagedexcrafting:ultimate_crafter` *(after: Packaged Extended Crafting, 9x9: Ultimate Table)*
   > What: the Ultimate Crafter runs 9x9 Ultimate Table recipes automatically from the network.
   > How: same recipe as the basic crafter, with an Ultimate Auto Table in the middle (check JEI).
   > Tip: the Seed of a New World is a 9x9 recipe. Feed it from the network and the capstone crafts itself once its ingredients exist.

### Building and Decoration

*A god's house should look like one.*

1. **Effortless Building**: `effortlessbuilding:randomizer_tool` *(after: Wood and a Workbench)*
   > You remember raising cities with a gesture. For now, a few clever tools will do.
   > What: Effortless Building adds mirror, array and line modes for big builds.
   > How: open its menu (check Controls) and pick a mode. The randomizer places a random mix of the blocks you load into it.
   > Tip: the randomizer with stone, andesite and cobblestone makes natural-looking walls.
2. **Construction Stick**: `constructionstick:wooden_stick` *(after: Wood and a Workbench)*
   > What: a Construction Stick places a whole row or face of blocks matching the one you click, using blocks from your inventory.
   > How: three sticks in a column (check JEI). Right-click the face of a wall to grow it by one layer.
   > Tip: the tooltip lists its modes, such as only extending in a straight line.
3. **Diamond Stick**: `constructionstick:diamond_stick` *(after: Construction Stick)*
   > What: better sticks place more blocks per click and last longer: copper, iron, diamond, then netherite.
   > Chemistry: diamond is pure carbon (C) in a tight 3D lattice, which makes it the hardest natural material.
   > How: a diamond on two sticks (check JEI). Templates applied at a smithing table add tricks like placing in mid-air.
4. **Rechiseled**: checkmark *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Rechiseled adds a chisel that turns stone, planks, metal blocks and more into decorative patterns: bricks, tiles, pillars, gears.
   > How: craft the chisel from an iron ingot on a stick, right-click to open it, put blocks in and pick a pattern. Click the checkmark when you've chiseled something.
   > Tip: many patterns connect to each other when placed side by side. Try the iron and copper block patterns for a machine room.
5. **Building Gadgets**: `buildinggadgets2:gadget_building` *(after: LV Machine Hull)*
   > What: FE-powered gadgets that place, swap, copy and remove blocks in bulk.
   > How: iron, redstone, lapis and a diamond (check JEI). Charge it in an energy cube.
   > Tip: link the gadget to a chest or backpack so it pulls blocks from storage.
6. **Copy and Paste**: `buildinggadgets2:gadget_copy_paste`; `buildinggadgets2:template_manager` *(after: Building Gadgets)*
   > What: copy an area and paste it elsewhere; the Template Manager saves copies to share.
   > How: check JEI. Great for repeating a chemical reactor cell or a row of machines.
   > Tip: templates can be shared with friends on the server.
7. **Exchanging Gadget**: `buildinggadgets2:gadget_exchanging` *(after: Building Gadgets)*
   > What: replaces blocks in an area with another block, keeping the shape.
   > How: check JEI. Perfect for turning a cobble shell into something pretty.
   > Tip: it uses the blocks in your inventory, so carry enough.
8. **Destruction Gadget**: `buildinggadgets2:gadget_destruction` *(after: Building Gadgets)*
   > What: removes a box of blocks in front of you, sized however you like. It can undo its last action.
   > How: check JEI. Set the depth, width and height in its menu.
   > Tip: perfect for hollowing out a hillside for a new factory floor.
9. **Framed Blocks**: 8x `framedblocks:framed_cube`; `framedblocks:framed_hammer` *(after: Wood and a Workbench)*
   > What: FramedBlocks are wooden frames that take the look of any block you right-click into them, in dozens of shapes.
   > How: framed cubes are planks and sticks (check JEI). Right-click a framed block with any full block to apply it as camouflage. The Framed Hammer takes it back off.
   > Tip: camouflage with glass, ores or even machine casings: the block keeps its shape and takes the texture.
10. **Slopes and Panels**: 6x any of `or(item(framedblocks:framed_slope)item(framedblocks:framed_corner_slope)item(framedblocks:framed_stairs)item(framedblocks:framed_slab)item(framedblocks:framed_panel)item(framedblocks:framed_pillar)item(framedblocks:framed_prism))` *(after: Framed Blocks)*
   > What: any six framed slopes, corner slopes, stairs, slabs, panels, pillars or prisms.
   > How: each shape is made from framed cubes (check JEI). Most come several at a time.
   > Tip: framed slopes camouflaged as stone make roofs and ramps that look hand-carved.
11. **Secret Storage**: `framedblocks:framed_secret_storage` *(after: Slopes and Panels)*
   > What: a full-block chest that wears any block's texture, so it disappears into a wall.
   > How: a framed chest surrounded by framed cubes (check JEI), then camouflage it.
   > Tip: hide your rarest elements in one. Nobody suspects the third stone brick from the left.
12. **Somewhere to Sit**: 2x any of `or(item(handcrafted:oak_chair)item(handcrafted:spruce_chair)item(handcrafted:birch_chair)item(handcrafted:jungle_chair)item(handcrafted:acacia_chair)item(handcrafted:dark_oak_chair)item(handcrafted:mangrove_chair)item(handcrafted:cherry_chair)item(handcrafted:bamboo_chair)item(handcrafted:crimson_chair)item(handcrafted:warped_chair))`; 1x any of `or(item(handcrafted:oak_table)item(handcrafted:spruce_table)item(handcrafted:birch_table)item(handcrafted:jungle_table)item(handcrafted:acacia_table)item(handcrafted:dark_oak_table)item(handcrafted:mangrove_table)item(handcrafted:cherry_table)item(handcrafted:bamboo_table)item(handcrafted:crimson_table)item(handcrafted:warped_table))` *(after: Wood and a Workbench)*
   > What: Handcrafted adds furniture you can actually sit on: chairs, couches, benches, tables, desks.
   > How: any wood works (check JEI). Right-click a chair to sit.
   > Tip: the Handcrafted Hammer changes the shape of trims and some other pieces (check its tooltip).
13. **A Proper Study**: 2x any of `or(item(handcrafted:oak_couch)item(handcrafted:spruce_couch)item(handcrafted:birch_couch)item(handcrafted:jungle_couch)item(handcrafted:acacia_couch)item(handcrafted:dark_oak_couch)item(handcrafted:mangrove_couch)item(handcrafted:cherry_couch)item(handcrafted:bamboo_couch)item(handcrafted:crimson_couch)item(handcrafted:warped_couch))`; 1x any of `or(item(handcrafted:oak_desk)item(handcrafted:spruce_desk)item(handcrafted:birch_desk)item(handcrafted:jungle_desk)item(handcrafted:acacia_desk)item(handcrafted:dark_oak_desk)item(handcrafted:mangrove_desk)item(handcrafted:cherry_desk)item(handcrafted:bamboo_desk)item(handcrafted:crimson_desk)item(handcrafted:warped_desk))` *(after: Somewhere to Sit)*
   > What: couches connect into long sofas; desks connect into long workbenches.
   > How: any wood (check JEI). Cushions and sheets add colour.
   > Tip: a desk by your Alchemistry machines makes a fine place to read the quest book.
14. **Macaw's Furniture**: 2x any of `or(item(mcwfurnitures:oak_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:spruce_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:birch_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:jungle_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:acacia_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:dark_oak_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:mangrove_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:cherry_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:crimson_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:warped_wardrobe)item(mcwfurnitures:oak_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:spruce_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:birch_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:jungle_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:acacia_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:dark_oak_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:mangrove_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:cherry_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:crimson_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:warped_drawer)item(mcwfurnitures:oak_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:spruce_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:birch_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:jungle_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:acacia_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:dark_oak_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:mangrove_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:cherry_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:crimson_kitchen_cabinet)item(mcwfurnitures:warped_kitchen_cabinet))` *(after: Somewhere to Sit)*
   > What: Macaw's Furniture adds kitchens, wardrobes, drawers, bookshelves and more. Many of them store items.
   > How: any two wardrobes, drawers or kitchen cabinets (check JEI for the full set).
   > Tip: drawers and cabinets open like chests, so a kitchen can double as food storage.
15. **Doors**: 2x any of `or(item(mcwdoors:oak_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:spruce_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:birch_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:jungle_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:acacia_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:dark_oak_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:mangrove_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:cherry_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:bamboo_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:crimson_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:warped_barn_door)item(mcwdoors:oak_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:spruce_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:birch_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:jungle_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:acacia_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:dark_oak_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:mangrove_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:cherry_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:bamboo_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:crimson_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:warped_modern_door)item(mcwdoors:oak_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:spruce_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:birch_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:jungle_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:acacia_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:dark_oak_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:mangrove_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:cherry_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:bamboo_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:crimson_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:warped_glass_door)item(mcwdoors:oak_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:birch_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:jungle_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:acacia_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:dark_oak_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:mangrove_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:cherry_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:bamboo_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:crimson_cottage_door)item(mcwdoors:warped_cottage_door))` *(after: Wood and a Workbench)*
   > What: Macaw's Doors adds barn, cottage, glass, modern and many more doors in every wood.
   > How: any two barn, modern, glass or cottage doors (check JEI for recipes).
   > Tip: glass doors let you see into a machine room without opening it.
16. **Trapdoors**: 4x any of `or(item(mcwtrpdoors:oak_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:spruce_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:birch_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:jungle_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:acacia_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:dark_oak_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:mangrove_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:cherry_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:bamboo_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:crimson_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:warped_barn_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:oak_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:spruce_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:birch_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:jungle_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:acacia_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:dark_oak_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:mangrove_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:cherry_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:bamboo_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:crimson_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:warped_glass_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:oak_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:spruce_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:birch_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:jungle_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:acacia_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:dark_oak_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:mangrove_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:cherry_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:bamboo_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:crimson_classic_trapdoor)item(mcwtrpdoors:warped_classic_trapdoor))` *(after: Doors)*
   > What: decorative trapdoors that match the doors: barn, glass, classic and more.
   > How: any four barn, glass or classic trapdoors (check JEI).
   > Tip: trapdoors on walls make great shutters and cabinet fronts.
17. **Windows**: 4x any of `or(item(mcwwindows:oak_window)item(mcwwindows:spruce_window)item(mcwwindows:birch_window)item(mcwwindows:jungle_window)item(mcwwindows:acacia_window)item(mcwwindows:dark_oak_window)item(mcwwindows:mangrove_window)item(mcwwindows:cherry_window)item(mcwwindows:oak_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:spruce_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:birch_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:jungle_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:acacia_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:dark_oak_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:mangrove_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:cherry_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:crimson_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:warped_pane_window)item(mcwwindows:oak_four_window)item(mcwwindows:spruce_four_window)item(mcwwindows:birch_four_window)item(mcwwindows:jungle_four_window)item(mcwwindows:acacia_four_window)item(mcwwindows:dark_oak_four_window)item(mcwwindows:mangrove_four_window)item(mcwwindows:cherry_four_window))` *(after: Doors)*
   > What: Macaw's Windows adds framed windows, shutters, curtains, blinds and parapets.
   > How: any four framed windows (check JEI).
   > Chemistry: glass is mostly silica (SiO₂) melted with soda (Na₂CO₃) and lime (CaO), then cooled before it can crystallise.
18. **Roofs**: 16x any of `or(item(mcwroofs:oak_roof)item(mcwroofs:spruce_roof)item(mcwroofs:birch_roof)item(mcwroofs:jungle_roof)item(mcwroofs:acacia_roof)item(mcwroofs:dark_oak_roof)item(mcwroofs:mangrove_roof)item(mcwroofs:cherry_roof)item(mcwroofs:bamboo_roof)item(mcwroofs:crimson_roof)item(mcwroofs:warped_roof)item(mcwroofs:oak_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:spruce_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:birch_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:jungle_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:acacia_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:dark_oak_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:mangrove_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:cherry_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:bamboo_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:crimson_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:warped_steep_roof)item(mcwroofs:stone_roof)item(mcwroofs:cobblestone_roof)item(mcwroofs:blackstone_roof)item(mcwroofs:deepslate_roof)item(mcwroofs:andesite_roof))` *(after: Doors)*
   > What: Macaw's Roofs adds sloped, steep, attic and top roof pieces that join into proper roofs.
   > How: any sixteen roof pieces (check JEI). Wooden roofs come from logs and sticks.
   > Tip: place the lower and upper pieces together to make a gentler slope.
19. **Fences and Hedges**: 8x any of `or(item(mcwfences:oak_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:spruce_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:birch_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:jungle_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:acacia_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:dark_oak_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:mangrove_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:cherry_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:bamboo_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:crimson_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:warped_picket_fence)item(mcwfences:oak_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:spruce_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:birch_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:jungle_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:acacia_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:dark_oak_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:mangrove_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:cherry_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:bamboo_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:crimson_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:warped_stockade_fence)item(mcwfences:oak_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:spruce_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:birch_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:jungle_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:acacia_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:dark_oak_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:mangrove_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:cherry_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:bamboo_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:crimson_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:warped_wired_fence)item(mcwfences:oak_hedge)item(mcwfences:spruce_hedge)item(mcwfences:birch_hedge)item(mcwfences:jungle_hedge)item(mcwfences:acacia_hedge)item(mcwfences:dark_oak_hedge)item(mcwfences:mangrove_hedge)item(mcwfences:cherry_hedge))` *(after: Doors)*
   > What: Macaw's Fences adds picket and stockade fences, wired fences, hedges and gates.
   > How: any eight picket, stockade or wired fences or hedges (check JEI).
   > Tip: hedges are made from leaves, so you can keep a garden looking natural.
20. **Paths**: 16x any of `or(item(mcwpaths:cobblestone_basket_weave_paving)item(mcwpaths:andesite_basket_weave_paving)item(mcwpaths:brick_basket_weave_paving)item(mcwpaths:sandstone_basket_weave_paving)item(mcwpaths:cobblestone_honeycomb_paving)item(mcwpaths:andesite_honeycomb_paving)item(mcwpaths:brick_honeycomb_paving)item(mcwpaths:sandstone_honeycomb_paving)item(mcwpaths:cobblestone_diamond_paving)item(mcwpaths:andesite_diamond_paving)item(mcwpaths:brick_diamond_paving)item(mcwpaths:sandstone_diamond_paving)item(mcwpaths:stone_flagstone)item(mcwpaths:andesite_flagstone)item(mcwpaths:brick_flagstone)item(mcwpaths:sandstone_flagstone))` *(after: Stone Age)*
   > What: Macaw's Paths adds paving patterns (basket weave, honeycomb, flagstone) in stone, brick and sandstone.
   > How: any sixteen paving blocks (check JEI).
   > Tip: a path from your base to your waystone saves getting lost at night.
21. **Bridges**: 8x any of `or(item(mcwbridges:rope_oak_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_spruce_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_birch_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_jungle_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_acacia_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_dark_oak_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_mangrove_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_cherry_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_crimson_bridge)item(mcwbridges:rope_warped_bridge)item(mcwbridges:stone_brick_bridge)item(mcwbridges:cobblestone_bridge)item(mcwbridges:brick_bridge)item(mcwbridges:iron_bridge)item(mcwbridges:sandstone_bridge))` *(after: Paths)*
   > What: Macaw's Bridges adds bridge pieces that connect into rope, stone, brick and iron bridges, with piers and stairs.
   > How: any eight bridge pieces (check JEI).
   > Tip: an iron bridge over a lava lake makes the Nether commute much safer.
22. **Lamps and Lanterns**: 4x any of `or(item(mcwlights:classic_street_lamp)item(mcwlights:double_street_lamp)item(mcwlights:chain_lantern)item(mcwlights:bell_lantern)item(mcwlights:cross_lantern)item(mcwlights:tavern_lantern)item(mcwlights:garden_light)item(mcwlights:wall_lamp)item(mcwlights:iron_chandelier)item(mcwlights:golden_chandelier)item(mcwlights:copper_chandelier))` *(after: Light in the Dark)*
   > What: Macaw's Lights adds street lamps, lanterns, garden lights and chandeliers.
   > How: any four of them (check JEI).
   > Tip: street lamps along your paths keep mobs from spawning on the way home.
23. **Lava Lamp**: `mcwlights:lava_lamp` *(after: Lamps and Lanterns, Water)*
   > What: a lava lamp for your desk. This one really has lava in it.
   > Chemistry: a real lava lamp holds wax in water. The bulb heats the wax, it expands, its density drops below the water's and it rises; at the top it cools, gets denser and sinks.
   > How: iron nuggets, a lava bucket and an iron ingot (check JEI).
24. **Jars**: 2x `supplementaries:jar` *(after: Wood and a Workbench)*
   > What: Supplementaries jars hold items, liquids or even small mobs, and show what's inside.
   > How: glass and a wooden slab (check JEI). Right-click with an item or a bucket to fill.
   > Tip: a shelf of jars, one per element, makes a lovely periodic table display.
25. **Rope and Sacks**: 16x `supplementaries:rope`; `supplementaries:sack` *(after: Jars)*
   > What: rope you can climb and pull things up with, and sacks that store items and can be carried.
   > How: grow flax (seeds from wild flax), then craft rope and sacks from it (check JEI).
   > Tip: ropes hanging down a mineshaft are a quick way up and down (check JEI for the pulley block).
26. **Soap**: 2x `supplementaries:soap` *(after: Jars)*
   > What: soap washes the dye off coloured blocks and items, and it powers the Bubble Blower.
   > Chemistry: saponification. Wood ash is rich in potash (K₂CO₃); in water it makes lye. Lye splits fat into glycerol and fatty-acid salts, which is soap. People made it this way for thousands of years.
   > How: a water bucket, four ash and a porkchop (check JEI). Ash is left behind when fire burns blocks away; check JEI for other sources.
27. **Bubbles!**: `supplementaries:bubble_blower` *(after: Soap)*
   > You remember making nebulae. These are smaller, but they're very pretty.
   > What: the Bubble Blower uses soap to blow bubbles; it can also make Bubble Blocks (check its tooltip).
   > Chemistry: a soap film is a thin water layer between two layers of soap molecules. The colours come from light reflecting off its front and back surfaces and interfering.
28. **Create Deco Lamps**: 4x any of `or(item(createdeco:yellow_andesite_lamp)item(createdeco:red_andesite_lamp)item(createdeco:green_andesite_lamp)item(createdeco:blue_andesite_lamp)item(createdeco:yellow_brass_lamp)item(createdeco:red_brass_lamp)item(createdeco:green_brass_lamp)item(createdeco:blue_brass_lamp)item(createdeco:yellow_copper_lamp)item(createdeco:red_copper_lamp)item(createdeco:green_copper_lamp)item(createdeco:blue_copper_lamp)item(createdeco:yellow_iron_lamp)item(createdeco:red_iron_lamp)item(createdeco:green_iron_lamp)item(createdeco:blue_iron_lamp)item(createdeco:yellow_zinc_lamp)item(createdeco:red_zinc_lamp)item(createdeco:green_zinc_lamp)item(createdeco:blue_zinc_lamp))` *(after: Create Deco)*
   > What: Create Deco lamps in four colours and five metals light up a factory floor properly.
   > How: a nugget, a torch and a plate of the metal (check JEI). Any four lamps.
   > Tip: lamps can be switched off with redstone: red lamps as warning lights next to a reactor are a nice touch.
29. **Hulls and Supports**: 4x any of `or(item(createdeco:andesite_hull)item(createdeco:brass_hull)item(createdeco:copper_hull)item(createdeco:iron_hull)item(createdeco:zinc_hull)item(createdeco:industrial_iron_hull))`; 4x any of `or(item(createdeco:andesite_support)item(createdeco:brass_support)item(createdeco:copper_support)item(createdeco:iron_support)item(createdeco:zinc_support)item(createdeco:industrial_iron_support))` *(after: Create Deco Lamps)*
   > What: hulls are riveted metal walls; supports are girders for roofs and catwalks.
   > How: any four hulls and four supports (check JEI).
   > Tip: copper hulls weather like copper blocks do. Wax them with honeycomb to keep the shine.
30. **Copycats**: 2x `copycats:copycat_block`; 4x any of `or(item(copycats:copycat_slope)item(copycats:copycat_stairs)item(copycats:copycat_board)item(copycats:copycat_beam)item(copycats:copycat_slice)item(copycats:copycat_vertical_slope))` *(after: Andesite Casing)*
   > What: Copycats+ adds many more Create copycat shapes: slopes, boards, beams, slices. Each takes the look of the block you apply to it.
   > How: cut zinc ingots in a stonecutter (zinc ore is found underground; check JEI), then right-click a placed copycat with a block.
   > Tip: copycat boards cover pipes and shafts while leaving them working.
31. **Laboratory Blocks**: 16x `laboratoryblocks:laboratory_block` *(after: Nether Quartz)*
   > You remember a laboratory of white stone and light. You can build it again.
   > What: Artemis' Laboratory Blocks adds clean lab walls, tiles, glass, screens, vents and fans.
   > Chemistry: the recipe uses Nether quartz, which is silicon dioxide (SiO₂), the same compound as sand and glass.
   > How: eight stone around one Nether quartz makes eight laboratory blocks (check JEI). Stonecut them into tiles, pillars and doors.
32. **Glowing Panels**: 8x `laboratoryblocks:glowstone_particles`; 8x `laboratoryblocks:glowing_laboratory_block` *(after: Laboratory Blocks)*
   > What: glowing laboratory blocks give off light, so a lab can be bright with no torches.
   > How: one glowstone dust makes eight glowstone particles; one particle plus a laboratory block makes a glowing block (check JEI).
   > Chemistry: real glow-in-the-dark materials store energy from light and release it slowly (phosphorescence). Glowstone just glows; it's magic.
33. **Lab Screens**: 4x any of `or(item(laboratoryblocks:clear_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:text_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:wave_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:quantum_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:glowing_clear_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:glowing_text_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:glowing_wave_laboratory_screen)item(laboratoryblocks:glowing_quantum_laboratory_screen))` *(after: Laboratory Blocks)*
   > What: decorative screens: clear, text, wave and quantum patterns, plain or glowing.
   > How: clear screens from laboratory blocks, laboratory glass and iron screws; add a dye for the patterns (check JEI).
   > Tip: put a wave screen next to your reactor and a ComputerCraft monitor beside it for real numbers.
34. **Fans and Vents**: `laboratoryblocks:laboratory_fan`; 2x `laboratoryblocks:laboratory_vent` *(after: Laboratory Blocks)*
   > What: animated lab fans and vents. The redstone-controlled fan only spins when powered.
   > Chemistry: real labs work with HCl, NH₃ and Cl₂ under a fume hood, which pulls vapours away from your face. Breathing chlorine is a very bad idea.
   > How: laboratory blocks with iron nuggets and iron screws (check JEI).
35. **Lab Doors and Glass**: 2x any of `or(item(laboratoryblocks:laboratory_door)item(laboratoryblocks:glass_laboratory_door)item(laboratoryblocks:mesh_laboratory_door))`; 8x `laboratoryblocks:laboratory_glass` *(after: Laboratory Blocks)*
   > What: lab doors (solid, glass or mesh) and laboratory glass for viewing windows.
   > How: doors from laboratory blocks; laboratory glass is eight glass around a laboratory block (check JEI).
   > Tip: a glass wall around your Alchemistry room lets visitors admire it without touching anything.
36. **A Proper Laboratory**: checkmark *(after: Glowing Panels, Lab Screens, Fans and Vents, Lab Doors and Glass)*
   > White walls, humming fans, glass and light. You remember this place. It was always where you were happiest.
   > What: a challenge: build a real laboratory around your chemistry machines using these blocks.
   > How: include glowing panels, screens, a fume fan and a glass wall. Click the checkmark when you're proud of it.
   > Tip: share a screenshot with your server. Bragging rights are a demigod's privilege.

## Magic and Relics

### Ars Nouveau

*Spellcraft, Source, rituals and the Wilden: magic that runs on stored energy.*

1. **Novice Spell Book**: `ars_nouveau:novice_spell_book` *(after: Whispers of Magic)*
   > You remember speaking and the world listening. The words come back slowly.
   > What: the Novice Spell Book holds spells built from glyphs: a form (how it's cast), effects, then augments.
   > How: craft it (check JEI), then open it to combine glyphs, e.g. Projectile + Break to mine at range.
   > Tip: the Worn Notebook explains every glyph. Mana regenerates over time.
2. **Scribe's Table**: `ars_nouveau:scribes_table` *(after: Novice Spell Book)*
   > What: the Scribe's Table crafts new glyphs, which unlock more spell parts.
   > How: place it, put your spell book on it, and pick a glyph. JEI and the table show each glyph's cost.
   > Tip: Light and Harvest are cheap early glyphs that pay off right away.
3. **Source**: `ars_nouveau:source_jar`; `ars_nouveau:imbuement_chamber` *(after: Scribe's Table)*
   > What: Source is Ars Nouveau's magical energy. Jars store it; the Imbuement Chamber spends it to make source gems and essences.
   > How: craft both (check JEI). Place jars near the chamber; they share Source within a few blocks.
   > Tip: empty jars do nothing. The next step is a Sourcelink to fill them.
4. **Volcanic Sourcelink**: `ars_nouveau:volcanic_sourcelink` *(after: Source)*
   > What: the Volcanic Sourcelink burns fuel and pushes the Source into nearby jars.
   > Chemistry: burning is fast oxidation: C + O₂ → CO₂ plus heat. The sourcelink claims that energy as Source.
   > How: check JEI. Feed it logs or coal with a hopper or pipe; archwood logs give more.
   > Tip: the other sourcelinks feed on crops (Agronomic), food (Mycelial), mob deaths (Vitalic) and potions (Alchemical).
5. **Enchanting Apparatus**: `ars_nouveau:enchanting_apparatus`; `ars_nouveau:arcane_core`; 4x `ars_nouveau:arcane_pedestal` *(after: Volcanic Sourcelink)*
   > What: the Apparatus sits on an Arcane Core and fuses items from surrounding Arcane Pedestals, using Source.
   > How: Core first, Apparatus on top, pedestals within a few blocks. Put the main item in the Apparatus and right-click (check JEI).
   > Tip: it also applies enchantments to gear: more ways to make divine equipment.
6. **Ritual Brazier**: `ars_nouveau:ritual_brazier` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: rituals are area effects: sunrise, cloudshaping, overgrowth, flight and more. Each needs a brazier and a ritual tablet.
   > How: craft the brazier (check JEI), place it, and right-click it with a ritual tablet.
   > Tip: most rituals draw Source from nearby jars, so build next to your source room.
7. **Ritual of Flight**: `ars_nouveau:ritual_flight` *(after: Ritual Brazier)*
   > You remember the ground being optional. Around your home, it is again.
   > What: gives creative flight to players nearby, paid for with Source. Good for base flight.
   > How: craft the Flight tablet (check JEI), put it in the Ritual Brazier and keep the jars topped up.
   > Tip: for flight everywhere, see the Angel Ring and jetpacks in Tools and Comfort.
8. **Starbuncle**: `ars_nouveau:starbuncle_charm` *(after: Source)*
   > What: a Starbuncle carries items from one chest to another. Cute logistics.
   > How: befriend a wild Starbuncle in an Archwood forest for its charm, or check JEI. The Worn Notebook explains binding it to chests.
   > Tip: Starbuncles are great for carrying Botany Pot harvests to storage.
9. **Apprentice Spell Book**: `ars_nouveau:apprentice_spell_book` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: the second spell book tier: more mana, more slots and access to tier 2 glyphs.
   > How: upgrade your Novice book in the Enchanting Apparatus (check JEI).
   > Tip: more mana means longer spells and more casts between rests.
10. **Archmage Spell Book**: `ars_nouveau:archmage_spell_book` *(after: Apprentice Spell Book)*
   > What: the last book tier, with tier 3 glyphs.
   > How: upgrade the Apprentice book in the Apparatus (check JEI).
   > Tip: tier 3 glyphs include some of the strongest effects in the mod.
11. **Other Sourcelinks**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_nouveau:agronomic_sourcelink)item(ars_nouveau:mycelial_sourcelink)item(ars_nouveau:vitalic_sourcelink)item(ars_nouveau:alchemical_sourcelink))` *(after: Volcanic Sourcelink)*
   > What: every sourcelink feeds on a different kind of energy. Agronomic eats crop growth, Mycelial eats food, Vitalic eats mob deaths, Alchemical eats potions.
   > Chemistry: all of them are the same idea in a costume: stored chemical energy (sugars, fats, potions) released and captured.
   > How: craft any one of them (check JEI) and place it near your Source Jars.
   > Tip: an Agronomic Sourcelink next to a crop farm is free Source forever.
12. **Ars Additions Charms**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_additions:undying_charm)item(ars_additions:fall_prevention_charm)item(ars_additions:fire_resistance_charm)item(ars_additions:night_vision_charm)item(ars_additions:water_breathing_charm)item(ars_additions:void_protection_charm))` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: Ars Additions charms save you once from a specific danger: falling, fire, drowning, the void, or death itself.
   > How: made in the Enchanting Apparatus around a glass bottle (the Undying Charm takes a Totem of Undying, crying obsidian and glowstone). Recharge them in the Imbuement Chamber.
   > Tip: a Void Protection Charm is cheap insurance before your first trip to the End.
13. **Handy Haversack**: `ars_additions:handy_haversack` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: the Handy Haversack links to a container so items you pick up go straight there.
   > How: craft it (check JEI), then bind it to a chest or your storage as its tooltip says.
   > Tip: bind it to your AE2 or Occultism storage interface for a bottomless pocket.
14. **A Familiar**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_nouveau:familiar_starbuncle)item(ars_nouveau:familiar_wixie)item(ars_nouveau:familiar_drygmy)item(ars_nouveau:familiar_whirlisprig)item(ars_nouveau:familiar_bookwyrm)item(ars_nouveau:familiar_amethyst_golem)item(ars_elemental:firenando_familiar)item(ars_elemental:flashjack_familiar)item(ars_elemental:siren_familiar))` *(after: Ritual Brazier)*
   > What: a familiar is a bound creature that rides along and gives a passive bonus: Starbuncles speed you up, Wixies boost potions, Bookwyrms cut spell costs.
   > How: run the Ritual of Binding with the creature's charm nearby to turn it into a familiar item (the Worn Notebook has the details). Use the item to call your familiar.
   > Tip: Ars Elemental adds elemental familiars (Firenando, Flashjack, Siren) that boost that school of magic.
15. **Ritual of Chunk Loading**: `ars_additions:ritual_chunk_loading` *(after: Ritual Brazier)*
   > What: an Ars Additions ritual that keeps its chunk loaded while it has Source.
   > How: craft the tablet (check JEI) and run it in a Ritual Brazier next to your Source Jars.
   > Tip: FTB Chunks can also force-load claimed chunks; use whichever you prefer.
16. **Too Cool for Logistics**: `ars_nouveau:starbuncle_shades` *(after: Starbuncle)*
   > You remember having servants who took themselves very seriously. This one does not.
   > What: Starbuncle Shades: tinted glass and a source gem. Give them to a Starbuncle.
   > Tip: they do nothing useful. That is the point.
17. **Wilden Hunt**: kill 5x `ars_nouveau:wilden_hunter` *(after: Novice Spell Book)*
   > What: Wilden are Ars Nouveau's wild magical beasts. Hunters charge, Stalkers fly, Guardians throw spikes.
   > How: they roam the Overworld at night, more often near archwood forests and Wilden dens. Bring a shield.
   > Tip: keep every horn, spike and wing they drop. Rituals need them.
18. **Wilden Wings**: 3x `ars_nouveau:wilden_wing` *(after: Wilden Hunt)*
   > What: Wilden Wings drop from Wilden Stalkers, the flying ones.
   > How: hunt Stalkers at night. The Ritual of Flight tablet needs three wings (purple archwood log, 3 wings, 2 diamonds, an ender pearl).
   > Tip: a Ritual of Wilden Summoning will need three more Wilden drops, so keep hunting.
19. **The Wilden Chimera**: kill 1x `ars_nouveau:wilden_boss` *(after: Wilden Wings, Ritual Brazier)*
   > You remember beasts that were many beasts at once. You remember winning.
   > What: the Wilden Chimera combines all three Wilden. It drops Wilden Tribute, used for Ars Elemental's Mark of Mastery.
   > How: craft the Ritual of Wilden Summoning (purple archwood log, 3 Wilden drops, a lapis block), place it in a brazier away from your base, and fight.
   > Tip: it has phases and calls spikes from the ground. Keep moving and bring healing.
20. **Mark of Mastery**: `ars_elemental:mark_of_mastery` *(after: The Wilden Chimera)*
   > What: the Mark of Mastery upgrades elemental foci and turns Arcanist armor into elemental mage armor.
   > How: imbue a Wilden Tribute with all seven essences plus anima essence (check JEI). One tribute gives five marks.
   > Tip: anima essence comes from imbuing a source gem with a wither skeleton skull, bone meal and a golden apple.
21. **Lesser Elemental Focus**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_elemental:lesser_fire_focus)item(ars_elemental:lesser_water_focus)item(ars_elemental:lesser_earth_focus)item(ars_elemental:lesser_air_focus))` *(after: Source)*
   > What: an Ars Elemental focus is a curio that strengthens one school of spells (fire, water, earth or air) and lowers its cost.
   > Chemistry: the four classical elements were the first attempt at a periodic table. You know there are 118, but the spells don't.
   > How: in the Imbuement Chamber: an amethyst shard with 3 matching essences and 3 gold ingots on pedestals.
   > Tip: choose the school your favourite spell uses most.
22. **Greater Elemental Focus**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_elemental:fire_focus)item(ars_elemental:water_focus)item(ars_elemental:earth_focus)item(ars_elemental:air_focus))` *(after: Lesser Elemental Focus, Mark of Mastery)*
   > What: the full focus gives a much bigger bonus to its school and protects you from matching hazards.
   > How: put the lesser focus in the Enchanting Apparatus with a Mark of Mastery on a pedestal (5,000 Source).
   > Tip: a fire focus pairs well with Ignite and Flare; a water focus with Watery Grave.
23. **Mage Armor**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_nouveau:sorcerer_robes)item(ars_nouveau:arcanist_robes)item(ars_nouveau:battlemage_robes))` *(after: Enchanting Apparatus)*
   > What: Ars Nouveau armor comes in three styles (Sorcerer, Arcanist, Battlemage). Each piece adds mana and mana regeneration.
   > How: made in the Enchanting Apparatus around a golden armor piece with magebloom fiber (check JEI for each style).
   > Tip: grow magebloom early; every piece needs fiber.
24. **Armor Tiers**: checkmark *(after: Mage Armor)*
   > What: mage armor has three tiers. Each tier raises mana and opens more thread slots.
   > How: place the armor in the Enchanting Apparatus with the upgrade items on pedestals: tier 2 needs blaze rods, tier 3 needs more (check JEI under 'armor upgrade').
   > Tip: threads go into the Alteration Table: Spellpower, Magic Capacity, Repairing and more. Click the checkmark once you've upgraded a piece.
25. **Elemental Mage Armor**: 1x any of `or(item(ars_elemental:fire_hat)item(ars_elemental:aqua_hat)item(ars_elemental:earth_hat)item(ars_elemental:air_hat)item(ars_elemental:fire_robes)item(ars_elemental:aqua_robes)item(ars_elemental:earth_robes)item(ars_elemental:air_robes))` *(after: Mark of Mastery, Mage Armor)*
   > What: Ars Elemental turns Arcanist armor into fire, water, earth or air armor, each with resistances and a spell bonus.
   > How: in the Enchanting Apparatus: an Arcanist piece plus a Mark of Mastery, a netherite ingot and two matching essences (7,000 Source).
   > Tip: a full set matching your focus makes that school very cheap to cast.
26. **Two Roads to the Sky**: checkmark *(after: Ritual of Flight)*
   > What: a note on the Ritual of Flight and the pack's other flight sources.
   > How: read the second page, then click the checkmark.
   > {@pagebreak}
   > Ritual of Flight: the tablet is a purple archwood log, 3 Wilden Wings, 2 diamonds and an ender pearl. Ars Elemental adds a second recipe: a flashing (yellow) archwood log, 1 wing, 2 diamonds and a feather.
   > Range: the ritual only grants flight near its brazier and drains Source while you fly. It's base flight, not world flight.
   > Elsewhere: the Infusion Pylon with a Potion of Flying (hydrogen from the Dissolver), Iron Jetpacks (MI circuits) and the Angel Ring (digital circuit) all work anywhere. See Tools and Comfort.

### Apotheosis

*Affix loot, gems, enchanting beyond level 30, spawners and Gateways.*

1. **Gem Dust**: 4x `apotheosis:gem_dust` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Apotheosis gear can roll affixes and sockets. Gems drop from mobs and chests; gem dust builds its tables.
   > How: crush spare gems into gem dust (JEI shows how).
   > Tip: keep good gems for sockets and crush the rest.
2. **Simple Reforging**: `apotheosis:simple_reforging_table` *(after: Gem Dust)*
   > What: the Simple Reforging Table rerolls affixes on gear of lower rarities.
   > How: an enchanting table, iron, smooth stone and gem dust.
   > Tip: reforging costs rarity materials; salvage junk gear to get them.
3. **Salvaging**: `apotheosis:salvaging_table` *(after: Simple Reforging)*
   > What: the Salvaging Table breaks affixed gear into materials of its rarity.
   > How: a smithing table, iron tools, copper, a lava bucket and gem dust (check JEI).
   > Tip: every affixed sword you don't want is a reforge for one you do.
4. **Gem Cutting**: `apotheosis:gem_cutting_table` *(after: Salvaging)*
   > What: the Gem Cutting Table upgrades gems to higher purity using matching gems and dust.
   > Chemistry: real gems are cut along cleavage planes, flat directions where the crystal's bonds are weakest.
   > How: planks, smooth stone, shears and gem dust.
5. **Divine Reforging**: `apotheosis:reforging_table` *(after: Gem Cutting, Into the Fire)*
   > You remember weapons that chose their wielders. These at least choose good affixes.
   > What: the full Reforging Table handles every rarity, up to mythic gear.
   > How: the Simple Reforging Table, netherite, nether bricks and arcane sands (check JEI).
6. **Better Bookshelves**: 4x `apothic_enchanting:hellshelf` *(after: Gem Dust, Into the Fire)*
   > What: Apothic Enchanting adds shelves that raise enchanting power, quanta and arcana. Hellshelves are the first step.
   > How: bookshelves, nether bricks and a blaze rod. Create: Enchantment Industry can automate enchanting later.
   > Tip: enchanting power above 30 unlocks enchantments beyond vanilla levels.
7. **Sigil of Socketing**: `apotheosis:sigil_of_socketing` *(after: Gem Cutting)*
   > What: adds a socket to gear in a smithing table, so you can slot in a gem.
   > How: gem-fused slate, amethyst, gunpowder and gem dust (check JEI).
   > Tip: a Sigil of Withdrawal pulls gems back out later.
8. **Augmenting**: `apotheosis:augmenting_table` *(after: Divine Reforging)*
   > What: the Augmenting Table improves or rerolls one affix at a time.
   > How: an enchanting table, a Nether Star, a godforged pearl and polished blackstone (check JEI).
   > Tip: save it for gear you plan to keep for the rest of the game.
9. **Bottled Monsters**: `minecraft:spawner` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: with Apothic Spawners a monster spawner can be picked up with a Silk Touch pickaxe and moved home.
   > How: find one in a dungeon or mineshaft, light it up, then mine it with Silk Touch. Check its tooltip after placing.
   > Tip: spawners are mob farms for drops you can't make chemically: bones, string, ender pearls, blaze rods.
10. **Spawner Runes**: 1x any of `or(item(apotheosis:spawner_rune)item(apotheosis:burning_spawner_rune)item(apotheosis:redstone_control_spawner_rune)item(apotheosis:ignore_players_spawner_rune)item(apotheosis:silent_spawner_rune)item(apotheosis:youthful_spawner_rune))` *(after: Bottled Monsters, Gem Dust)*
   > What: spawner runes change a spawner when you right-click it: spawn without players nearby, obey redstone, set mobs on fire, stay silent, and more.
   > How: a basic Spawner Rune is gem dust, gem-fused slate and spawner chains (check JEI for where chains come from). Each modifier rune adds one item, like campfires for Burning.
   > Tip: Redstone Control first, so you can switch the farm off when you don't need it.
11. **Elite Hunting**: kill 50x `minecraft:zombie` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Apotheosis gives some monsters affixed gear and makes rare elites with a coloured name. Their drops can roll affixes and sockets.
   > How: kill 50 zombies. Pick up any named or glowing item they drop and read its tooltip.
   > Tip: elites hit hard. Gear up before poking one with a stick.
12. **Potion Charm**: `apotheosis:potion_charm` *(after: Gem Dust, The Brewer's Art)*
   > What: a Potion Charm holds a potion effect and applies it while switched on, using durability.
   > Chemistry: blaze powder is a reactive powder; here it fixes the potion so it lasts, a bit like a preservative.
   > How: three identical potions in the middle row and six blaze powder around them. Infusing it later makes it permanent (check JEI).
   > Tip: Night Vision or Haste charms are the most useful early.
13. **Rarity Materials**: 4x `apotheosis:luminous_crystal_shard` *(after: Salvaging)*
   > What: each rarity salvages into its own material: common scrap metal, uncommon timeworn fabric, rare luminous crystal, epic arcane sands, mythic godforged pearls.
   > How: salvage rare (blue) gear in the Salvaging Table until you have 4 luminous crystal shards.
   > Tip: reforging uses the material of that rarity, so sort your loot by colour before salvaging.
14. **Gate Pearl**: `gateways:gate_pearl` *(after: Into the Fire)*
   > What: a Gate Pearl opens a Gateway that releases waves of a chosen mob. Beat every wave for rewards.
   > How: the Blaze gate is five blaze rods, an ender pearl and three nether bricks. Apotheosis adds tiered gates (Frontier and up); JEI shows them all.
   > Tip: a gate is a controlled mob farm: blaze rods, ender pearls and XP on demand.
15. **Close a Gateway**: checkmark *(after: Gate Pearl)*
   > What: the gateway closes when every wave is defeated and pays out its reward.
   > How: throw the pearl in an open area, stay close (leaving fails the gate) and kill each wave. Click the checkmark when it closes.
   > Tip: tiered Apotheosis gates hand out affix gear and rarity materials.
16. **Sigil of Withdrawal**: `apotheosis:sigil_of_withdrawal` *(after: Sigil of Socketing)*
   > What: pulls every gem out of an item without destroying them, so a good gem can move to new gear.
   > How: gem-fused slate, blaze rods, ender pearls, a lava bucket and gem dust (makes 4).
   > Tip: withdraw gems before salvaging or reforging old gear.
17. **Sigil of Enhancement**: `apotheosis:sigil_of_enhancement` *(after: Divine Reforging)*
   > What: upgrades a socketed gem to the next rarity at the Reforging Table.
   > How: gem-fused slate, gem dust and a godforged pearl (makes 4). Godforged pearls come from salvaging mythic gear.
   > Tip: save these for gems you'll keep: a mythic gem in endgame gear is worth it.
18. **Infusion**: `apothic_enchanting:infused_hellshelf` *(after: Better Bookshelves)*
   > What: Apothic Enchanting can 'infuse' items: enchanting a hellshelf at the right power makes an Infused Hellshelf.
   > How: put a hellshelf in the enchanting table with enough shelves around it. JEI's infusion page shows the exact Eterna, Quanta and Arcana it needs.
   > Tip: the table's three stats are shown on the screen: power, randomness and how many extra enchantments you get.
19. **Enchantment Library**: `apothic_enchanting:library` *(after: Infusion)*
   > What: the Library absorbs enchanted books and stores their enchantments as points, then pulls out any level you've collected.
   > How: four ender chests, four infused shelves and an enchanting table.
   > Tip: it only gives back levels up to the highest single book you fed it, so feed it every spare book.
20. **Endshelves**: 4x `apothic_enchanting:endshelf` *(after: Infusion, The End)*
   > What: endshelves push enchanting power past what hellshelves and seashelves allow.
   > How: end stone bricks, a bookshelf, an ender pearl and Infused Breath (dragon's breath infused at the enchanting table).
   > Tip: collect dragon's breath in glass bottles during the Ender Dragon fight.
21. **Scrap Tome**: `apothic_enchanting:scrap_tome` *(after: Better Bookshelves)*
   > What: a Scrap Tome in an anvil with enchanted gear strips off some of its enchantments into a book (the gear is destroyed).
   > How: eight books around an anvil makes eight tomes.
   > Tip: the Extraction Tome (infused) keeps the item; check JEI's infusion page.

### Relics and Artifacts

*Found treasures and crafted curios: Relics, Artifacts and Reliquary.*

1. **Relics**: `reliquary:mob_charm_fragment` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: three mods of found and crafted treasures. Reliquary items are crafted from mob parts; Relics and Artifacts hide in chests and structures.
   > How: mob charm fragments drop from mobs. JEI shows the charm they build.
   > Tip: a Mob Charm stops that mob type attacking you.
2. **Alkahestry Tome**: `reliquary:alkahestry_tome` *(after: Relics)*
   > What: charged with redstone, the tome duplicates common items in a crafting grid.
   > Chemistry: alchemists searched for alkahest, a universal solvent. None exists (what would you keep it in?), but aqua regia, HCl + HNO₃, dissolves even gold.
   > How: check JEI for the tome and its recipes. Recharge it with redstone.
3. **Relic Hunter**: checkmark *(after: Relics)*
   > What: find any Relics or Artifacts item: a cloud in a bottle, running shoes, a leafy mantle, a mimic's treasure.
   > How: loot chests in dungeons, mineshafts and towers, and beware chests that bite. Click the checkmark when you have one.
   > Tip: Relics items level up as you use them; check their tooltips.
4. **Fortune Coin**: `reliquary:fortune_coin` *(after: Alkahestry Tome)*
   > What: pulls nearby items and XP toward you while active.
   > How: check JEI. Right-click to toggle it.
   > Tip: turn it off near your sorting system or it'll grab the items.
5. **A True Relic**: 1x any of `or(item(relics:chorus_staff)item(relics:experience_disperser)item(relics:ghostly_mantle)item(relics:glitchy_mantle)item(relics:hunting_belt)item(relics:jellyfish_necklace)item(relics:kinetic_belt)item(relics:leafy_mantle)item(relics:midnight_mantle)item(relics:pet_bone)item(relics:piglin_mask)item(relics:reflective_necklace)item(relics:rider_flute)item(relics:ring_of_the_seven_deadly_sins)item(relics:roller_skate)item(relics:shield_of_retaliation)item(relics:sphere_of_self_sacrifice)item(relics:springy_boot)item(relics:chef_hat)item(relics:clot_of_time)item(relics:cut_glass_boot))` *(after: Iron)*
   > You remember owning things no mortal smith made. A few of them seem to have survived.
   > What: Relics are found, never crafted: mantles, belts, necklaces, boots, a chef's hat, a ring of the seven deadly sins. Each has abilities that grow with use.
   > How: loot chests in dungeons, mineshafts, temples, villages and modded structures. Any one relic completes this quest.
   > Tip: read the tooltip: each relic says what it does and how it gains experience.
6. **Relic Growth**: checkmark *(after: A True Relic)*
   > What: relics earn experience when you use their abilities, and their levels make those abilities stronger.
   > How: keep the relic equipped and use it. Open its ability screen (the tooltip names the key) to spend levels. Click the checkmark after your first upgrade.
   > Tip: one well-levelled relic beats a drawer full of new ones.
7. **An Artifact**: 1x any of `or(item(artifacts:anglers_hat)item(artifacts:antidote_vessel)item(artifacts:aqua_dashers)item(artifacts:bunny_hoppers)item(artifacts:charm_of_shrinking)item(artifacts:charm_of_sinking)item(artifacts:chorus_totem)item(artifacts:cloud_in_a_bottle)item(artifacts:cowboy_hat)item(artifacts:cross_necklace)item(artifacts:crystal_heart)item(artifacts:digging_claws)item(artifacts:eternal_steak)item(artifacts:everlasting_beef)item(artifacts:feral_claws)item(artifacts:fire_gauntlet)item(artifacts:flame_pendant)item(artifacts:flippers)item(artifacts:golden_hook)item(artifacts:helium_flamingo)item(artifacts:kitty_slippers)item(artifacts:lucky_scarf)item(artifacts:night_vision_goggles)item(artifacts:novelty_drinking_hat)item(artifacts:obsidian_skull)item(artifacts:onion_ring)item(artifacts:panic_necklace)item(artifacts:pickaxe_heater)item(artifacts:plastic_drinking_hat)item(artifacts:pocket_piston)item(artifacts:power_glove)item(artifacts:rooted_boots)item(artifacts:running_shoes)item(artifacts:scarf_of_invisibility)item(artifacts:shock_pendant)item(artifacts:snorkel)item(artifacts:snowshoes)item(artifacts:steadfast_spikes)item(artifacts:strider_shoes)item(artifacts:superstitious_hat)item(artifacts:thorn_pendant)item(artifacts:umbrella)item(artifacts:universal_attractor)item(artifacts:vampiric_glove)item(artifacts:villager_hat)item(artifacts:warp_drive)item(artifacts:whoopee_cushion)item(artifacts:withered_bracelet))` *(after: Iron)*
   > What: Artifacts are simple, always-on curios: Running Shoes, Cloud in a Bottle, Digging Claws, a Lucky Scarf, Night Vision Goggles and many more.
   > How: found in chests underground and in structures, and dropped by Mimics. Any one artifact completes this quest.
   > Tip: a Cloud in a Bottle (double jump) and Running Shoes make early exploring much easier.
8. **That Chest Has Teeth**: kill 1x `artifacts:mimic` *(after: An Artifact)*
   > What: a Mimic looks exactly like a chest until you open it. It always drops an artifact.
   > How: they hide in mineshafts, dungeons and caves. If a chest stands somewhere odd, hit it before opening it.
   > Tip: Mimics are tough for their size; fight one with real armor, not a wooden sword.
9. **Divine Comedy**: `artifacts:whoopee_cushion` *(after: An Artifact)*
   > You remember the other gods laughing at something like this. You never admitted it was you.
   > What: the Whoopee Cushion. Equip it and crouch.
   > Tip: it does exactly what you think.
10. **Mob Ingredients**: 2x `reliquary:zombie_heart` *(after: Relics)*
   > What: Reliquary items are built from rare mob parts: zombie hearts, rib bones, catalyzing glands, slime pearls, molten cores and more.
   > How: they drop from their mobs (zombies drop zombie hearts). In this pack they can also be crafted: a zombie heart is rotten flesh in a ring of iron ingots.
   > Tip: a Looting sword makes the drops much more common than the iron recipe.
11. **Mob Charm Belt**: `reliquary:mob_charm_belt` *(after: Mob Ingredients)*
   > What: the belt holds Mob Charms, so you can be ignored by several mob types at once.
   > How: three leather and five mob charm fragments (check JEI). Each fragment is made from that mob's drops.
   > Tip: creeper and enderman charms are the most valuable around a base.
12. **Void Tear**: `reliquary:void_tear` *(after: Mob Ingredients, Ghast Tears)*
   > What: a Void Tear stores a huge stack of one item, and pulls more of it from your inventory.
   > How: a ghast tear, a nebulous heart, a slime pearl and lapis (shapeless).
   > Tip: it's also an ingredient for the Infernal Tear, Emperor's Chalice and Midas Touchstone.
13. **Lantern of Paranoia**: `reliquary:lantern_of_paranoia` *(after: Mob Ingredients)*
   > What: the lantern places torches from your inventory wherever it's dark enough for mobs to spawn.
   > How: iron ingots, glass, a slime pearl and a molten core (check JEI).
   > Tip: carry a stack of torches when caving and let it do the lighting.
14. **The Handgun**: `reliquary:handgun` *(after: Mob Ingredients)*
   > What: a pistol that fires magazines of special bullets: neutral, exorcism, blaze, ender, storm, seeker and more.
   > How: barrel, grip and hammer assemblies plus iron and a slime pearl. Bullets and magazines have their own recipes (check JEI).
   > Tip: Seeker bullets home in on targets; good against flying mobs.
15. **Phoenix Down**: `reliquary:phoenix_down` *(after: Mob Ingredients, Into the Fire)*
   > What: saves you from death, heals you, and gives you a few seconds of protection. It has several charges.
   > How: three Angelheart Vials and an Angelic Feather. Vials need milk, glass, fertile essence and an infernal claw (check JEI).
   > Tip: keep it in your inventory before boss fights and Nether trips.

### Occultism

*Spirits, pentacles, otherworld storage and miners that dig where you can't.*

1. **Demon's Dream**: 4x `occultism:datura` *(after: Iron)*
   > You remember seeing everything at once. One bite, and some of it comes back.
   > What: eating Demon's Dream Fruit gives the Third Eye: you see Otherworld blocks hidden behind normal ones.
   > How: break grass for Demon's Dream seeds, grow them, and eat a fruit. The Dictionary of Spirits explains the rest.
   > Tip: with the Third Eye active, otherworld trees appear among normal oaks.
2. **Your First Pentacle**: `occultism:chalk_white`; `occultism:golden_sacrificial_bowl`; 4x `occultism:sacrificial_bowl` *(after: Demon's Dream)*
   > What: rituals happen on chalk pentacles, with a Golden Ritual Bowl at the centre and Sacrificial Bowls holding the ingredients.
   > Chemistry: real chalk is calcium carbonate, CaCO₃: the shells of tiny sea creatures, pressed into rock.
   > How: impure chalk from otherstone and otherworld wood dust, purified in Spiritfire. The Dictionary shows each pentacle's layout.
3. **Bind a Foliot**: `occultism:book_of_binding_bound_foliot` *(after: Your First Pentacle)*
   > What: a bound Book of Binding holds a spirit's name and starts its rituals. Foliots are the weakest spirits.
   > How: craft the Book of Binding (taboo book, purified ink, awakened feather), then bind it as the Dictionary describes.
   > Tip: foliots can sort items, chop trees or farm for you.
4. **Iesnium**: 2x `occultism:iesnium_ingot` *(after: Bind a Foliot, Into the Fire)*
   > What: iesnium ore hides in the Nether and only shows up with the Third Eye. You need an infused pickaxe to mine it.
   > How: spirit-attuned gems (a diamond in Spiritfire) make the infused pickaxe. Smelt the ore.
   > Tip: iesnium tools and the miner spirits come next.
5. **Spirit Miners**: `occultism:dimensional_mineshaft`; `occultism:miner_foliot_unspecialized` *(after: Iesnium)*
   > What: the Dimensional Mineshaft holds a miner spirit that pulls ores from the Otherworld over time.
   > How: both come from rituals (check the Dictionary and JEI). The miner needs an iesnium pickaxe and a magic lamp.
   > Tip: in this pack miners can't produce chemistry-milestone ores: uranium, uraninite, osmium, fluorite, platinum, iridium or tungsten. Those still come from real processing.
6. **Surprisingly Large Bag**: `occultism:satchel` *(after: Bind a Foliot)*
   > What: a satchel with a bound foliot inside that keeps it bigger on the inside.
   > How: a foliot crafting ritual: a chest, two leather, string and a silver ingot around the golden bowl (check the Dictionary for the pentacle).
   > Tip: the Dictionary lists the other foliot rituals: lumberjack, farmer, cleaner and item transporter.
7. **The Wild Hunt**: kill 5x `occultism:wild_hunt_wither_skeleton` *(after: Your First Pentacle, Into the Fire)*
   > What: the Wild Hunt ritual calls a pack of wither skeletons with a good chance of dropping wither skeleton skulls.
   > How: activated with a skeleton skull; it needs copper, silver and gold blocks, a diamond, netherrack and soul sand, plus a sacrifice (check the Dictionary). Then fight.
   > Tip: this is the easiest way to collect three skulls for the Wither.
8. **Bind a Djinni**: `occultism:book_of_binding_bound_djinni` *(after: Bind a Foliot)*
   > You remember spirits who bowed when you passed. This one only negotiates.
   > What: djinn are the second rank of spirits. Their rituals make storage, soul gems, familiar rings and better miners.
   > How: craft a Book of Binding (Djinni), then bind it with the spirit's name, the same way as the foliot (the Dictionary explains it).
   > Tip: djinni pentacles are bigger; check each layout in the Dictionary before placing chalk.
9. **Dimensional Matrix**: `occultism:dimensional_matrix` *(after: Bind a Djinni)*
   > What: the Dimensional Matrix binds a djinni to hold items in a pocket dimension.
   > Chemistry: the quartz it needs is SiO₂, the same crystal that keeps time in a real watch by vibrating at a fixed frequency.
   > How: a djinni crafting ritual with three quartz blocks and an ender pearl.
10. **Storage Actuator**: `occultism:storage_controller` *(after: Dimensional Matrix)*
   > What: the Dimensional Storage Actuator stores thousands of items and shows them in one searchable screen.
   > How: a foliot ritual makes the Storage Actuator Base (otherstone pedestal, copper, gold). Put the matrix on top of the base in a crafting grid.
   > Tip: it's an alternative to AE2 for early storage. Stabilizers expand it.
11. **Storage Stabilizers**: 2x `occultism:storage_stabilizer_tier1` *(after: Storage Actuator)*
   > What: stabilizers pointed at the Actuator raise how many items and item types it holds.
   > How: tier 1 is a foliot ritual: a tier 0 stabilizer, a copper block, blaze dust and a spirit-attuned gem. Higher tiers need stronger spirits.
   > Tip: place them within a few blocks, facing the Actuator.
12. **Storage Remote**: `occultism:storage_remote` *(after: Storage Actuator)*
   > What: opens your Storage Actuator from anywhere.
   > How: a djinni ritual with an inert remote, two ender pearls and quartz. Link it to the Actuator by clicking it.
   > Tip: one remote per player saves a lot of walking back to base.
13. **Soul Gem**: `occultism:soul_gem` *(after: Bind a Djinni)*
   > What: a Soul Gem traps a living creature so you can move it and release it elsewhere.
   > How: a djinni ritual: a diamond, copper, silver and gold ingots, a fragile soul gem and soul sand.
   > Tip: it's the easy way to move villagers or animals to your base.
14. **Familiar Ring**: `occultism:familiar_ring` *(after: Soul Gem)*
